# Multiphysics engine roadmap

Reference date: 16 October 2026. Translated to English on 19 October 2026. The current order of work is set by [ARCHITECTURE.md](../../ARCHITECTURE.md) §9.1 and §10 "Current order"; this roadmap keeps the phases, their dependencies and their closing criteria.

**A specific pause requested by the user:** an independent nondimensionalization tool is planned in [its own plan](../history/frontend/plan-nondimensionalization-equations.md), preferably in another chat of the same project. It is not yet integrated with the simulator and does not change the order or state of F3–F10. The delivery has its own authorized scope; it is not another priority document for the multiphysics engine.

**Main plan and continuity record. F1 and F2 integrated within the published scope. The registry holds eight substances; UNIFAC-LLE admits seven and keeps methanol blocked for lack of data. The next point of the plan is F3. Claude continues the work of the plan already under way.** The event and domain incidents belong to F9; they do not open another phase or automatically replace the task in progress.

Coordination update: the earlier instruction to put a new reliability assignment ahead of F2 is withdrawn. The evidence of the NRTL failure is kept and its result remains uncertified. Documenting an incident does not change the order of the plan.

## Current user priority (20 October 2026)

### M1 — Minimum usable multiphysics engine

**User decision, 22 October 2026:** establish a finite minimum and move into Transform/Solve as soon as its deduction prerequisites are met. This milestone supersedes earlier instructions to extend coefficient-case partitions or complete every deduction adapter before proceeding. **Status, 23 October 2026: Claude approved all five D1 criteria for frozen M1-A/B/C and independently verified U1 for the same fixed suite; M1 is complete for M1-A/B/C.** Existing scoped capabilities remain available, but their passing tests do not establish this complete acceptance suite.

**Objective:** a user can build, replay, transform, solve and inspect a small coupled nonspatial model in the existing worksheet, from fundamental laws and explicit constitutive premises, using one versioned formulation with verified units, validity conditions and invalidation. M1 covers finite-dimensional first-order explicit ODE systems with algebraic quantities that can be uniquely reconstructed in a declared regular regime. Existing supported thermodynamic closures remain compatible; M1 does not require developing a new equilibrium package or general DAE solver.

There are two separate gates. **D1 ends general deduction polishing and starts Transform/Solve implementation. U1 establishes usable end-to-end multiphysics.** A missing compiler or solver bridge is a U1 task and cannot, by itself, justify extending the symbolic kernel after D1.

#### Fixed acceptance suite

These are internal acceptance documents authored through shared operations, not new product-library templates or equipment-specific code. Freeze their physical assumptions, parameter ranges, finite test horizons, reference equations and error scales before running acceptance. Parameter/assumption variants exercise the same three cases; they do not expand the milestone into a model catalog.

| ID | Coupled case | Required derivation and independent reference |
|---|---|---|
| M1-A | A well-mixed single-phase region with two components and material/thermal exchange | Component conservation, total mass derived from components, first-law storage/transport and declared caloric closure. Use positive initial inventory and explicitly balanced boundary mass flows; deduce constant total mass by summing component balances while retaining every dynamic component, total-mass and energy balance. This reference does not introduce an additional fixed-inventory DAE constraint. Compare composition and temperature against the analytic constant-coefficient mixing/heating reference; unequal molar masses check the mass/mole distinction. |
| M1-B | Two material inventories coupled through pressure and a dissipative flow relation | Matter, Cauchy momentum, hydrostatic inventory/pressure reconstruction and a recorded constitutive resistance. Preserve a flow-inertia state where selected. Check internal-flow cancellation and compare the regular constant-density linear regime with an independently assembled linear-system solution. Close only the storage/passage provenance needed by this case. |
| M1-C | Two thermal capacities exchanging heat | Separate first-law balances, explicit energy/temperature definitions and a declared heat-transfer relation. Check equal/opposite internal heat exchange, constant total energy for the isolated pair and the analytic exponential temperature-difference decay. This case exercises a thermal application without a chemical-operation-specific equation path. |

All three use arbitrary quantity/region identities and shared authoring operations. M1-C does not require a new physical-family UI. A declared heat-capacity or resistance law is a constitutive premise, not something claimed to follow from conservation. Additional mechanical/electrical kernel references may diagnose a generic defect; publishing a new discipline adapter is not part of M1. Distillation-column capability is a later milestone.

**M1-C input document frozen, 23 October 2026:** [the internal common-model document](../../tests/reference_data/m1-c.json) is authored by [the shared-operation recipe](../../tests/m1_acceptance.py), with no terminal ODE in its saved history. It contains two closed rigid regions, one material per region, no reaction, negligible kinetic energy, no viscous/body work or volumetric source, uniform specific internal energy, one phase without phase change, and a declared linear caloric law. The explicit boundary conditions state that the one internal connection exhausts both regions' heat exchange. No adiabatic condition is incorrectly imposed on either individual region.

| Frozen M1-C item | Value / identity |
|---|---|
| Initial masses and specific caloric coefficients | `region_a`: 2 kg and 500 J/(kg K); `region_b`: 3 kg and 1,000 J/(kg K). Both remain positive; retained component conservation gives zero mass rates. |
| Thermal data | Conductance 10 W/K, initial temperatures 360 K and 300 K, reference temperature 300 K and reference specific energy zero. Caloric validity interval: 290–370 K. |
| State and reconstruction identities | `m__region_a__1(t)`, `m__region_b__1(t)`, `U__region_a(t)`, `U__region_b(t)` are the retained inventories; `T__region_a(t)` and `T__region_b(t)` reconstruct from their respective caloric inventories. The derived temperature-rate rows and combined total-energy row are consequences, not additional independent states. |
| Parameters and closure dependencies | `caloric_capacity__region_a`, `caloric_capacity__region_b`, both energy/temperature references and `conductance`; the two `energy_heat_rate__…(t)` quantities connect explicitly to `internalHeat(t)`. The common-model data retain their units; automatic binding of the workbench conductance to numerical execution remains a U1 task. |
| Horizon, samples and error scales | 0–600 s, 121 equally spaced samples; 60 K temperature scale, 5 kg total-mass scale, 60,000 J energy scale and 600 W heat-rate scale. U1 thresholds below remain unchanged. |
| Independent reference | With `e = exp(-t/75 s)`, `T_a = 315 K + 45 K e`, `T_b = 315 K - 15 K e`; `U_a = (1,000 J/K)(T_a - 300 K)`, `U_b = (3,000 J/K)(T_b - 300 K)`, and `U_a + U_b = 60,000 J`. Initial masses remain constant. |

The 25 recorded workbench operations pin their equation inputs: they declare the heat quantity, conductance, constitutive heat law and positive regime; substitute equal/opposite boundary heat into each first law; differentiate each caloric inventory; substitute its derived zero mass rate and first-law energy rate; isolate the temperature derivative; and combine the two energy balances. Positive mass/capacity and bounded temperature domains remain explicit. The frozen document stores input histories, selected equation IDs and reference data; graph expressions are rebuilt by the shared engine. The recipe uses the same operation session as Model with the immutable physical source cached, not a fixture-specific evaluator.

Verification: the current six-test thermal acceptance run passes in 188.290 s, including fresh authoring, exact law ancestry and dimensions, independent analytic references and parameter variants, total-energy cancellation, persistence, closure invalidation, Undo/Redo and exact Transform handoff. Complete A/B/C command results and current frontend evidence are recorded below. This is not browser or numerical U1 acceptance.

**M1-A/B input freeze, 23 October 2026:** [M1-A](../../tests/reference_data/m1-a.json) and [M1-B](../../tests/reference_data/m1-b.json) use the shared authors in `tests/m1_acceptance.py`. M1-A starts with component conservation and the first law, then declares boundary flow/composition and stream enthalpy premises. M1-B regenerates the existing internal hydraulic candidate with `node frontend/verificar_hydraulics.mjs --inputs-only`, then records pressure coupling, wall-law substitution, flow continuity and isolation in the same mathematical session as Model. Neither document stores a terminal ODE or invokes an older hydraulic/thermal solver. These are frozen inputs for review, not a declaration that their physical proofs pass D1.

Primary workspace: Model. Inputs: physical journals, generic region/component/connection identities, classified boundary and constitutive premises, units and regular regimes. Outputs: pinned operation histories and selected derived equation identities. Transform consumes the exact rows and conditions; closure or physical-source changes invalidate downstream history. Solve remains blocked on the new relations/conditions until D1 permits U1 work. Product layout, library contents and persisted protocol identifiers are unchanged.

| Frozen item | M1-A | M1-B |
|---|---|---|
| Physical regime | One rigid, well-mixed region; two nonreacting components; single phase without phase change; negligible kinetic, viscous/body work and volumetric source; declared common constant caloric coefficient; closed material boundary remainder. | Two prismatic free-surface inventories and one fixed circular path; single-phase, externally isothermal constant-density Newtonian medium; quasi-steady laminar wall closure with retained bulk flow inertia; no drive and equal path endpoint elevations. No thermal or equilibrium certification is claimed. |
| Initial values and parameters | Component masses 2 and 8 kg; molar masses 0.02 and 0.05 kg/mol; inlet/outlet mass rate 0.1 kg/s; feed mass fraction 0.7; initial/feed temperature 300/330 K; heat input 2,000 W; caloric coefficient 1,000 J/(kg K); zero reference energy at 300 K; equal stream flow-work offset 100 J/kg. Temperature domain 290–360 K. | Inventories 40 and 10 kg; flow initially zero; density 1,000 kg/m3; viscosity 0.05 Pa s; path length 10 m and diameter 0.02 m; each storage area 0.02 m2; both surface pressures 101,325 Pa; gravity 9.80665 m/s2; temperature 293.15 K. Positive inventories, pressure above 2,500 Pa and Reynolds number below 2,100 define the reference regime. |
| States and reconstructions | `m__mixture__1(t)`, `m__mixture__2(t)`, `U__mixture(t)` are inventories. Temperature follows `thermodynamics.mixture.caloric-inventory`; `amount1(t)` and `amount2(t)` use the respective molar masses. Mass/mole fractions reconstruct from those inventories. `operation.20` is a dependent total-mass rate, not a fixed-inventory DAE constraint. `operation.43` is a dependent temperature rate. | `m__upper__1(t)`, `m__lower__1(t)`, `q__s1__1(t)` are the three states. Storage heights and absolute port pressures reconstruct from the same inventories; `q__s2__1(t)` follows fixed-path mass compatibility. The path inventory is geometric, not an extra integrated state; total mass and pressure/height rows are dependent consequences. |
| Horizon and predeclared scales | 0–600 s, 121 samples; mass 10 kg, amount 500 mol, composition 1, temperature 50 K, energy 500,000 J. | 0–600 s, 121 samples; mass 50 kg, flow 0.1 kg/s, height 2 m, pressure 20,000 Pa. |

Thus mass is 10 kg while total amount changes from 260 mol toward 410 mol; mass fraction initially 0.2 differs from mole fraction 5/13. | Let `A = pi D^2/4`, `I = L/A`, `R = 128 mu L/(pi rho D^4)`. The independent matrix has rows `(0,0,-1)`, `(0,0,1)`, `(g/(A_upper I),-g/(A_lower I),-R/I)` and solution `exp(matrix*t) [40,10,0]`. Reconstruct `h_v = m_v/(rho A_v)` and `p_v = p_surface + g m_v/A_v`; dissipation is `R q^2/rho`. Reversed-head and equal-head variants use the same matrix. |

Exact workbench mapping (the JSON retains every input signature and the physical journals):

- **M1-A, 43 operations:** 1–7 declare parameters; 8–11 and 14–17 declare/substitute component feed and well-mixed outlet flows; 12–13 and 18–19 define component amounts; 20 sums component conservation; 21–28 declare positive inventory/capacity/molar-mass/flow and temperature/feed-fraction domains; 29–30 and 33–34 substitute component flows in the first law; 31–32 and 35–36 declare/substitute inlet and outlet enthalpy closures; 37–38 declare/substitute heat; 39 differentiates the caloric inventory; 40–41 substitute both component rates; 42 substitutes the first-law rate; 43 isolates the temperature rate. The energy input is `energy.mixture.rates.11`; component inputs are `matter.mixture.{1,2}.fixed-volume-apply`.
- **M1-B, 31 operations:** 1 substitutes `momentum.channel.wall.area-form` into `momentum.channel.path.pressure-form`; 2–4 / 8–10 evaluate each `momentum.{upper,lower}.storage.port-pressure` at its floor and substitute its material total definition; 5–7 / 11–13 declare pressure continuity to the path and substitute the reconstructed pressures; 14–17 declare and remove drive/elevation difference; 18 isolates the inlet-flow derivative; 19 isolates the outlet flow from `momentum.channel.path.mass-compatibility`; 20 substitutes it in the lower inventory law; 21 sums both inventories; 22–31 declare positive path area/length/density/viscosity and storage area/density/gravity. The selected upper inventory is `matter.upper.1.closed`; pressures are 4/10, lower inventory 20, flow 18 and dependent total 21. Heights remain `momentum.{upper,lower}.storage.height`.
- **M1-C, 25 operations:** 1–4 declare heat, conductance, the constitutive relation and its positive regime; 5–7 / 15–17 declare and apply each exhaustive heat boundary and the constitutive relation; 8–10 / 18–20 declare mass/capacity/temperature domains; 11–14 / 21–24 differentiate caloric inventories, substitute mass and energy rates and isolate temperature rates; 25 combines the first laws. All three references retain their dynamic conservation equations.

#### D1 — Deduction readiness and stop rule

D1 passes only when **all three** acceptance documents satisfy these criteria:

1. Every active balance starts at its relevant fundamental law. Each used physical assumption and mathematical step has replayable inputs and actual before/after expressions; definitions, constitutive premises and conditions retain their distinct roles. Audit chronology for the controls used by these documents. Completing every other physical adapter is not required.
2. Units are known and consistent. Needed substitutions, sums, product/chain operations, integration/reduction and unique isolation are supported through shared tools. Denominators, branch choices and domain assumptions are explicit; unsupported regimes are rejected. No exhaustive parameter partition is needed when a declared, checkable regular regime suffices.
3. The selected system identifies states, their first derivatives, reconstructible quantities, parameters, initial/boundary requirements and closure dependencies with stable IDs. It has no unexplained source terms or unresolved constitutive obligations in its selected regime. Redundant totals and dependent consequences are marked. Missing numerical values are reported for Solve, not invented by Model.
4. Replay, removal/change of a used assumption, Undo/Redo, persistence and same-ID source changes behave correctly. The exact selected graph and conditions reach the Transform boundary. An invalid history remains inspectable and cannot execute a valid prefix or older system.
5. Independent algebra/reference checks pass and no confirmed soundness defect remains on the admitted paths. Claude's audit supplies evidence and counterexamples; it is not an unlimited feature request. Record any unresolved concern that prevents these specific claims as a located blocker.

- **Concern (a), first-law proof order: not a blocker.** The recorded A/C journals differ from the proof only in the first two controls (pressure separation, then kinetic removal). Applying them in the recorded order to `energy.<owner>.specific.1` gives the proof row `stress.3` up to the linearity of divergence (-div(p v) against div(-p v)) for `mixture`, `region_a` and `region_b`. The two substitutions act on disjoint symbols. Each recorded control keeps its actual before/after expressions through cursor replay, and the [common-model contract](../contracts/common-model.md) already states that the energy proof is in dependency order, not an edit transcript. The replacement regression now enforces that actual-expression commutation; the energy producer is unchanged.
- **Wall root: not a blocker.** `momentum.channel.wall.cauchy` and `wall.newtonian` are classified `constitutive` and feed only the laminar resistance, which M1-B declares as a recorded constitutive premise. The main path law is connected to `momentum.channel.local.x`. The B1 repair takes option (b): the title now states that it is a declared auxiliary constitutive premise and that local-Cauchy projection is not deduced; the rule is explicitly typed `constitutive_premise`.
- **B1, blocker for D1(1), M1-B only.** `storage_reduction` in `core/balances/momentum_reductions.py` adds `momentum.{upper,lower}.storage.cauchy` as a new `balance` root with placeholder terms. `storage.force` then zeroes them by flag names, with no graph inputs from the recorded assumptions. The same graph already develops `momentum.<owner>.local.z.step-7` (rho dv_z/dt = -rho g - dp/dz) and the declared `local.z.zero-rate`, but the storage chain does not use them. It also writes the pressure as p(z, t) without a recorded premise, although the x/y rows with their zero-rate conditions give dp/dx = dp/dy = 0. The storage pressure feeds selected rows `operation.4`, `operation.10` and `operation.18`. An ancestry sweep of every selected row in A/B/C found no other balance root outside the fundamental laws; the other roots are definitions, recorded conditions or declared constitutive premises.
  - **Minimal repair (Codex, owner of the D1 repairs):** derive `storage.force` from `local.z.step-7` and `local.z.zero-rate`, derive the horizontal pressure independence from the x/y rows, and keep every downstream storage row and selected expression unchanged.
  - **Acceptance:** `momentum.<owner>.local.z` is an ancestor of `outputs[owner + '.pressure']`; no storage balance root has empty inputs; removing the zero-rate assumption invalidates the storage pressure; the selected expressions and the frozen M1-B document are unchanged; `tests/test_m1_coupled_acceptance.py` passes. Claude then verifies and decides D1(1).

The prefix audit covers A 18, B 60 and C 34 controls: 106 change an expression and 6 are the same deferred premises (`fija` in A and C; `uniformSections`, `impermeableWall` and `constantArea` in B). |
| D1(5) | Passed | The references from the verdict above still hold. Rerunning every earlier kernel reproducer (`.tmp/claude-kernel-audit/repro_*.py`, `.tmp/claude-kernel-reaudit/repro_*.py`, `probe_power.py`) gives the expected rejections and accepted controls. `repro_handoff.py` now stops at its first step with a structured `MathematicsError`, because differentiating by a constrained scalar is rejected: that is the defect-1 repair, not a regression. |

- **Non-blocking observation (capability finding for later):** `storage.cauchy` restricts p(x,y,z,t) to p(z,t) as a generic substitution. Its inputs are the two zero horizontal gradients, plus a recorded textual premise: "Connected Cartesian region; zero x/y gradients imply p = p(z,t)". The step is valid under that premise, but the kernel does not check the reduction of arguments itself. A typed shared operation (restrict a dependency from vanishing partial derivatives on a declared connected domain) would make it checkable. It does not change any selected expression of these documents.
- **Consequence:** D1 passes for M1-A/B/C. `m1-bridge` may start. Defects 5–6 remain non-blocking backlog.

**Once D1 passes, stop general deduction expansion and immediately prioritize Transform/Solve for U1.** A new kernel operation is then justified only by a minimal reproducer that blocks a numbered M1 criterion, or by a confirmed correctness defect in an admitted path. Every proposed addition must name that criterion and its acceptance test; otherwise it goes to the later backlog. One scoped independent audit plus verification of its fixes is sufficient; repeat broad audits only when new evidence or changes justify them. Do not keep raising the gate to include newly imagined models.

#### U1 — End-to-end usability

After D1, implement the smallest shared numerical bridge required by the fixed suite:

- **Transform:** all three cases support the identity formulation. One case additionally passes the existing optional multiplicative scaling path, including physical reconstruction. Scaling is never mandatory. Preserve selected equation/condition IDs and formulation revision; no alternate display-only system.
- **Solve:** lower the selected derived expressions into the existing shared explicit-ODE execution path, including unit-bearing constants and required algebraic reconstructions. Do not select a final RHS by fixture/equipment name or silently substitute an older physical adapter. Validate required data, initial conditions, structure and all applicable validity conditions; conditions that can fail during integration must be checked with explicit failure/event behavior. Unhandled constraints or DAEs remain rejected.
- **Numerical acceptance:** run all three fixed references with the supported RK45 and BDF methods. For each published state/reconstructed quantity, the maximum absolute error divided by a positive, predeclared physical reference scale must be at most `1e-5`. Integrated conservation residuals divided by their predeclared inventory/energy scales must be at most `1e-6`. Tightening tolerances by a factor of ten must reduce the reference error or keep it below these thresholds; do not claim an observed convergence order from this check alone. Exact symbolic cancellation of internal transfers is checked separately. Missing scales or reference definitions make the test incomplete; thresholds must not be weakened after seeing failures.
- **Results and lifecycle:** charts/tables publish the actual physical solution, units, conservation checks and exact model/formulation/run provenance. Changing a physical assumption, unit or closure makes old artifacts stale; changing only labels does not. Missing closure, invalid data, a violated domain and unsupported numerical lowering produce explicit diagnostics without fallback.
- **Product acceptance:** exercise one coupled case in an isolated browser storage context through the existing Model → Transform → Solve → Results flow, including a change, invalidation and a new run. Verify the other two through common API/storage integration. Preserve equations left/assumptions right, existing user data and the empty product-template library. API/SSR evidence alone does not certify this browser check.

U1 passes when all of these criteria have current reproducible evidence and no unresolved defect invalidates the claimed scope. This permits normal use of the admitted nonspatial subset; it does not certify arbitrary physics or every numerical method.

**M1 numerical bridge, 23 October 2026 (Codex; independently verified by Claude the same day, see below).** `m1-bridge` consumes a versioned selection of actual Transform quantity/equation identities, source pin, unit-bearing numerical data, initial algebraic identities, reconstructions and conservation scales. `core/compilador/derived.py` replays the original graph, lowers its selected expressions into the existing explicit AST and delegates to `SistemaExplicito` and the shared RK45/BDF integrators. The existing common compilation/Solve entry points select this branch explicitly. No fixture/equipment name selects an RHS, no terminal equations are stored in the selection, and unsupported lowering never falls through to an older physical adapter.

The compatible [common-model contract §7a](../contracts/common-model.md#7a-replayed-graph-to-explicit-ode-bridge-m1-23-october-2026) defines `motores.derivedExecution` v1, its read-only source-pin API and optional compilation-report extension. Solve carries exactly the source and operation rows published by Transform. A/B retain three physical states each; C retains four. Temperatures, heights, pressures, the compatible outlet flow, fixed path inventory and dependent totals are algebraic reconstructions. Net-rate integrals are separate auxiliary conservation diagnostics. Exact unit conversion, generated AST dimensional analysis, consistent initialization and runtime scalar-domain checks precede or accompany execution. Parameters appearing only in observables/guards are now loaded by the shared explicit compiler.

The reduced hydraulic scope reuses its existing physical admission and pressure/Reynolds validity monitor; the evolved RHS comes exclusively from the selected graph. The selected B pressure reconstruction evaluates storage-floor ports. Other pressure closures and general pump/network/DAE execution are outside this bridge. `core/solvers/integrador.py` and phase-event detection are unchanged. Optional M1-C positive multiplicative scaling uses the existing transformation engine and `TransformedSystem` with physical reconstruction, rather than adding a scaling implementation.

**Validity and capability findings.** Inherited interval identities must refer to actual replayed equalities. Conditions on eliminated local coordinates/fields remain explicit conditional proof premises, with their source identities, and are distinguished from numerically enforced state/data conditions. Pointwise certification of those spatial fields is not delivered. A newly recorded spatial condition is rejected rather than waived as an inherited premise. General constraint/residual execution and automatic predicate implication remain pending. The typed dependency-restriction operation found during D1 is not needed by this numerical bridge and was not implemented. UI selection controls, full storage/lifecycle integration, tolerance-refinement acceptance and the isolated browser walkthrough remain `m1-u1`; no U1 criterion is marked passed from the bridge smoke runs.

**Frozen-scope protection.** A/B/C inputs, the shared acceptance author, the acceptance tests and workbench operation counts **43/31/25** are preserved. Kernel-backlog behavior in the units service, session and workbench stays unchanged, and its independent review continues separately. No Claude thermodynamics file is edited. F6 is `planned`, depends on `f3`, with next action “General pump and network execution after F3 solution methods”. MAT-DERIV is `planned`, depends on `m1-bridge`; this increment compiles existing deductions rather than starting a new deduction milestone.

**Short reference runs.** Each run uses the frozen 600 s horizon and 121 requested samples, `rtol=1e-9`, and physical absolute tolerances `1e-11 kg`, `1e-11 kg/s` and `1e-6 J` for retained mass, flow and energy fields respectively. Error is the maximum across published physical states and reconstructions divided by the frozen positive quantity scale. Conservation is the inventory change minus the independently integrated net rate, divided by its declared inventory/energy scale. A/C use analytic references; B uses an independently assembled matrix exponential. These are bridge regression results, without the tenfold tolerance-refinement or browser/lifecycle acceptance required for U1.

Reconstructed component and dependent total masses agree with independent mass accounting. RK45 and BDF at two tolerances give a maximum scaled error of 5.13e-9, falling below 6.69e-10 under tenfold refinement. Owner/component renaming, exact Transform rows, serialization, Undo/Redo, removed closure, zero coefficients and permanent zero-rate rejection are checked. This does not certify general mass-matrix/DAE, reaction thermodynamics, equilibrium volumetric closure or spatial execution.

**MAT-DERIV independent review (Claude, 24 October 2026): lowering verified, one blocking finding.** Derived by hand from component conservation d(rho V)/dt = q_in + G - C with the recorded mean-inventory and product-rule steps: decay V drho/dt = -k V rho^2 gives rho0/(1+k rho0 t); filling with common port density gives rho dV/dt = rho Q, so V0+Q t; dilution gives V(t) drho/dt + rho Vdot = q, so (m0+q t)/(V0+Vdot t). Probes in `.tmp/claude-mat-deriv-review/` (`probe.py`, `probe_feed.py`, JSON outputs): the lowered right-hand side equals these laws at 20 random (t, state) points per case (relative error <= 2.0e-16); with coefficients different from the fixtures (k = 0.25, rho0 = 4; Q = 0.5, V0 = 3; Vdot = 0.7, q = 0.1, V0 = 1.5) the trajectories match the closed forms within 3.0e-9 scaled; a 2000 L volume gives the m3 result (5.1e-10); an initial mass inconsistent with rho0 V0 is rejected (`invalid_initialization`); a negative consumption coefficient stops as `parcial` at the analytic blow-up t = 3.333 s. Codex's `MatterLoweringTests` pass (4 tests, 39.6 s). *Finding MD-1 (blocking for the filling case):* the stream's declared mass flow is neither used nor checked once `densidadComunPuertos` substitutes q = rho Q. With the declared feed changed from 0.6 to 0.9 kg/s (rho Q = 0.6 kg/s), the run is still `resuelto` with V(5) = 3 m3 and m(5) = 9 kg, whereas the model's own stream datum implies 10.5 kg; nothing in `verificaciones` or diagnostics reports the contradiction. Required repair: a contradicted or unused boundary datum fails closed (as `inconsistent_parameter_premise` does for B) or is removed from the stream by a recorded operation. *Finding MD-2 (non-blocking):* when a prescribed volume reaches zero inside the horizon (Vdot = -0.5 m3/s, V = 0 at 4 s), the run stops as `parcial` at t = 4.000 s with only the generic integrator message and no event naming the declared positive-volume domain. Verdict: `mat-deriv` stays in review until MD-1 is repaired and probe 4 of `probe.py` is rerun.

A declared 0.9 kg/s against the recorded 3 kg/m3 and 0.2 m3/s raises `LoweringError` with `inconsistent_physical_binding`, the port equation ID and the datum name; both compilation and Solve expose that diagnostic through HTTP 422. Constant per-component mass-flow data are admitted; nonconstant residual relations and other flow bases/modes fail explicitly rather than silently ignoring the datum. A coherent 900 g/s and 0.3 m3/s case agrees with the independently written mass accounting m(t) = 6 + 0.9 t under RK45 and BDF. The negative regression failed on the original code (`LoweringError not raised`) before the repair.

#### Objective and acceptance criteria

Primary workspace: **Model**, connected to **Transform → Solve → Results**. Build a granular, extensible mathematical authoring engine: fundamental laws plus explicit assumptions and constitutive premises produce the requested equations through replayable typed operations. Inputs include equation identities, quantities and dependencies, units, physical regions and interfaces. Outputs include actual before/after algebra, conditions, source identities and reversible history. A physical edit invalidates downstream formulations and results; browsing a derivation does not.

The priority is improving the engine, not producing an attractive example. Never insert a terminal equipment equation and call it a deduction. If an operation is missing, extend the shared engine and replay from the relevant law. Separate conservation balances, definitions, constitutive relations and constraints. Preserve dynamic inventories even when a derivative happens to vanish; a permanent zero-rate condition must remain explicit. Unsupported numerical lowering must block execution, never silently solve the earlier formulation. General symbolic authoring does not imply a general DAE solver or the ability to derive every physically valid model.

Keep the existing Model interface: `ModelWorksheet.tsx` owns equations on the left and assumptions on the right; all families and examples use it. New operations extend those controls. Do not add another editor or repopulate the example library. Use English for code, interface text and documentation. Spatial extensions remain deferred. Read repository instructions and apply [the equation-deduction skill](../../.agents/skills/model-equation-deduction/SKILL.md).

### General deduction engine: execution plan (21 October 2026)

**Mathematical foundation refinement (22 October 2026, user direction):** the mathematical engine sits below multiphysics. It accepts declared equations, quantities, dependencies, units and validity premises without equipment identities or a closed physical-family catalog. Multiphysics supplies laws and constitutive premises through adapters. A tank, a separation assembly or a non-chemical model must exercise the same mathematical operations.

The current implementation plan is:

1. **Audit questions, in priority order.**

1. **Equivalence and exceptional cases:** can cancellation, division, substitution or isolation admit an equation outside its recorded validity conditions? Test zero coefficients, vanishing denominators, repeated roots, contradictory/insufficient domains and identities versus inconsistent equations. Distinguish a wrong accepted result from a clearly rejected unsupported capability.
2. **Units and domain proofs:** do typed constants preserve dimensions through sums, products, roots and calculus? Does SI-magnitude projection preserve the meaning of membership and every selected parameter premise? Check different compatible units, exact rational factors, open endpoints, singleton intersections, dimensionless scaled units, incompatible units and affine/nonrational rejection. Include compound and fractional dimensions where relevant; document unsupported conversions honestly.
3. **Actual provenance and replay:** does every claimed operation have the correct typed inputs and actual before/after expressions? Check complete branch evidence, transitive conditions, unit-conversion rows, singleton substitutions, input pinning, no-op premises, atomic failed edits, Undo/Redo and legacy unsigned records. Look for invented historical steps or conditions that exist only as display text and are not enforced where their truth is required.
4. **One formulation and recovery:** confirm Model and Transform carry the same declaration/conversion/case rows for both component-free and instantiated models. Check stale inputs, changed endpoint units and invalid histories. Unsupported graphs must block Solve/Results reuse; a valid prefix or an older model must never become an implicit execution fallback. Do not infer a numerical execution defect merely because constructing a solver object is permitted; inspect its actual admission/compilation boundary.
5. **Generality and the next step:** check that names, equation groups and equipment categories do not select algebra. Use neutral mechanical/electrical or abstract references through shared operations. Assess D1 for the fixed M1-A/B/C suite. Map each blocking finding to a numbered D1/U1 criterion; separate later improvements. Recommend moving to Transform/Solve once D1 is met. Exhaustive coefficient-case partitions are not a prerequisite unless a minimal M1 reproducer requires them. Do not implement extensions during this audit.

**Method and change boundary.** Production code, tracked tests, UI, saved user data, product examples and capability status are read-only for this assignment. Run existing tests and create minimal additional reproducers under ignored `.tmp/claude-kernel-audit/`; these are executable debugging aids, not a second documentation report. Do not edit assertions just to obtain a passing result. Use hand-derived algebra, exact rational checks or an independent reference; calling the same SymPy routine twice is not independent validation. A declared constitutive test premise must be identified as such, and product models still start from fundamental laws. Execute the audit rather than returning only a review plan.

Useful initial checks are `python tests/test_mathematical_kernel.py`, `python tests/test_mathematical_domains.py`, `python tests/test_equation_manipulation.py`, `python tests/test_equation_workbench.py` and `python tests/test_workspace_handoffs.py`. The full workbench suite generates publications for `node frontend/verificar_equation_workbench.mjs`; run them in that order. These suites are starting points, not a requirement to rerun unrelated expensive physics tests. Avoid concurrent runs that overwrite the same generated fixtures. Do not regenerate the Checklist merely for an audit. Record exact commands, outcomes and omitted checks. No backend restart, process termination, package upgrade, commit, push, clean/reset, library repopulation or UI redesign is part of this assignment.

**Deliverable.** Update only the audit findings/evidence subsection below in this canonical document. For each confirmed issue, record severity, reviewed file and line, minimal input/operation sequence, expected mathematics, actual behavior, reproducible command and affected consumers. Separate confirmed defects, documented capability limits, unverified hypotheses and optional improvements. State whether each confirmed defect blocks D1 or U1, cite the acceptance case/criterion, and propose a focused repair with a regression criterion. If no defect is found, say which scope was tested and what remains unverified; never claim universal correctness. Return a concise summary in the Claude chat so the user can bring it back to the implementation chat. Do not message another chat automatically.

**Audit findings and evidence (Claude, executed 22 October 2026).** Scope: the working-tree kernel `core/mathematics/*`, `core/unidades/servicio.py` (`exact_conversion`), the workbench admission path in `core/modelo/equation_workbench.py` and the Transform summaries in `core/compilador/presentation.py`, inspected and exercised directly; frontend storage readers were inspected only. SHA-256 hashes of these files were recorded at the start in `.tmp/claude-kernel-audit/hashes-start.txt` and rechecked unchanged before this entry was written. Reproducers live in the ignored `.tmp/claude-kernel-audit/` and run from the repository root: `python .tmp/claude-kernel-audit/repro_kernel.py`, `repro_units_domains.py`, `repro_provenance.py` and `repro_handoff.py`. Expected results are hand-derived; no expectation was computed with the routine under test. No production code, tracked test, UI, saved model, server or Checklist entry was changed.

*Confirmed defects, in priority order.* Every one is reproducible with the commands above. None changes a numerical run today, because derived `operation.*` rows are excluded from the executed `systemEquations`. All of them do publish false or unconditioned mathematics as recorded deductions in Model, Transform and the Results `equationWorkbench` attachment.

1. **High: differentiation with respect to a symbol the equation constrains yields a false consequence without conditions.** `core/mathematics/graph.py:141`, `core/mathematics/session.py:393`. Sequence: symbols `F` [N], `k` [N/m], `x` [m]; root `F = k*x`; `differentiate` with respect to `x`. The relation only implies `dF/dx = k` if it holds identically in `x`. Then `F` must depend on `x`. Treating `F` as an independent symbol should be rejected. It must never yield `0 = k`. Actual result: `Eq(0, k)` is accepted with no conditions. The flag `requires_lowering=False`, so a model containing only such operations passes `ensure_executable` (`equation_workbench.py:357`). The electrical form `V = R*I`, differentiated with respect to `I`, gives `0 = R` (repro R1/R1b). Through the public v3 draft API, Model and Transform publish the identical row `0 = k_q`, with `assumptions: []` (`repro_handoff.py`). `integrate` (`graph.py:136`) has the same missing premise: the equation must hold identically over the integration coordinate.
2. **High: substitution admits values that violate recorded validity conditions and accepts non-finite equations.** `session.py:380-392`. Finiteness is checked only in `parse_expression` (`dimensions.py:101`), never on derived equations. Sequence: root `y = 1/(x-1)` with condition `x - 1 != 0`, relation `x = 1`, then `substitute`. The substitution must be rejected. Actual result: `Eq(y, zoo)` is accepted (R2). If the declared relation is `y = (x^2-1)/(x-1)`, the result is `Eq(y, nan)` (R2b). The draft API publishes `y_q = \tilde{\infty}` identically in Model and Transform. The result escapes only by accident when the left side has dimensions, because `zoo` is treated as dimensionless.
3. **High, structural: validity conditions are stored as LaTeX or free text, so nothing enforces them.** Affected code: `graph.py:27,36,160-169`, `session.py:323`, `dimensions.py:95` and `domains.py:171`. `EquationNode.assumptions` holds strings such as `'a \\neq 0'` (repro P2). Consequently, the kernel cannot detect when a later substitution falsifies them (defect 2) or when they contradict a recorded domain premise. Sequence P1: root `a*y = a*b`, `declare_domain a zero`, plain `isolate y`. Expected result: rejection, because the case `a = 0` makes the equation an identity and leaves `y` undetermined. Actual result: `y = b` is accepted under `a \neq 0` while `a \in \{0\}` remains a recorded premise. This is also the main blocker for coefficient-case partitions, which need typed predicates to prove disjointness and completeness.
4. **Medium: indefinite integration loses a real-domain condition.** `graph.py:138` calls `sp.integrate` directly. Sequence R3: `dy/dx = 1/x` with condition `x != 0`, then `integrate`. Expected result: `y = log|x| + C`, or `log x + C` with a recorded `x > 0`. Actual result: `y(x) = C1 + log(x)` under only `x != 0`. For `x < 0` it has no real-valued `C1`.
5. **Repaired, 23 October 2026; independent verification pending. Low–medium: an irrational endpoint conversion was admitted as an "exact rational".** Original audit reproducer (before repair). Affected code: `core/unidades/servicio.py:123-144`. Pint 0.26.1 defines `pi` as a 50-digit decimal. The `Fraction` registry therefore turns `degree` into `31415…/18×10^50`, despite the docstring rule that nonrational conversions remain unsupported. Sequence D1: a quantity in `rad`, followed by an interval `[0, 90]` `degree`, is accepted with a rational upper bound that approximates `π/2`. The exact controls `km/hour = 5/18`, `inch = 127/5000`, `percent = 1/100`, `cal = 523/125`, `liter = 1/1000` and `minute = 60` match their SI definitions; `degF` is reported as affine and is rejected downstream.
6. **Repaired, 23 October 2026; independent verification pending. Low: unsigned or stripped operations were not distinguishable.** Original audit reproducer (before repair). Affected code: `session.py:270,448`. If `sourceSignatures` are removed from a recorded operation, it replays silently on a changed source. For example, `a*y = a*b` changed to `a*y = 3ab` yields `y = 3b` (P3). Its in-memory `OperationReceipt.input_signatures` holds the newly computed signatures, so it looks pinned. The documented behavior covers legacy histories: they are readable and are not backfilled. No published field marks an operation as unsigned. Any client that saves a definition can therefore bypass pinning. Receipts are not currently published, so the misleading receipt affects only internal and test consumers.

**Defects 5–6 repair (`m1-kernel-backlog`, 23 October 2026; independently verified by Claude the same day, see the last bullet).** This separately authorized non-blocking increment depends only on completed `m1-repairs`. It does not advance m1-bridge, R5 or U1, and leaves the independent D1 review and criteria unchanged.

- **Defect 5:** `exact_conversion` checks Pint definition dependencies before SI reduction, including aliases, prefixes and indirect references to mathematical irrational constants. Decimal representations of pi, ln10, Euler’s number, tansec and the Wien roots cannot become rational proof premises. The degree interval reproducer now fails with a structured 422 operation error; the six rational controls retain their exact factors and Fahrenheit remains affine/rejected. No approximation or symbolic-pi support is added.
- **Defect 6 decision:** preserve legitimate version-1 legacy inspection, with an explicit **Legacy/unsigned operation** finding for every active operation with unpinned equation inputs and an execution warning. Without original signatures it is impossible to know whether a source changed or a client stripped signatures. Therefore all such histories block compilation, even if replay succeeds. Undo/Rewind retains the history; deliberate re-recording pins reviewed current inputs. Redo and signed descendants cannot erase the block. Receipts distinguish recorded `input_signatures` from current `calculated_input_signatures`, with `signature_status` equal to `pinned`, `legacy/unsigned` or `not_applicable` for source-free declarations. No persisted keys, versions or historical signatures are rewritten; publication uses the existing report findings/execution contract. Direct unsigned saves cannot bypass execution admission.
- **Frozen-scope control:** complete regenerated A/B/C publications equal the previous publications, not just their selected rates; operation counts remain **43/31/25**. The frozen JSON documents, their author and both acceptance-test files retain their original SHA-256 digests. No equations, units, conditions, histories or physical source revision change. Protected thermodynamics files are unchanged. This increment does not edit D1/U1 entries; during validation Claude independently marked D1 done and updated its criteria, and those concurrent edits are preserved.
- **Negative controls:** the degree-interval and direct-unsigned-save regressions both fail against the preserved pre-repair modules (2 failures, 0 errors, 1.099 s); they pass with the repair.
- **Independent verification (Claude, 23 October 2026; read-only). Verdict: accepted; `m1-kernel-backlog` is done.** `tests/test_kernel_audit_backlog.py` passes 8 tests (4.0 s).
  - *Defect 5, checked without re-walking the definition graph.* A copied Pint 0.26.1 registry had pi, tansec, ln10, wien_x, wien_u and eulers_number replaced by different values. All 1,038 registry units were then compared with `exact_conversion`. None of the 870 accepted units changes factor or offset. degree, arcminute, revolution, rpm, parsec, franklin and decibel are rejected. The rational controls stay exact, and degF stays affine. One unrelated, pre-existing observation: `normalizar('µ')` returns `u`, which Pint reads as the atomic mass unit.
  - *Defect 6.* Five tampering variants absent from the delivered tests were applied on a neutral abstract graph, each with the unchanged source and with a changed source: null, empty and wrong-key `sourceSignatures`, stripping only a descendant, and stripping all. Every variant is either rejected on replay or blocked at compile admission; none is admitted. The common compiler, v2 assembly and derived bridge all enforce the block.

*Documented limitations, confirmed as conservative rejections and not as defects.* Linear isolation of `u(t)` from `du/dt = -k u` is rejected as "nonpolynomial" (R4), which is a capability limit. Unresolved branch membership is rejected for open-endpoint coalescence (`x^2 = c`, `c ∈ (0,4]`, `x ∈ (0,2)`; D3), for multiple-parameter sign reasoning (D6) and for undeclared parameters (D7). The kernel also rejects nonpolynomial and degree > 2 inversions.

*Verified within the tested scope.* The kernel passes these checks:

- Exact branch selection in D2 gives `x = sqrt(c)` for `c ∈ [1,4]` and `x ∈ [1,2]`.
- SI-projected membership across mixed units is correct in both directions. D4 admits `v = b/(2 s)` for `b ∈ [10,20] m` and `v ∈ [18,36] km/h`. D4b rejects the numerically similar `v ∈ [5,10] km/h`, which a non-SI comparison would wrongly admit.
- A zero leading coefficient reduces the degree correctly. In D5, `a = 0` gives `x = 1/b`.
- Contradictory domains are rejected (D8).
- Division retains `x-1 ≠ 0` (R5).
- A repeated root is resolved correctly: `(x-a)^2 = 0` gives `x = a` (R6).
- Signed stale inputs are rejected, and a failed append leaves the definition unchanged (P3 and P4).
- Combinations are marked dependent (P5).
- Model and Transform rows are byte-identical for the draft path.

*Optional improvements.* Some conditions remain even when recorded domains already imply them, such as `b ≠ 0` with `b ∈ [1,2]` in D5 and `x ≠ 0` with `x > 0` in P6. Simplifying them depends on defect 3. The previously optional unsigned marker is implemented by the defect-6 repair: missing historical evidence is visible and blocks compilation.

*Unverified.* This audit did not test the following:

- the instantiated-model path for defect 1, beyond inspecting the code (`ensure_executable` and `attach_report` do not block non-blocking kinds);
- the Reynolds/Gauss operators and the physical producers;
- `sp.ask` soundness beyond the cases above;
- live HTTP or the browser.

*Existing suites (run sequentially from the repository root):* `python tests/test_mathematical_kernel.py` 15 OK; `test_mathematical_domains.py` 33 OK; `test_equation_manipulation.py` 6 OK; `test_workspace_handoffs.py` 8 OK; `test_equation_workbench.py` 36 OK (409 s); `node frontend/verificar_equation_workbench.mjs` passed (readers, operation handoff, source invalidation, editable topology; 9,654 rendered formulas). Expensive physics suites, TypeScript/ESLint and the Checklist were not run or regenerated. These suites pass while defects 1–4 hold, so passing results do not cover these cases.

*Does any defect block the next increment?* Yes, measured against M1 above, which supersedes the coefficient-case extension. Defects 1 and 2 are confirmed soundness defects on admitted operations, so D1 criterion 5 cannot pass while they remain. Defect 3 prevents two required checks: D1 criterion 2 requires explicit denominators and domain conditions, and U1's Solve validation must check "all applicable validity conditions"; neither can be done mechanically while those conditions are LaTeX strings. It would also block any later coefficient-case partition, because a partition `{c ≠ 0} ∪ {c = 0}` cannot be checked for disjointness or completeness, or enforced by later operations, while its predicates are strings. Defects 4–6 do not block M1 unless an M1 acceptance document integrates `1/x`, uses a non-SI angle or relies on unsigned history. Repairing defects 1–3 is the smallest justified next step. Each repair cites D1 criterion 2 or 5 as its justification:

1. Store each generated condition as a typed SymPy relation or `Contains`, keeping the LaTeX for display and the free-text reasons separate. Include the typed conditions in signatures, which requires a source-revision bump.
2. After every created equation, reject `zoo`, `nan` and `±oo`. Substitute specializations into inherited typed conditions, and reject any condition that becomes `False` or contradicts recorded domain premises.
3. Admit `differentiate` and `integrate` with respect to `s` only when the equation contains a dependent quantity with `s` as an argument. Record the typed premise that the equation holds identically for `s` in its interval. Otherwise reject.
4. Make integration results with `log` or fractional powers either use `log|·|` or require a recorded positivity premise.
5. Reject conversions whose Pint definition involves an irrational constant.

Regression criteria: R1, R1b, R2, R2b and P1 must be rejected with located messages. If repairs 4 and 5 are made, R3 must yield `log|x|` or require a recorded `x > 0`, and D1 must be rejected. Every control above must keep its current result, and the listed suites must still pass. M1 defers coefficient-case partitions (D1 criterion 2 accepts a declared regular regime), so they belong to the later backlog. When they are resumed, the smallest extension is an explicit partition of the leading coefficient for degree-one and degree-two isolation:

- a typed case `LC ≠ 0` with its branch evidence;
- a typed case `LC = 0` that reuses the existing degree-reduction and identity/inconsistent diagnostics;
- an exact disjointness check, proving that the pairwise conjunction is unsatisfiable;
- an exact completeness check, proving that the disjunction holds over the recorded parameter domains.

Decisive tests for this extension are `a*x = b` with `a ∈ [0,1]` (two cases, the second inconsistent or an identity depending on `b`), `a*x^2 + b*x = d` with `a ∈ [-1,1]`, the zero-singleton reuse in D5, and the P1 contradiction, which must remain rejected.

**Re-audit of the targeted validity repair (Claude, 22 October 2026).** Scope: the working tree after the repair below (`SOURCE_REVISION` `physical-equation-graphs-6` in both `core/modelo/equation_workbench.py` and `frontend/app/models/equationWorkbench.ts`). Kernel hashes at the start of the re-audit are in `.tmp/claude-kernel-reaudit/hashes-start.txt`; `dimensions.py`, `graph.py`, `provenance.py`, `session.py`, `equation_workbench.py` changed and `validity.py` is new since the first audit. The four original reproducers were re-run unchanged; supplementary cases are in `python .tmp/claude-kernel-reaudit/repro_reaudit.py` and `probe_power.py`. Read-only: no production code, tracked test, UI, saved model or server was changed.

*Regression criteria — verified.* R1 and R1b are rejected ("Calculus requires a declared quantity depending on the selected coordinate"); the same holds through the public draft API (H1). R2 and R2b are rejected as contradicting an inherited validity condition, also through the API (H2). P1 is rejected. R3 now yields `y(x) = C1 + log|x|` with the typed guard `x ≠ 0` and the `HoldsOnInterval` premise, and blocks execution. Guards are typed (`Ne`, `Ge`, `HoldsOnInterval`) and stored separately from prose reasons (P2 now shows empty string assumptions). A declared relation `y = (w-1)/(w-1)` keeps `w - 1 ≠ 0` after parsing simplifies it to `y = 1` (H6). A dependent-quantity control, `F(x) = k x` differentiated in `x`, gives `dF/dx = k` under the recorded premise (C1). Every original control is unchanged: R4, D3, D4b, D6, D7, D8, P3 (signed) and P4 reject; R5, R6, D2, D4, D5, P5 and P6 keep their results. Defects 5 (`degree` admitted as a 50-digit rational) and 6 (an unsigned operation replays on a changed source, P3) remain open, as the repair entry states.

*New confirmed defect N1 (high, same class as defect 2): substitution can assign a non-real value to a real quantity.* `validity.finite` rejects only `zoo`, `nan` and `±oo`, and `expression_guards` captures only negative-power denominators; even roots and logarithms of declared relations get no domain guard (only `integrate` adds them). Public API sequence H5: declare `yq`, `wq` (dimensionless), relation `yq = (wq-2)^(1/2)` (also `^0.5`), constraint `wq = 1`, substitute. Expected: rejection, because `wq - 2 ≥ 0` is violated. Actual: `yq = i` is accepted with `assumptions: []`, published byte-identically in Model and Transform; the rendered `i` is also indistinguishable from the unbound component index. Kernel-level R2c (`y = sqrt(x-2)`, `x = 1` → `y = I`) and R2d (`y = log x`, `x = -1` → `y = iπ`) are accepted with the execution-blocking flag `False`. Smallest repair, justified by D1 criteria 2 and 5: generate typed `Ge(base, 0)` guards for even-denominator rational powers and `Gt(argument, 0)` guards for real logarithms in `expression_guards` (so substitution specializes them like denominators), and reject any created equation containing a non-real number. Regression: H5, R2c and R2d reject; R3, C1 and every control above keep their results.

*Minor, non-blocking.* Rejection messages name the failed rule but not the violated condition (for example "contradicts an inherited validity condition" without `x - 1 ≠ 0`); the regression criterion asked for located messages. Conditions that simplify to `True` are still stored and displayed (`\text{True}` in D4 and D5). A relation written with `**` is rejected by the expression parser with an `ErrorExpresion` listing internal aliases instead of a mathematical message. R1c (`F + G(x) = k x + G(x)` in `x`) is accepted as `G' = k + G'` under the recorded `HoldsOnInterval` premise; this is sound as a conditional, but the kernel does not check whether a premise is satisfiable.

#### Targeted validity repair following the audit

This increment addresses audit defects 1–3 against D1 criteria 2 and 5 and corrects the real logarithm example in defect 4. After validating these repairs and the re-audit's N1 fix, work resumed on the M1-C heat connection through existing shared operations, as recorded in the fixed acceptance suite above. The separate `intercambios` numerical topology is not silently substituted for the authored heat relations.

Primary workspace: Model, using the shared mathematical kernel. Generated denominator and isolation guards are now typed predicates, separate from explanatory prose. Their expression structure and the units of quantities appearing only in guards participate in source signatures. Substitution specializes inherited guards, including guards retained after cancellation, and checks recorded domains. Graph insertion rejects non-finite equations; singleton/sign and bounded univariate contradiction checks reject the audit's forbidden substitutions and singular-coefficient isolation. Generic set membership is converted to relations before inequality reasoning; an unevaluated `Contains` is never passed as a scalar inequality.

Calculus now requires declared coordinate dependence and records the typed interval-identity premise instead of claiming that an arbitrary scalar equality can be differentiated. The existing product/chain-rule paths remain shared. Real logarithmic primitives retain absolute values and excluded-zero conditions; even-root primitives retain a radicand condition. New operation guards without numerical enforcement block Solve. The frontend and backend source revision advance together to `physical-equation-graphs-6`; Model/Transform rows retain the guards in the existing assumptions display. Saved invalid histories remain stored and inspectable, Undo is available, and neither Transform nor Solve may consume their valid prefix as a replacement model.

**Repair verification, 23 October 2026:** the complete `python tests/test_equation_workbench.py` run passes 38 tests in 424.922 seconds, followed by `node frontend/verificar_equation_workbench.mjs` (11,239 rendered formulas; readers, operation handoff and source invalidation pass). Kernel 27, domain 33, energy 10, thermodynamic 11, momentum 23, hydraulic 14, workspace-handoff 8, physical-graph 12 and manipulation 6 tests pass. The kernel suite confirms identical generated-predicate signatures in three separate Python processes with hash seeds 1, 2 and 31. TypeScript passes after `npx next typegen` removes stale generated references to the deleted laboratory; scoped ESLint passes. These are current working-tree checks, not earlier certificates. **The repair milestone remains in review for Claude's independent reproducers.**

The re-audit's N1 is also repaired: even-denominator rational powers retain nonnegative-radicand guards (including before parser cancellation), logarithms retain positive-argument guards, and graph insertion/parser evaluation reject non-real numeric expressions. Root and logarithm forbidden substitutions reject; zero remains admissible for a square root but not an inverse square root. Public symbolic replay retains an old invalid root history, blocks Transform/Solve and permits recovery. The shared source revision is now `physical-equation-graphs-7`. Claude must independently re-run H5, R2c and R2d before this finding is closed. Regression expectations remain independent: R1/R1b, R2/R2b and P1 reject; `d(log|x|)/dx = 1/x` is checked on both signs; existing product/chain, electrical and mechanical references retain their results. Two older tests had differentiated independent scalar quantities; they now declare the intended dependence explicitly.

Live HTTP verification on the current validation backend at 8004 rejects invalid scalar calculus, cancelled poles and rational/decimal root violations with 422 responses. Recovery, exact Python/Transform parity, stale-input rejection and Undo/Redo pass on isolated unsaved drafts. The former validation process was absent; a new owned process was started without touching 8005 or saved user data. The editor does not admit `log(...)` declarations, so logarithm guards are kernel evidence, not a claimed HTTP authoring feature. The workspace handoff renderer also passes 317 formulas. This remains API evidence, not U1 browser acceptance.

**Remaining limits:** no general predicate implication solver or numerical guard evaluator is delivered. Legacy physical reasons remain prose; they are not fabricated into typed historical facts. The fixed M1 documents, their used physical-proof chronology, exact numerical lowering and browser acceptance remain pending. D1/U1 stay uncertified; this repair does not authorize open-ended kernel expansion.

**Starting prompt for a new Claude chat:**

```text
Work in C:\Users\randa\Desktop\reactive_distillation.

Read AGENTS.md and its required documents in order, then CLAUDE.md. Apply .agents/skills/model-equation-deduction/SKILL.md. Read “M1 — Minimum usable multiphysics engine”, “Conversation handoff — 22 October 2026” and “Independent mathematical-kernel audit” in docs/engine/multiphysics-roadmap.md, including the latest typed-constant and endpoint-unit increment.

Execute that independent audit against the actual current working tree. The objective is a general mathematical engine below multiphysics: laws and explicit premises must lead to equations through real, replayable operations, without equipment-specific formulas or manufactured history. Prioritize mathematical correctness, dimensions/domain reasoning, validity conditions, provenance and exact Model → Transform → Solve → Results consistency. Existing passing tests do not establish correctness beyond their tested scope.

Keep production code, tracked tests, UI and saved user data unchanged. You may run tests and create isolated reproducers in .tmp/claude-kernel-audit/. Record confirmed findings, independent references, verification commands and remaining uncertainty in the existing audit findings/evidence subsection of docs/engine/multiphysics-roadmap.md. Do not create another report or handoff file. Do not implement the next feature or fixes during this audit. Do not restart servers, stop processes, commit or push.

Give a prioritized, reproducible assessment against D1/U1 and the fixed three-case M1 suite. Do not expand the minimum into unlimited kernel polishing; recommend Transform/Solve as soon as D1 passes. Distinguish actual defects from intentionally unsupported capabilities. If no defects are found, report the tested scope and limitations. All work and documentation must be in English.
```

The target is an extensible equation-authoring engine, not a finite menu of equipment formulas. This does not promise arbitrary physical validity or numerical solvability. Primary workspace: **Model**. Inputs are fundamental equations, explicit constitutive premises, quantities and dependencies, units, assumptions and topology. The output is a replayable expression graph with conditions and actual before/after equations. Transform and Solve must consume its exact revision; physical changes invalidate downstream artifacts, while inspection does not.

1. **General mathematical authoring — implemented increment; visual acceptance pending.** Support dependent quantities as well as constants, explicit application of conditions and relations, and equation scaling. Preserve input identities, dimensional checks, branch conditions and reversible history. Expose these operations in the existing right panel, including unbound symbolic models; no new editor or equipment templates.
2. **Shared physical source graphs — scoped migration verified; consolidation continues.** Matter, first-law, thermodynamic and linear/angular-momentum graphs use typed operations. Moving-boundary transport and the straight-path Cauchy bridge have algebraic/reference checks. Constitutive laws remain explicit premises. Complete chronological edit/proof correspondence, passage first-law pressure recovery, auxiliary wall/storage bridges and remaining numerical lowering before claiming general completion.
3. **Exact handoff and execution admission — continuous gate.** Replay the same operations in Model and Transform. New physical relations and conditions without numerical lowering must block execution of the previous system. General residual/DAE compilation is a separate Solve capability, not implied by symbolic authoring.
4. **Adversarial verification and retirement — continuous gate.** Check different quantities, dependencies, equation inputs, singular branches, dimensions, undo/redo, stale-source recovery and persistence against independent algebraic references. Retire duplicate producers only after parity. Update the canonical audit and Checklist; structural provenance alone does not certify mathematics.

Finishing an increment does not close this whole plan or certify that every model can already be deduced. The first increment adds dependent quantities, chain-rule substitution, classified premises, applied zero/dependency conditions, conditional equation scaling and dimensioned partial-integration constants. The version-3 symbolic request supports the same operations before components exist. Validation: 30 workbench tests, six kernel tests, eight workspace-handoff tests, 14 hydraulic tests, strict frontend readers with 2,853 rendered formulas, 1,391 canonical-layout/provenance checks, TypeScript, scoped ESLint and maintained-document links pass. Backend 8004 was restarted and an isolated live API request verified five recorded operations, changed algebra and Undo/Redo without modifying saved user models. Browser inspection remains unavailable because local browser control is disabled in Work; do not describe this as a completed live worksheet test. That milestone has since reached the scoped four-family migration above; the current mathematical-foundation increment and remaining sequence supersede this earlier next-step instruction.

**Authoring audit and current work (21 October):** the [Model audit](model-workspaces.md#current-authoring-and-solve-audit) remains the consolidation boundary. The shared worksheet now identifies physical regions by name, explains assumption scope, separates optional expression operations from physical activation, previews operations without persistence, and reports located hydraulic admission requirements. Model admission is independent of numerical evaluation; Solve separately checks values and initialization. Display-label changes no longer invalidate flat-assembly physics. The saved coupled pressure document still requires its missing material deductions to be replayed through the UI and a new live run; internal fixture success is not a substitute. Complete the projected-momentum provenance bridge and remaining family/constraint lowering before claiming the general authoring engine complete. Reusable hierarchy follows the approved [multiphysics composition architecture](../../ARCHITECTURE.md#multiphysics-composition-architecture): generic physical regions, pinned model instances and typed interfaces in the same four workspaces. Instance assembly is pending; no plant-specific editor or equipment-category contract is authorized.

Consolidate nonspatial thermodynamics and its consistency with compilation/results before advancing spatial work. This supersedes earlier statements about the next chronological phase or agent ownership: Codex is continuing the work directly. No new meshes, spatial transport or geometries are in the current delivery. Keep their existing behavior and regressions, but mark extensions deferred in the Checklist.

## Demonstrable versions (30 October 2026)

**User decision, 30 October 2026.** The project keeps its phases (F0–F10), M1, family deductions, F9 and cleanup. A separate **demo track** groups already-reviewed work into versions the user can show. A demo version never introduces a parallel implementation: each item is delivered inside its owning phase, and the demo milestone only records which delivered pieces make the version usable from start to finish (Model → Transform → Solve → Results). Approved order: Demo 0.1 → 0.2 → 0.3. Spatial work, 3D, CAD import and equipment sizing come after 0.3.

**Rules for every demo version**

1. Only committed work with an independent review enters a version. In-progress work stays on its phase.
2. A version is a closed set: named models, an offline executable (`/actualizar-ejecutable`) and a git tag `demo-0.N`. The user approves the tag and the push.
3. Each version adds to the previous one and never breaks it. The demo models of earlier versions become regression fixtures in `frontend/testing/modelFixtures.ts`, never in the product library.
4. Demo models are new examples requested by the user on 30 October 2026. They are built with the shared fundamental-law deduction tools, following the [derivation standard](model-workspaces.md#model-derivation-standard-and-audit). A missing operation is recorded as an Engine finding on its phase. It is never bypassed with an example-specific path.
5. When a phase delivers something showable, the next demo version is updated in this section and on the board. That is how the demonstrable version keeps pace with the project.

### Parallel plan for the two Claude accounts (user decision, 5 November 2026; `dec-claude-absorbs-codex`)

Codex usage is now limited, so the two Claude accounts take over its lanes. Codex stays optional, for light items it claims as before. Each account owns one lane of files, and each lane's deliveries get their independent review from the other account. Within an account, nested sessions follow the model tiers in `CLAUDE.md`.

**Step 0, consolidation (claude-b, first, with the user's commit approval).** About 155 reviewed but uncommitted files sit in the main checkout, mixing Claude and Codex work. Commit them file by file in logical groups after the fast tier and the affected pytest files pass, then push. After that, both accounts work only in worktrees cut from the pushed `fase-a-adopcion`. The main checkout only receives fast-forward merges of committed work. The reviewed F5-C1 delivery (approve with repairs) is included, and its repairs follow as new commits.

**Lane A, claude-a: product and interface (former Codex frontend lane).** Files: `frontend/app/models/`, `frontend/motores/`, `frontend/app/motor/`, `core/resultados/`, plus the generated Checklist.
- A1 (can start before step 0; read-only; Opus): independent reviews of claude-b deliveries:
  - F5 `reaction_jacobian_bounds` for ideal gas and UNIFAC liquids;
  - F8b SLE and the Peng-Robinson mixture EOS;
  - F9-R10, R11, R12 and R14;
  - Codex's RV-4 and CR-2 repairs.
- A2 (after step 0; Opus plans, Sonnet implements): the Demo 0.2 2D process diagram. It edits the network topology: blocks are template instances, lines are streams. It uses the existing typed ports and index-set networks and lives inside the existing Model right-hand panel and topology controls; it is not another editor.
- A3: the Demo 0.3 chart groundwork findings, including the Python round-trip of assessment-bearing results; Results presentation of indexed networks; user approval of `demo-01-ui`.
- A4 (later): Demo 0.3 generic control module and real-time stepping.
- claude-a also refreshes the board artifact, which it owns.

**Lane B, claude-b: physics, deduction and compiler (former Codex compiler lane plus Claude's thermodynamics).** Files: `core/termodinamica/`, `core/reactions/`, `core/balances/`, `core/compilador/`, `core/solvers/`, `core/mathematics/`, `core/modelo/` (the network and topology contract), `tests/`, `.github/`, `tools/ci/`.
- B1 (Opus for F1 and F2b, Sonnet for F3-F6): the F5-C1 review repairs.
- B2 (Opus deduction): `demo-02-column`. A countercurrent equilibrium-stage cascade from the generic stage, with condenser, reboiler and feed stage as variants of that stage, and block-sparse solving.
- B3: F5 liquid-phase equilibrium and thermal coupling, which unblock the reactive stage.
- B4 (small): CR-1, F9-R13, `tests/test_*.py` in CI, and the `validar_motor_balances.py` quarantine.

**Interfaces and synchronisation.**
- A2 consumes the topology contract that B owns (`core/modelo/index_sets.py`, `template_instance.py` and the typed ports). claude-a requests contract changes through the mailbox and does not edit those files.
- Shared documents (board, roadmap, Checklist, `model-workspaces.md`) are edited only by exact replacement, re-read immediately before each edit.
- The Demo 0.2 release needs A2, B2 and the cross reviews.
- Integrating into a dirty main checkout (from the claude-b handoff, 7 November 2026): commit in the session worktree file by file, cherry-pick into the main checkout, verify in a clean detached worktree with a `frontend/node_modules` junction (remove it with `cmd /c rmdir` before `git worktree remove`), then push. The main checkout can hold other agents' uncommitted hunks in the roadmap, board, Checklist and `datos.ts`: stage only your own lines by writing a HEAD-based blob (`git hash-object -w` plus `git update-index --cacheinfo`) and a plain `git commit`, never `git commit -- <path>`, which takes the whole working file. Split working files on LF and strip CR, because they mix CRLF and LF. In a clean checkout, `tsc` reports only the known missing Next `LayoutProps` type.
- Local noise, never commit: `frontend/tsconfig.json` (rewritten by a dev server), `frontend/.next-*`, `docs/ejemplos/modelo-comun/*.json` (last-digit drift from the fast tier), `.claude/launch.json`.
