**Demo 0.1 models authored (Claude, 31 October 2026; uncommitted, independent review pending).** Both blockers above are closed by the derived-execution work committed in 3214490 and by R3 (below, under F5). Both models are authored in Python through the shared fundamental-law operations (`SharedAuthor`, the physical journals and the recorded Thermodynamics closure), with no final RHS, closure value or caloric constant written by hand. They are frozen as generated TypeScript modules in `frontend/testing/demo01/` and registered in `frontend/testing/modelFixtures.ts` (group "Demo 0.1"), never in the product library. Each test checks that the frozen fixture equals a fresh authoring.
- *Batch reactor* (`tests/demo01_reactor.py`, `tests/test_demo01_reactor.py`, `batchReactor.ts`): ethanol and acetic acid (5 + 5 mol, 0.65 L rigid, liquid-only, liquid formation reference, UNIFAC Dortmund), from 320 K with a jacket at 340 K and UA 5 W/K over 3 h. The rates are in detailed balance with the package: kr(Tref) = kf(Tref)/K_x,eq(Tref), where K_x,eq = 3.671 is taken from the certified Gibbs minimum (Δν = 0). Er = Ef − Δ_rH(Tref) = 52.99 kJ/mol (van 't Hoff), with Ef = 50 kJ/mol and kf(340 K) = 1e-7 m³/(mol s) as demonstration values. Against the independent DOP853 Gibbs-Helmholtz route it agrees to 2e-9 mol, 6e-10 relative in U and 1.7e-6 K in T. T peaks at 341.15 K and settles at 340 K. The F5-C3 row reports 4.2e-5 (tolerance 1e-3): the run ends at the certified equilibrium.
- *Flash drum* (`tests/demo01_flash.py`, `tests/test_demo01_flash.py`, `flashDrum.ts`): 100 kg of liquid (20 % ethanol by mass) at 300 K and 1 atm, fed 0.5 kg/s at 330 K with a 200 kW duty. Vapour is vented in proportion to its holdup, and liquid leaves through a 3 cm² orifice driven by the level, over 3000 s. The vapour appears once, as a located event at 127.2 s, bracketed by the independent bubble point. The drum settles two-phase at 362.54 K, with the level rising from 0.21 to 0.28 m and vapour ethanol 0.356 against 0.058 in the liquid. Against the direct `core.mezclas` integration it agrees to 2e-6 kg, 1e-8 relative in H, 2e-5 K and 1e-7 m in level. The reference integration now caps its step at 5 s (`max_step`), so its trial stages stay inside the package domain on this horizon.
- Checks: the two demo test files (7 tests), `tests/test_derived_package_closure.py`, `tests/test_derived_package_reactor.py` and `tests/test_liquid_only_admission.py` pass. The fixture verifiers `verificar_model_derivations`, `verificar_material_journals`, `verificar_physical_journals` and `verificar_workspace_handoffs` pass with 44 templates. Still open: the frontend presentation of closure series (a pending extension, not a demo blocker) and RV-2/RV-3 under F4.
- *Independent review of the Demo 0.1 models and R3 (Claude review session, 31 October 2026): approve with repairs; none blocks the demo.* Rerun here: `tests/test_liquid_only_admission.py`, `tests/test_demo01_reactor.py` and `tests/test_demo01_flash.py` (23 passed, 25 min); `verificar_model_derivations` and `verificar_physical_journals` (44 templates); `build_board.mjs --check`. Independent confirmations: (a) R3 is one Gibbs function: at (298.15 K, 1e5 Pa) g_L, h_L and s_L equal the Psat route to 2e-12, and dh/dT = cp_L, Gibbs-Helmholtz and dg/dP = v_L hold. The DIPPR-100 conversion is right: Perry 2-153 gives A = 139640, B = -320.8, C = 0.8985 J/(kmol K), so cp_L(298.15 K) = 123.86 J/(mol K), against CRC 123.3 and Poling 123.1; the Psat route gave 120.3 there and 103.4 at 370 K. (b) The vapour-liquid fingerprints are unchanged: all 387 constructible one- to three-species `Sistema(...).huella()` of every package equal those of HEAD, run from a `git archive HEAD core` copy. Only the liquid-only fingerprints change, as they should, and no file pins the old ones. (c) The reactor detailed balance holds: Er = Ef - Delta_rH is the van 't Hoff sign, and Delta_rH is partial molar, which is the right quantity for d ln K_x/dT at fixed x. Gibbs K_x against kf/kr is 3.9048/3.9222 at 320 K, 3.6710/3.6710 at 340 K and 3.4411/3.4612 at 360 K. The engine Arrhenius form (`core/balances/reaction_equations.py:62-67`, R = 8.31446261815324) matches the reference (R = 8.314462618). (d) The flash event is located well: the engine reports 127.209 s, and a 401-point reference refinement crosses between 127.125 and 127.219 s. The level settles (0.2788 m at 2900 and 3000 s). (e) Both fixtures equal a fresh authoring byte for byte, and nothing under `frontend/app` imports `frontend/testing/demo01` or `modelFixtures`. The derivation standard is met: the demo files only supply data to the reviewed `author_reactor`/`author_drum` shared-law authoring. Findings: **RD-1** (medium, provenance): `reverseRate` = 2.724088784474497e-08 and `reverseActivation` = 52993.8785542915 enter `batchReactor.ts` as bare data with origin `estudio`. The relation kr = kf/K_x,eq(Tref), Er = Ef - Delta_rH that produced them is recorded only in `tests/demo01_reactor.py`, so changing kf, Ef, Tref, the charge or the package in the Model silently breaks the detailed balance. Repro: `grep -n reverseActivation frontend/testing/demo01/batchReactor.ts`. Repair: record the derivation in the datum's provenance now. Recording it as a shared operation (reverse rate from the package equilibrium) is an Engine capability finding. **RD-2** (low, scope): with UNIFAC, K_x is composition dependent, so power-law concentration kinetics end at the Gibbs minimum only on this charge's reaction line, and only to first order in T away from Tref. A 2:1 ethanol charge has Gibbs K_x(340 K) = 3.417 against the kinetic 3.671 (7 %). Repro: `minimise_gibbs` on `PackagePhase(Sistema('unifac-dortmund-public', SPECIES, solo_liquido=True), 'liquido', anchor='liquid')` with n0 = (10, 5, 0, 0) at 340 K. Repair: state the scope in the demo docstring or Checklist; activity-based rates are a later extension. **RD-3** (low, test strength): `test_vapour_appears_once_at_the_independent_bubble_point` brackets the event only within one 37.5 s output interval, and its bubble point is the same `core.mezclas.equilibrio_PH`. The agreement is in fact 0.094 s, but the test would accept a 37 s error. Repro: integrate `reference_drum(np.concatenate([[0], np.linspace(112.5, 150, 401)]), DEMO)` and find the first state with vapour. Repair: refine the reference around the event and assert, for example, |t_event - t_ref| < 0.5 s. **RD-4** (low, data): Perry 2-153 runs 2.4-4.3 % above the VDI table (thermo `VDI_TABULAR`, first point 391.15 K): 151.5 against 145.3 J/(mol K) at 391 K. `test_cp_against_independent_sources` passes this at 5 %, so the margin is 0.8 %. Repair: document the deviation next to the source; tighten only with a second independent table. **RD-5** (info, range): narrowing `T_rango` to 289.81-391.05 K (the melting point and roughly the normal boiling point) is acceptable for Demo 0.1 (T stays within 320-341.2 K). It does cap any pressurised liquid-only acetic acid system at 391 K, so record it as a stated limitation. **Demo 0.1 script (about 10 minutes, offline executable or dev servers).**
1. *Home → Engine.* The library starts empty by decision. Explain Model → Transform → Solve → Results.
2. *Import model → `batch-reactor.rage-model.json`.* The Engine validates the formulation on import.
3. *Model.* Component mass conservation and the first law start from the local fundamental laws. In the right-hand panel, show the recorded assumptions: fixed boundary, impermeable, uniform generation, the liquid-only phase set and the liquid formation reference.
4. *Transform → Validate and use derived formulation.* Show the replayed operations (substitute, expand, isolate) and their validity conditions.
5. *Solve → Run derived model.* RK45 over 3 h, with T from the UNIFAC Dortmund UV closure at every step.
6. *Results.*
   - The amounts chart: reactants 5 → 1.71 mol, products 0 → 3.29 mol.
   - The K chart: 320 K heating to a 341.15 K overshoot, then the 340 K jacket.
   - Numerical diagnostics: closure 1.9e-20, equilibrium limit 4.2e-5 against 1e-3 (the run ends at the certified Gibbs minimum, because kr and Er come from K(T)), conservation 1e-16.
7. *Import `flash-drum.rage-model.json`.* Same steps. Results (about 2 min to solve):
   - T rises from 300 K to 362.5 K.
   - The total volume jumps when the vapour appears at 127.2 s; show the `phase_present` V series.
   - The liquid volume and level rise.
   - Vapour ethanol 0.356 against 0.058 in the liquid.
   - Certificate 2.6e-4 against 1, conservation 7e-16.
8. *Close.* State the limits: identity selection, one vapour plus one liquid, and concentration power-law kinetics.

**User decisions (1 November 2026, `dec-demo02-dynamic-first`).** These govern every equipment template.
1. *Everything is deduced dynamic.* Each template carries matter, energy and momentum, deduced from the fundamental laws through recorded operations. A steady state is never a separate deduction: it is the same dynamic model passed through the steady-state Transform, which sets the accumulation rates to zero (the existing version-5 path, extended to package-closure volumes).
2. *Templates that represent a real piece of equipment are marked as such* (an optional, versioned template marker in the common model). Other templates remain general deductions.
3. *Each template is connectable but is never connected here.* Its open boundary streams are typed ports: inlet ports with a declared exterior state, outlet ports drawn from a donor phase. Connecting instances is the job of a future 2D flowsheet (Aspen/DWSIM style). That flowsheet sits on top of this engine; the Model view does not wire equipment together.
4. *Assumptions describe physics, never an operation or a piece of equipment.* Examples: "the outgoing phases are in equilibrium with each other", "vapour holdup is negligible next to liquid holdup", "an outlet draws one phase of the donor volume". Nothing is called a "tray" or "column" assumption: a stage is a control volume under such assumptions.
5. *Visible results come first.* Each template ships as an importable file that runs in the app (Import → Transform → Solve → Results), like the Demo 0.1 models.

No Demo 0.1 fixture is marked; complete equipment derivations and wiring remain pending. - **Equipment templates.** A template is a recorded, parameterised deduction that is replayed for each instance; it is never a pasted final formula. Owners: Model workspace and Codex for `core/modelo/`. Claude adds the thermodynamic closures.
- **Multi-stage operations.** One generic stage j is deduced once, as a control volume with indexed streams. A column or a CSTR train is a network: an index set j = 1…n, the connectivity (liquid from j−1, vapor from j+1) and special pieces (condenser, reboiler, feed stages) that are variants of the same stage under other assumptions. n is a Solve datum, so changing it never repeats a deduction. This builds on `core/ensamblaje_red.py` and the F4 item "multiple flows, branching, mixing and larger networks".
- **Implicit domain constraints.** Mole fractions in [0, 1], amounts ≥ 0, T > 0 and P > 0 are declared once in the common model, always present like thermodynamics. Solve enforces them through log variables or step-to-boundary, as in the F5-EQ-1 repair, and never by clipping a result. Owning phases: F3 and F9.
  **Partial solver work (Codex, 6 November 2026; not delivered as the implicit-domain capability).** The generic Newton kernel now starts an outward box-crossing trial at 99% of the distance to its declared bound, before Armijo backtracking, and rejects an unrepresentable or outward boundary step as `nonlinear_domain`. The analytic trace-amount and interior-root tests plus existing Newton regressions pass. This does not infer physical domains: `ExplicitSystem` builds `dominio` only from `modelo.restricciones`, and steady/nonlinear admission uses declared bounds and guards. The earlier RK45/BDF path could evaluate an RHS or numerical Jacobian before the accepted-state domain check. Required next: add versioned physical-quantity domain metadata in the common model for mole fractions [0,1], amounts [0,∞), T and P (0,∞), including closure and algebraic quantities; intersect it with user domains without inferring meaning from names or units. In each compiler lowering propagate quantity IDs, open endpoints and domain evaluators to every steady/nonlinear unknown and dynamic state, closure and Jacobian probe, including scaled coordinates; reject contradictions and report quantity ID, bound and location. The integrator must enforce those domains on every stage, numerical Jacobian and dense-output/event probe, use a positivity-preserving transformation or boundary-limited/rejected step, and preserve a localized admissible prefix without clipping. Verify an actual near-disappearing-phase flash and reactant-depleting reactor against independent references, plus unchanged valid trajectories, before marking the capability complete. These model/compiler paths are outside this Codex claim and concurrently owned.
  **Integrator partial repair (Codex, 6 November 2026; independent review: approve with repairs; committed).** RK45/BDF guard declared nonnegative margins before RHS, supplied Jacobian and event calls; BDF finite-difference RHS probes use the same guard and a one-sided Jacobian fallback when initialization encounters a boundary. Invalid stages retry at smaller steps and a minimum-step failure records the domain description, margin, time and step without clipping. Dense-output queries are guarded. An exactly reached outward boundary emits an exhaustion event. The first-order A → B analytic solution and a stiff relaxation against DOP853 pass for RK45 and BDF; a drain approaches its finite boundary without negative RHS evaluations, and a time-boundary case reaches it exactly. The first-order reaction approaches zero asymptotically rather than reaching zero in finite time. Relative to the pre-change integrator at HEAD, the valid first-order trajectory has identical step counts and bit-identical final state and 101 dense samples for both methods. Limitations: arbitrary dense interpolants are checked at query points, not certified throughout each interval; a violation discovered after publication cannot retroactively reduce the step. Domain failures during event reintegration or initialization retain the valid prefix but are not always retried down to h_min. Near a finite boundary the minimum-step stop is localized to the accepted side, not necessarily the exact mathematical event. Physical quantity identities, typed bounds and open endpoints are not supplied by the current domain-margin protocol. Independent review and these repairs remain pending; do not mark implicit physical domains complete. Part A (versioned physical domains and compiler propagation) waits for flowsheet integration.
  **Follow-up (Codex, 6 November 2026; independent review: approve with repairs; committed).** A failed dense query retries from the segment start with a halved maximum step and transactionally rebuilds its suffix and later event records; reaching h_min raises a located DomainViolation rather than publishing the invalid sample. Domain failures while locating or reintegrating a signature event discard provisional segments and retry the parent step with a quarter step; a minimum-step failure identifies the margin, time and step. Tests inject dense and event failures, cover both retry and rejection, and compare a finite constant-rate drain with its analytic depletion time. Forty-one tests pass, including Newton and event regressions; the unchanged reaction matches the pre-change HEAD bit for bit at the final state and 101 dense samples on both methods. This is still partial: on a constant-rate drain with h_min=1e-3 the accepted-side stop precedes the analytic root by 4.71e-4 (RK45) and 1.27e-3 s (BDF), so no tolerance-controlled mathematical root is certified. Rejected stage data alone are not a certified interpolant across the boundary; a protected bracket and error-controlled event localization remain required. A domain failure while constructing a retry can still escape the retry loop, and dense checking only covers queried points. Part A remains pending until flowsheet integration.

IAPWS-95 at 273 K is an extrapolated liquid-vapour comparison below the triple point. The existing Model controls and TypeScript workbench reader now accept source metadata and recorded typed domains; `frontend/verify_custom_relations.mjs` verifies the production endpoint and reader, including incompatible units, replay recovery after test-document source changes and undo/redo. It does not exercise browser interactions. Pending: independently review both subsets; add a reversible edit for an earlier source without discarding dependent history; propagate and enforce relation predicates in `core/compilador/derived.py` after the F5 B3 part 2 claim is released and verify runtime rejection outside the range. No complete runtime capability is claimed.

**Independent re-review of the Codex repairs (claude-b session, 6 November 2026; read-only): all four items approve.** RV-5 (`ResultadosHoja.tsx` `PublishedChecks`): routes ids with prefix `reactions.equilibrium_limit.` to a diagnostics section without a verdict, and every other check keeps the explicit Passed/Failed state in the indexed, steady and technical views (the steady and indexed views previously showed no state); the saved n=3 result carries those ids at 0.15/0.068/0.030 against 0.001, as expected; the rendering regression covers both the diagnostic and a real failure. Cosmetic: the technical view no longer formats values with `toExponential(3)`. RV-6: recomputed from the saved n=2 and n=3 HTTP results, the maximum steady-versus-dynamic difference is 2.065e-9 K (stage 3 of n=3; stages 1 to 3 at n=3 grow 2.2e-10, 8.2e-10, 2.1e-9), so 1e-7 K is a 48-fold margin; the deviation grows with stage count, so re-measure before extending the loop beyond n=3. RV-7: the 17.28 ratio equals 984.295/56.945, and the replay-inclusive gains equal (21.232+74.845)/(21.232+8.756)=3.20 at n=5 and (111.380+984.295)/(111.380+56.945)=6.51 at n=40, matching the table. RV-2: in a scratch worktree at HEAD, removing the residual-scale comparison (`steadyContract.ts:140`) leaves the pre-repair verifier green (83 checks) while the new verifier fails with "Re-signed ... scale edit must be rejected"; removing the upper-bound comparison (`steadyContract.ts:144`) leaves the pre-repair verifier green with the signature-mutation loop disabled (43 checks) and fails the new one. Rerun in the main checkout: inverse verifier 87 checks, `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, board check passes. Not rerun: the live n=2/n=3 HTTP flow.

**Flowsheet of recorded instances (claude-b, 6 November 2026; committed 973a700..f22de7a; independent review: approve with repairs, re-check: approve).** This resolves the 2D topology dependency. The optional `rage.flowsheet` v1 carries, on a shell model with one shared temporal study, embedded equipment templates, separately identified instances with their own declared data, explicit `instance.port -> instance.port` material connections, explicit exposures and declared recycles, plus a presentation-only `flowsheetLayout`. Admission: Python and TS check direction, kinds and the SI variable contract, unique binding, self-connection, CAS sets, package model and recorded energy reference, dangling ports, and undeclared or false cycles; both readers give identical codes and paths for all 36 parity cases, and a connection is located by identity at `flowsheet.connections.<id>`; CR-2 is enforced at assembly (`index_network_pressure_mismatch`). Invalidation: instances replay through the template-instance service under per-instance namespaces; source, instance, connection, compilation and result signatures hash numbers by binary64 value, so a browser JSON round trip changes none of them; the source signature excludes `flowsheetLayout`, so moving blocks keeps Transform, Solve and Results, while any topology or data edit gives `stale_flowsheet_execution`. Execution: `FlowsheetDerivedSystem` reuses the indexed connected assembly; steady mode is the existing zero-accumulation specialization; Results reuse `indexedNetwork` v2 metadata with instance position as j. Recycles are structural only, not executed. Verification: the network R1 -> heated liquid drum -> R2 (two templates, three identities) gives steady temperatures 335.243964 / 344.588553 / 345.642769 K and conversions 0.349817 (equal to the single CSTR) / 0.543864; the 20000 s BDF run matches the author's independent reference to 9.7e-8 relative, with element and energy budgets 3.7e-16 and 7.8e-12. The reviewer's own simultaneous reference agrees to 2.5e-10 in steady state and 1.2e-8 with LSODA over 20000 s. Repairs applied and re-checked: F1 portable binary64 signatures with browser round-trip regressions; F2/F3 Python/TS reader parity for non-string references and prototype keys; F4 identity connection paths; F6/F8 wording (the drum's `no_reaction` holds because the esterification kinetics belong to the reactor's catalyst; no column stage is connected yet). Regressions: flowsheet module 8 passed, TS parity verifier 74 checks, indexed lowering and template instances 16, `tsc` and the fast tier. Finding: a flash drum with the esterification species is blocked because acetic acid is admitted only in a liquid phase (`REGISTRO_SOLO_LIQUIDO`), so the chain's drum records the liquid-only assumption. Open: a phase-compatibility rule before vapour ports become connectable; an ethanol-water flash network with the recorded Demo 0.2 flash drum; an acetic-acid vapour association model for reactor -> flash; an executed recycle once a mixer or two-inlet template exists; heat and work connections (v2).

**demo-02-column committed (claude-b, 7 November 2026; independent review: approve with repairs, none blocking).** The countercurrent equilibrium-stage cascade reuses one recorded stage (the drum recipe, operations 1-100); the two extra inlets enter the same component and energy balances through recorded `declare_relation` + `substitute` pairs (operations 101-118); total mass is a check and reconstruction, not a posed balance; equilibrium is the `select_closure` package closure; condenser, feed and reboiler variants change only recorded data. The reviewer's own steady MESH (component flows, chemical-potential equality from `Sistema.mu`) agrees with the engine to <= 3e-11 K and <= 1.2e-11 relative flow in seven cases (n = 5 and 10, feed on stages 2 and 4, near-total reflux, n = 2 combined condenser over a fed reboiler); engine BDF agrees with LSODA (rtol 1e-10) to 1.1e-8 relative at n = 5; envelope closure 1e-15 (total), 2e-8 J/s (energy); infeasible specifications fail with coded errors, never wrong answers. Tests at the tip: test_demo02_column 12, indexed lowering and index sets 16, flowsheet 8, fast tier green. R1 applied at commit: the bare common-model document is renamed `docs/ejemplos/demo-0.2/column.index-set.json`, because Import model accepts only `rage.model-file` v1 wrappers. Follow-ups: R2 (core) - a dynamic run that dries the reboiler's liquid donor raises an uncaught `Detencion` inside the BDF re-initialisation because `IndexedDerivedSystem` lacks `stop_from_exception`, and the network guards keep stage-local keys; add both with a regression. R3 - the reactive stage named in the original milestone was not delivered and is a pending extension (B3 thermal coupling now exists). `column_model` refuses n < 3 although the index set admits n = 2.

**Codex RV-4 / CR-2 repairs (5 November 2026; re-review pending).** Lowering instance keys now encode bound constants with float.hex(), decoding only inside evaluation, so signed zero and adjacent binary64 values cannot share an instance. The regression verifies both sign preservation and cache reuse. Indexed network assembly now checks the recorded discharge pressure against the receiving package volume pressure and admits only equal positive prescribed values; unequal or unresolved/variable pressures produce located `index_network_pressure_mismatch` before either dynamic or steady solving. The check uses replayed relations and volume identities, with no equipment-specific pressure formula. Dynamic pressure coupling remains pending; equality is not inferred merely from equal initial values.

**Independent physics review of the CSTR and train (claude-b, 5 November 2026).** demo-02-cstr: approve with repairs; demo-02-stages physics: approve with repairs. The replayed deduction starts from component mass, energy and momentum laws; V(t) is one control-volume quantity in concentrations and sources; the jacket is a constitutive relation; formation enthalpies carry chemical energy once (a sensible-enthalpy form with an explicit reaction-heat term gives the same roots within 1.4e-6 K); steady is zero accumulation of the same rows. An independent simultaneous reference reproduces conversions 0.349816725/0.521552009/0.597046442/0.644943481 and the compiled n=1, n=2 roots to ~1e-12. Repairs: CR-1 the "no distributed wall friction" premise is false for the CSTR path data (laminar f L/D ~ 100-150 vs K = 8); CR-2 (PR-6) stage discharge pressure is not coupled to the receiving stage; CR-3 numeric initial jacket heat datum; CR-4 record the liquid-only, uniform-pressure and constant-molar-volume premises; CR-5 open wording items PR-1..PR-4. Combined physics commit waits for the f5 release of derived.py (PR-8).

**CR-1 consequence, open at the claude-b close (7 November 2026; uncommitted in worktree `interesting-swanson-797545`, report `demo02-physics-repairs-report.md`).** The repaired outlet path shortens the flow inertance time from 0.59 s to 3.8 ms, which makes the CSTR path stiff: SciPy Radau needs about 800-1400 LU factorizations per simulated second and SciPy BDF about 3900 LU over 600 s, against 16 LU (4.3 s wall) for LSODA, so the CSTR and flowsheet references moved to LSODA. That switch is not yet verified against the 3e-6 tolerances or a tight Radau case, and the model's own 60000 s runtime is unmeasured (24 s for a 60 s horizon, 18 s of it lowering). If the cost is unacceptable, CR-1 needs path data with a longer inertance time.

**Demo 0.2 demo script acceptance (Codex prompt 6).** Import gives a new model id and clears Results by design, so the smoke test is: import keeps the diagram, topology and data, and re-solving reproduces the results.

### Demo 0.3 — see and control

Results chart groundwork delivered (2 November 2026; review pending): optional rage.result-charts v1 selects published result series for time_series, xy, parametric, final-value bar and table views. Existing Results owns configuration/rendering; old graficas is retained unchanged and switching is explicit. Python chart-data converts selected display values and event coordinates through core.unidades; affine units, nulls and phase-absence gaps remain explicit. An optional chartReference v1 pins a strict result for labelled steady/dynamic overlays without changing physical execution signatures. Scalar steady overlays are constant reference lines over a sampled time extent, not simulated trajectories. Spatial axes, meshes and multi-axis series reject; future spatial support requires another explicit version. Verification: 137 headless chart checks over fresh Demo 0.1 drum/reactor and Demo 0.2 drum results; 30 production-client HTTP result-flow checks; 35 indexed-stage HTTP checks; TypeScript, all 31 fundamental scripts, 54 Demo publication checks, 131 derivation checks and 1476 physical checks pass. Headless rendering checks production React/Recharts element/data/marker structure and static markup, not browser pixels. Own backend PIDs were stopped.

**Indexed Results presentation (6 November 2026; review pending).** The opt-in rage.result-charts v1 defaults now select published steady stage profiles and group dynamic stage series by published identity, time axis and unit. Stage labels distinguish traces; Python remains the source of every value and null gap. The existing generic chart controls select other published series. The v1 limit is 100 charts and 100 series per chart; defaults retain the first 100 charts and split larger stage groups. Existing saved layouts are unchanged. `tests/test_indexed_chart_publication.py` generated a connected two-stage dynamic and steady network and checked every chart point, temperature and both phase-composition profiles through the common reader and renderer preparation (1 passed; two divide-by-zero warnings during evaluation). `verificar_result_charts.mjs` passed 137 checks; `verificar_indexed_results.mjs --connected` passed 60; TypeScript passed. The separate column producer is under an active Claude claim; this generic presentation has not yet been verified against a newly generated column result. Chart layout changes do not invalidate scientific results; source or Solve changes retain existing invalidation rules.

**Reader finding for Claude/common Results owner (RV-1 closed, 6 November 2026):** A newly solved Demo 0.1 dynamic drum result, read and serialized by the app-flow JavaScript, is accepted by `core.resultados.leer_resultados` with `resultSignatureVersion: 2`; `tests/test_assessment_roundtrip.py` also passes 10 tests including an actual Node JSON round trip. The five stale 2 November flash and batch files were removed and regenerated; all five pass the Python reader. The full app flow passed 65 checks and the chart verifier passed 137. The indexed execution, index sets, indexed results and template instance pytest-to-Node pairs passed; their producers are `test_indexed_execution_contract.py`, `test_index_sets.py`, `test_indexed_results.py` and `test_template_instance.py`. The new `test_demo02_app_prerequisites.py` generates fresh app-flow results against an isolated backend for the two live HTTP verifiers; it passed in 450 s followed by the 137 chart checks. These live checks need `NEXT_PUBLIC_API_URL` set to that backend and no Next.js server; they repeat slow solves. No CI-owned file was edited. The original finding was: the live HTTP Demo 0.1 drum result passes the strict TS reader but `core.resultados.leer_resultados` rejects the JavaScript round-tripped JSON at procedencia.assessment with 'Assessment does not match the published result or certification evidence.' The old reproducer was the 2 November `.tmp/demo02-app-results/demo-0.1-flash-drum-dynamic.json`; the file at that path has since been regenerated. No protected reader was edited. Chart conversion does not re-certify a result: the client validates the scientific result, selects exact rows, and Python strictly validates/converts only the display payload. The scientific evidence is retained unchanged. Full Demo 0.3 still depends on Demo 0.2 release, control equations and real-time stepping; this authorization advances only chart groundwork.


- **Results chart engine.** A chart specification in the [results contract](../contracts/results-format.md), with several chart types and room for meshes later.
- **Visible handoffs.** Each workspace shows what it receives, what it hands on and what a change invalidates. This extends the existing [visible mathematical handoffs](model-workspaces.md#visible-mathematical-handoffs-20-october-2026).
- **Generic control module.** Sensor, controller (PID first) and actuator are equations attached to any dynamic model, not only to chemical ones. They are declared in Model; the sampling and the solver interaction belong to Solve.
- **Real-time stepping.** An incremental integrator accepts disturbances while it runs: flows, levels and set points.

### After Demo 0.3

- F7 spatial work (meshes, discretization, PDE solvers, mesh visualisation) stays deferred until the user resumes it.
- 3D flowsheet.
- CAD import: STEP or STL as geometry for meshing. Drawing CAD or technical drawings inside the simulator is out of scope.
- Equipment sizing builds on the F3 slice-5 inverse specifications, for example the diameter that meets a pressure drop. Plant design algorithms come later.

**External integration rule (from now on).** The engine runs headless through a versioned JSON contract over HTTP or as a Python package. The web interface is one client; a future 3D environment would be another. This restates the rule that the interface does not calculate.

### Distribution and landing page (requirements; user decision, 6 November 2026; `dec-distribution-download-first`, open `dec-product-offer`)

Work on the landing page starts after Demo 0.2. Until then these requirements constrain current work and are not a task.

**Distribution model (taken).** The simulator is a free download that anyone can install and run locally: the desktop app with its local Python backend. The user does not operate a hosted compute service, so local machines carry the computation and maintaining the backend means release engineering (installers, updates), not server operations. GitHub serves technical users (code, issues, releases); the landing page serves non-technical users. The user also wants to learn to sell a product through it.

**Constraints on current work.**
- The current app assumes one trusted local user: models are stored in the browser and the backend has no authentication, quotas or isolation. It must never be exposed on a public server as it is.
- New work must not deepen that assumption without a clear boundary. Keep the backend stateless, keep models as versioned documents, and keep long solves suitable for asynchronous jobs, so that a hosted option stays possible later without a rewrite.
- The landing page is a separate static site. It is not a page, route or frontend of the simulator.

**Landing page content (first version).**
- One sentence, without jargon, that states the problem the simulator solves.
- A short video or animation of the Demo 0.2 flow: flowsheet diagram, Solve, verified Results.
- Windows download, with system requirements.
- Three to five short tutorials, from "my first reactor" to "a network of equipment", plus plain-language explanations of the method (Model → Transform → Solve → Results, deduction from fundamental laws).
- A "why trust it" section: every formula is checked against independent references, and every model is deduced from fundamental laws with recorded assumptions.
- An optional email sign-up for news, to measure interest. Analytics, if any, respect privacy.

**Product requirements for the download (harder than the page itself).**
- A signed Windows installer, so the operating system does not flag it as unsafe, and an update channel.
- A guided first run for someone who has never seen the program.
- One product name used everywhere. The README says "Rage Engine" while the executable is `SimuladorDR`; settle one English, searchable name before publishing.

**Open decisions (`dec-product-offer`), to settle before building the page.**
- **License.** Open source (MIT or Apache for reach, GPL to keep derivatives open) or a free closed binary. This determines what can be sold later.
- **Name and brand.**
- **First audience.** Students and teachers of chemical engineering are the suggested starting point: the pain is clear, they share within communities and they do not require industrial certification. Industry and researchers (a Python package of the engine) come later.
- **Later monetization**, which leaves the free base unchanged: courses, advanced tutorials, support or professional features.

### Research dossiers (planned; Gemini not yet in use)

When the user brings Gemini in, it acts as a researcher and never writes code. Before an equipment item or a physics is deduced, it delivers one dossier per topic in `docs/references/<topic>.md`, with fixed sections:

1. Governing equations with their sources (book or article, with page).
2. Standard assumptions and where they fail.
3. Validation cases with published numbers: inputs and expected results.
4. Parameters and correlations with their validity ranges.

Claude checks every equation numerically before it is used. No dossier formula enters the code without that check. Gemini may also give a second mathematical opinion on a recorded deduction. These dossiers are reference material, not plans, so they do not conflict with the documentation maintenance standard.

This plan belongs to the independent engine `/motor`. The earlier code and `/laboratorio` are background and references; their capabilities do not automatically count as implemented in `/motor`. [docs/history/build-log.md](../history/build-log.md) is a historical log, not the state of this plan.

This document keeps the authorized scope and the dependencies. It does not enable capabilities by itself. No pending item is declared done because its contract was written. Delivery reports and annexes bring evidence to a phase; they are not alternative plans.

## Single progress and continuity record

Update this record when a delivery arrives. Do not create another board with different states in coordination notes, reports or contracts.

### Progress percentages (user-facing; user decision, 6 November 2026; `dec-progress-table`)

The user follows overall progress with this table. Every Claude or Codex delivery that changes a board status updates the affected rows and the total in the same change. The goal it measures is `dec-nonspatial-100-pde-ready`: 100% without spatial execution, while the kernel can deduce PDEs.

**Method.** Each row is scored from the board.
- Status gives the score: done = 100, review ≈ 85, planned = 0.
- An active row gets a stated estimate of the delivered fraction, based on its recorded sub-items.
- Deferred items are excluded: F7 spatial execution, F9-R8 and the native v2 retirement.
- The total weights the blocks as follows: phases and PDE 40%, Demo 0.2 20%, Demo 0.3 10%, and M1, families, reliability, cleanup and Demo 0.1 together 30%.
- A percentage is a judgement from recorded evidence, never a claim of acceptance. A row reaches 100 only when its milestone is done.

### Continuity rule

- Claude continues the milestone already under way; Codex integrates the latest verifiable delivery and prepares the next point that does not require inventing a contract or physics.
- Each advance is recorded in this document with its phase/milestone ID, owner, evidence and concrete pending item. Where the detailed foundations live does not change the priority.
- A cross-cutting incident is added to F9 with the affected operation and its scope. It blocks only the part that cannot be run or certified; it does not restart completed deliveries or automatically stop another phase.
- If an incident prevents meeting a criterion of the active milestone, it is fixed within that milestone. A broader reprioritization is the user's decision, not the creation of a new document.
- A partial delivery keeps what was checked. The next advance starts from the identified pending item, without assigning the whole context again.
- For each phase: first the canonical contract and data, then reader/UI/service, finally evidence and items. Do not create provisional fields the backend does not publish yet.

## 1. Delivered base

| Area | Available | Limit that remains |
|---|---|---|
| Matter | Conservation per component, accumulation, streams and coupled volumes | The thermodynamic assembly does not yet accept every possible expression or closure |
| Energy | U/H, enthalpy transport, heat, boundary work, prescribed pressure or rigid vessel | Limited scope of geometry, models and accepted relations |
| Water | IAPWS-95, TP/PH/UP/UV and TV where applicable, L–V coexistence | Critical region excluded; ice only in static evaluation |
| Mixtures | Ideal and NRTL; stability and split in general dimension | Current catalog of seven species; NRTL data incomplete for many sets |
| Cross-checked dimension | Ideal up to seven species; NRTL up to four in sets with data | Three phases and three volumes accepted; they are not theoretical limits of the architecture |
| States | Phase fractions, compositions, potentials and provenance | Additional properties and models need their own data and cross-checks |
| Diagrams | Binary T–xy and P–xy; P–T of water with ice | No complete LL/LLV/SLE plotting, folds or N-component slices |
| Solution | Coupled transient within the published combinations | General steady-state, inverse and DAE solution is missing |
| Verification | Certificates, conservation and second law where evaluable | Stability by finite search and phase watching at the step's points |
| UI | Sheets, editing, Undo/Redo, units, count and charts per variable | Future extensions must keep the same compact path |
| UNIFAC | Original, Dortmund and the LLE table in the independent engine: state, energy, networks and diagrams | Qualitative prediction; ideal vapor up to 5 bar. LLE between 283.15 and 313.15 K; no group for methanol |
| Momentum | Recorded Cauchy reductions and scoped reduced hydraulics | General constrained, energy/rotor, real-fluid and spatial momentum coupling remain pending |

Sources of the current state: [N delivery](thermodynamics-n-components.md), [N integration](../history/frontend/integration-delivery-n-components.md), [assembly](dynamic-assembly.md), [multiphase](multiphase-equilibrium.md), [diagrams](equilibrium-diagrams.md) and [checklist](engine-checklist.md).

## 2. Phase map

| Phase | Priority | Result sought | Dependencies |
|---|---|---|---|
| F0 | Immediate | Reproducible base, start-up and reliable contracts | Current base |
| **F1A** | **Integrated** | **Original UNIFAC: state, energy, dynamics and diagrams** | Minimum F0 and audit U0 |
| **F1B** | **Integrated** | **UNIFAC Dortmund as a package of its own** | Interface and tests of F1A |
| F1C | Integrated with partial coverage | A UNIFAC set specific to LLE, if useful and verifiable | Audit of groups, parameters and evidence |
| F2 | High | Extensible catalog and consistent properties, with controlled overrides | F1A; the documentary part can advance in parallel |
| F3 | High | Equation assembly and steady-state/inverse/DAE solution | Current base; adapters from F1/F2 |
| F4 | High for real equipment | Open boundaries, non-condensable gases and calculated flows | F2 and the numerical closure F3 needs |
| F5 | High for reactors | Kinetic reaction and chemical equilibrium with consistent energy | Formation references and F2 data; F1/F3 |
| F6 | Next | Momentum and a hydraulic network coupled to matter/energy | F3; transport and state properties |
| F7 | Next | Transfer out of equilibrium and a coupled transient 1D model | F1/F2/F3; F6 when the flow is calculated |
| F8 | By branches | Mixture EOS, solids, critical regions and advanced diagrams | Stable thermodynamic base and data per branch |
| F9 | Cross-cutting | Performance, reproducibility, uncertainty and reliable operation | Required from F0; extended with each phase |
| F10 | After validating `/motor` | Formal comparison and convergence with `/laboratorio` | Common cases and foundations already closed |

F8 is not a monolithic block: a need for gases at high pressure brings its EOS branch forward; a crystallization brings its solid branch forward. The change of priority is documented without mixing it into the UNIFAC assignment.

No weeks or end dates are set without the data and performance audit. Each milestone closes on evidence and on working, not on a date or on a chart obtained.
