**F0 change-of-reference probe (Claude, 29 October 2026; read-only, no production change).** Reproducer: `python .tmp/claude-f0-reference/probe.py` (about 1 min). It runs `case.py` in two separate processes: with the package reference (pure ideal gas at 298.15 K and 1e5 Pa, h = s = 0 per species) and with every pure-species `h_ig` and `s_ig0` in `core/mezclas_datos.py` shifted by formation-like constants a_i (−2.858e5, −3.273e5 and −2.776e5 J/mol for water, 1-butanol and ethanol) and b_i (69.95, −225.8 and 160.7 J/(mol K)). Every pure-species h, s and g in the mixture layer derives from these two functions, so the whole reference moves consistently.
- **Cases.** TP and PH (at the TP enthalpy in each reference) for ethanol–water VLE (original UNIFAC, 355 K), water–1-butanol LLE (Dortmund UNIFAC and NRTL-LLE, 300 K) and ethanol–water with the ideal package (360 K), all at 101325 Pa; two closed heating differences; and the F0-5 network transient (closed isobaric water–1-butanol, Dortmund UNIFAC, Q = 100 W, 60 s, 11 samples, through `resolver_v2`).
- **Invariant, 138 checks, 0 failures.** T is identical in TP and returns to within 1e-12 K in PH; phase sets, compositions and phase amounts agree within 4e-14; the stability margin `tm_nueva` within 2e-14; H and U shift by Σ n_i a_i, S by Σ n_i b_i and G by Σ n_i (a_i − T b_i) within 2e-9 J; the heating differences agree within 5e-11 J. The network transient keeps `resuelto`, certification `cumple` and the same three events (LL appearance 12.401 s, disappearance 33.999 s, vapour 50.434 s); event times agree within 1.4e-7 s, T(t) within 1.9e-7 K and U(t), H(t) shift by the constant Σ n_i a_i.
- **Observation, not a defect.** The residual differences in the transient come from RK45 error control: the energy state U enters `rtol` through |U|, so the reference moves the local tolerance. They are far inside the integrator tolerance, as in the energy-tolerance observation of audit part 2. No regression test was added because no defect was found.
- **Not covered.** The IAPWS-95 water package and ice have their own fixed reference and were not shifted; chemical reactions are outside the published scope, so formation references in reaction energy remain an F5 item.

**F0 audit — capability, parameters and initialization (Claude, 22 October 2026; read-only, no production change).** Reproducers in the ignored `.tmp/claude-f0-audit/`, run from the repository root: `probe_static.py` (the published `/motor/termodinamica/capacidades`, `/motor/catalogo` and assembly v2 capabilities, dumped in the same folder), `probe_dynamic.py` (TP through the public `evaluar` contract for each mixture package and published species, at an interior temperature and at temperatures inside the package domain but outside that species' P_sat or ideal-gas Cp data) and `probe_initialization.py` (every revision-2 assembly example under `docs/ejemplos/`).

- **Consistent.** The eight published models appear in the catalog. Each published species list equals the catalog's admitted species, and the TP/PH/UP/UV specifications agree. The assembly lists every model except `agua-iapws95-hielo-ih`, whose dynamic exclusion it states. All 29 initialized volumes of the 22 assembly examples start in a combination that their package publishes (one phase, liquid–vapor or liquid–liquid).
- **No silent extrapolation.** Each of the 62 dynamic probes outside a species' data domain returned the structured `MOTOR_ESTADO_FUERA_DE_DOMINIO`, naming the effective system domain: the intersection of the present species' P_sat and Cp ranges with T < 0.9 Tc. All 39 interior controls were evaluated.
- **Finding F0-1, medium: a published capability contradicts the available parameters.** The Checklist and the catalog state that UNIFAC-LLE admits seven species at 10–40 °C, and the package publishes T ∈ [283.15, 313.15] K. Ethyl acetate's ideal-gas Cp data start at 298 K (its P_sat at 289 K), so every system containing it is rejected below 298 K. Its usable range in this package is 25–40 °C. The catalog admission rule checks only missing per-species requirements, not domain coverage.
- **Finding F0-2, low: package temperature domains are envelopes, not system domains.** For example, `mezcla-ideal`, `nrtl-chemsep` and both public UNIFAC packages publish [159.05, 582.615] K: the lowest species P_sat bound to the highest 0.9 Tc. Pure water is usable only from 275 K and acetone only up to 457 K. A client cannot pre-check a system from the capability; it learns the effective domain from a rejection. Nothing invalid is certified.
- **Repaired, 22 October 2026, at the user's request.** The catalog (revision 1, additive) now publishes `paquetes[].especies[].dominio` for every admitted species. It is computed as the runtime domain of that species alone. It also publishes `paquetes[].dominio` as the declared envelope with its pressure range; the generator asserts that the envelope equals the state route's published domain. The UNIFAC-LLE Checklist line now states ethyl acetate's 25–40 °C range. The runtime was not changed. No Cp data were extended: no sourced correlation below 298 K is in the registry.
  - **Regression test:** `python tests/test_catalog_species_domains.py` (5 tests, about 2 s). It recomputes every range independently from the catalog's own pure data and source-written table bounds, checks the envelope against the state route, checks ethyl acetate in UNIFAC-LLE at [298, 313.15] K, accepts pure species 0.01 K inside each edge and rejects them 0.01 K outside, and limits a water–ethyl acetate mixture to the intersection. **Negative control:** against the previous catalog module, 53 subtests error because no range is published.
  - **Examples and verification:** only `docs/ejemplos/motor-catalogo/motor-catalogo.json` was regenerated. The generator also rewrote four diagram and assembly examples with timing-only or rounding-level differences that come from uncommitted `core/mezclas.py` changes; those four were restored. Passing after the repair: the new test; `node tests/verificar_contrato_catalogo.mjs`; `validar_motor_catalogo` 126; engine thermodynamics 585; diagrams 78; assembly v2 114; `validar_api`; frontend `test:catalogo-propiedades` 142, `test:catalogo-red` 183, `test:entrega-n` 693, `test:motor-fundamental` and `verificar_physical_journals` (Checklist snapshot regenerated); TypeScript with 0 errors. The backend on 8004 serves the previous catalog until an authorized restart. F0-1 and F0-2 are closed; the other F0 items remain.
- **Not covered by this part.** Event limits; NRTL pair coverage and UNIFAC interaction gaps, which are diagnosed per set at evaluation; the pressure domain.

**F0 audit part 2 — residual scales, traces and zeros (Claude, 23 October 2026; read-only).** Reproducer: `python .tmp/claude-f0-audit/probe_traces.py`. It covers original UNIFAC for ethanol–water, water–1-butanol and benzene–water–ethanol at 101325 Pa, with each species in turn at 1e-6, 1e-9, 1e-12, 1e-16, 1e-30, 1e-100, 1e-300 and exactly 0, at two or three temperatures, in TP and in PH at the TP enthalpy: 256 cases. The checks are computed from the published phases, not from the engine's residuals.
- **Consistent.** In all 246 published states, each species' own reconstruction |Σ_a n^a x_i^a − n_i| / n_i is at most 1.3e-16, including 1e-300 traces. The engine scales its `reconstruccion_material` residual by the total amount, but with this result the per-species scale hides nothing. Exact zeros stay exactly zero in every phase, and every composition is finite, non-negative and normalized. The chemical-potential residual compares μ_i/RT, which already uses a logarithmic scale per species. The energy tolerance 1e-10 · max(|H|, RT n) depends on the enthalpy reference through |H|. With the common ideal-gas reference this changes it by a factor of about 20 at most, under 1e-7 K in temperature: an observation, not a defect.
- **Finding F0-3, medium: uncontrolled exception in the ternary liquid stability test.** Benzene–water–ethanol with a water trace from 1e-9 to 1e-300 (1:1 benzene:ethanol), PH at the 345 K TP enthalpy, raises `FloatingPointError: divide by zero encountered in log` out of `equilibrio_PH`; TP at the same state succeeds. Cause: in `prueba_estabilidad` for three species, Nelder-Mead moves unbounded logits. Past about 745, `_softmax3` underflows to an exact zero, and `mu` (under `errstate(divide='raise')`) fails inside the objective, where no handler converts it into a rejected trial. The binary path is bounded to |u| ≤ 690 since the F9-R2 fix. Nothing invalid is published, but callers receive an exception instead of a structured result. 6 of 256 cases.
- **Finding F0-4, low: false rejection with a 1e-300 trace in a liquid–liquid system.** Benzene–water with a 1e-300 ethanol trace is rejected at 300 K and 345 K, in TP and PH, as not certified (tm −0.833 and −0.407), although the same feed with traces from 1e-6 to 1e-100, and with an exact zero, splits and certifies. This is a false rejection, not a false certificate, of the same class as the near-pure F9-R2 plateau defect.
- **Repaired, 23 October 2026, at the user's request, in `core/mezclas.py`, without relaxing any tolerance.**
  - **F0-3.** In the three-species liquid stability search, a Nelder-Mead trial whose composition underflows to an exact zero is now rejected (infinite objective) instead of being evaluated. The bounded binary search behaves the same way.
  - **F0-4.** In `_pulir`, a seed that cannot be decoded is now rebuilt instead of abandoned. Such a seed has a trace at the minimizer's bound (exactly 0, or a subnormal 1e-14 n for n ≈ 1e-300), whose phase share underflows or whose log/exp round trip, with relative error ~|ln n| ε ≈ 1e-13, makes the dependent phase negative. Species with a phase share below 1e-6 restart with the same composition in every phase, and Newton solves their partition. This applies only when the original seed fails, so paths that already converged are unchanged. The second pass found the same defect at the isobaric search's lower bracket edge (278.68 K), where the trace came in as an exact zero; it is covered by the same rule.
  - **Regression test:** `python tests/test_trace_phase_search.py` (3 tests, about 2 min). Independent references are the trace-free state (binary path), the infinite-dilution partition coefficient (equal at 1e-100 and 1e-300) and PH at a TP enthalpy returning that TP state. **Negative control:** against the pre-fix module, 5 subtests fail with exactly the reproduced errors. Passing after the repair: the new test; invariant polish 5; near-pure isobaric flash 6; phase discovery 9; catalog domains 5; thermodynamic derivation 11 and events 6; thermodynamics; thermodynamic layer 179; water 161; engine thermodynamics 585; diagrams 78; catalog 126; assembly 245; assembly v2 114; n-components 82; consolidation v2 128; vertical slice 71; balances 30; H3 3,729; UNIFAC layer 82; engine UNIFAC 136. The 256-case trace matrix now has 0 uncontrolled exceptions, 0 rejections and every per-species reconstruction within 1.3e-16. F0-3 and F0-4 are closed. The F0 item on stability and event limits remains.

**F0 audit part 3 — stability and event limits (Claude, 23 October 2026; read-only, no production change).** Reproducers in `.tmp/claude-f0-audit/`, run from the repository root: `probe_stability.py` (about 4 h 20 min), `probe_events.py <z_water> <z_butanol> [t0 t1]` and `probe_event_steps.py`.

- **Declared limits.** Phase stability: a split is attempted when tm < −`TM_INTENTO` (1e-14) and certified to `TOL_TM` (1e-9). A phase counts as absent below `N_AUSENTE` (1e-12) n, and at most `MAX_FASES` (3) phases are allowed. A trial phase within 1e-5 of a present phase is treated as that phase (`_candidatas_nuevas`). Searches: binary, a logit grid ±34 in steps of 0.05, refined to |u| ≤ 690; ternary, a 60-division lattice with Nelder-Mead from its 12 lowest points; N > 3, Michelsen successive substitution from the pure vertices, the ideal estimate and Sobol points (seed 12345), then L-BFGS-B polishing. These are finite multistart searches, not proofs of the global minimum. Network events (`core/ensamblaje_red.py`, `integrar`): the phase signature is checked at the quarter points of each accepted RK45 step. The boundary is bisected to `RES_EVENTO` (1e-12) tf, the largest step is tf/(samples − 1), and the integrator stops below h_min = 1e-10 tf.
- **Stability search: no defect found.** For a single-liquid reference at each feed, the minimum from `prueba_estabilidad` was compared with an independent dense search of the same tm function. The binary search used 400 001 logit points in [−40, 40], each grid minimum refined. The ternary search used a 400-division lattice, Nelder-Mead from its 40 lowest points, and also `_estabilidad_general`. The 468 states cover water–1-butanol (UNIFAC original and original-LLE), ethanol–water (negative control), benzene–water–ethanol and water–1-butanol–ethanol at 300 K and 1 atm; 159 are unstable. There are 0 missed instabilities. The largest excess of the package minimum over the dense minimum on an unstable state is 2.3e-15; for the general method on the ternaries it is 1.0e-14. The probe does not cover plait-point neighbourhoods closer than its 1/14 feed lattice.
- **Finding F0-5, medium: a transient phase window shorter than about a quarter of an integrator step is missed, and the trajectory is still certified.** Case: a closed isobaric water–1-butanol volume (Dortmund UNIFAC, 101325 Pa, Q = 100 W, T0 = 300 K, tf = 60 s). The water-rich solubility of 1-butanol has a minimum of 0.027865 near 329.6 K, so the feed crosses a closed liquid–liquid window on heating: L → LL → L → LV. The independent reference is a PH flash scan along H(t) = H0 + Q t. With z_butanol = 0.027866 (window 22.30–23.85 s), every run from 601 down to 3 samples publishes LL appearance at 22.2992 s and disappearance at 23.8504 s. With 2 samples both events are absent. With z_butanol = 0.027865 (window 22.76–23.39 s), 61 samples find the window and 11 or fewer miss it. Every run is certified `cumple` and publishes only the later vapour appearance at 50.419 s. Cause, from instrumented steps: with constant Q the liquid-state ODE is almost linear, so RK45 reaches the cap tf/(samples − 1). The windows fall inside the accepted steps [12, 24] s and [18, 24] s, and none of their four check points lands inside. The published samples lie outside the window, so no published state is wrong; the event record and the phase history are incomplete. A second, not reproduced, weakness: when a change is seen only at an interior check point, the bisection still brackets [t_a, t_b] with a predicate that is false again at t_b, so it can lose that window too. Wider windows (10 s and 21 s) are located at the same instants for every sample count from 601 to 2. Repair options for the user to decide: tie the step cap to a phase-stability margin (tm or a phase-amount trend), or bracket the bisection between t_a and the first interior check point that changed, plus a regression on this case.

- **Repair of F0-5 (Claude, 23 October 2026; user decision: both options).**
  - **Interior bracket.** The event bisection now brackets [t_a, first check point whose phase set changed] instead of [t_a, t_b], so a closed window seen at an interior check point is not lost when the phase set has returned to the original one by t_b.
  - **Stability-margin step cap.** Each flash state publishes the appearance margin `estabilidad.tm_nueva`: the smallest tm of a trial phase that is not present. For binaries this includes the grid minima that the search leaves unrefined (tm ≥ 1e-4), so a liquid candidate stays visible far from its boundary. The trial list, the certification and the published trial counts do not change. `integrar` tracks this margin and the smallest phase fraction per volume. An accepted step whose margin above `MARGEN_MIN` (1e-8) falls below `MARGEN_CAIDA` (0.5) times itself between consecutive checks is redone at a quarter of its length, down to `H_MARGEN_MIN` (1e-8 tf). After each accepted step the running solver's `max_step` is set so that the linear trend of each falling margin keeps that ratio. Declared resolution: a window whose margin dips by less than about 1e-8 can still be missed.
  - **Regression test:** `python tests/test_transient_phase_window.py` (2 tests, about 2 min 15 s). The independent reference is the window boundaries along H0 + Q t, located with PH flashes and bisection to 1e-4 s. The 0.64 s window (z_butanol = 0.027865) is published with 11 and with 2 samples, within 1e-3 s of the reference. The 21.6 s window is unchanged. **Negative control:** against the pre-repair modules both narrow subtests fail with only the vapour event published (`.tmp/claude-f0-audit/run_f0_5_test_on_old.py`). Also checked with 2 samples: 0.46 s and 0.22 s windows (z_butanol = 0.0278649 and 0.02786482), at the same event times as with 601 samples.
  - **Passing after the repair:** the new test; trace phase search 3; invariant polish 5; near-pure isobaric flash 6; phase discovery 9; catalog domains 5; thermodynamic derivation 11 and events 6; thermodynamics; thermodynamic layer 179; water 161; engine thermodynamics 585; diagrams 78; catalog 126; assembly 245; assembly v2 114; n-components 82; consolidation v2 128; vertical slice 71; balances 30; H3 3,729; UNIFAC layer 82; engine UNIFAC 136. The layer validators rewrote two `docs/ejemplos/modelo-comun/` examples with 1e-9-level differences. These come from earlier uncommitted `core/mezclas.py` hunks: the same output is regenerated with the pre-repair module. Both were restored. F0-5 is closed.

**F0 verdict-separation audit (Codex, 27 October 2026; read-only; proposal awaiting user decision).** This advances the unchecked separation item in Solve and Results; it does not claim general rank proofs, reopen M1, or implement any adapter/MD-2 change. Existing internal fixtures are used without publishing a new model or inserting an alternative physical equation. Reproduce from the repository root with `python .tmp/codex-f0-verdict-audit/probe.py`, then `node .tmp/codex-f0-verdict-audit/probe_ui.mjs`. The adjacent `evidence.json` retains each mutated request, actual endpoint response and result; `ui-evidence.json` records the live TypeScript consumer outputs. The Python probe invokes endpoint functions directly (including their HTTP status/detail), not a running server. The frontend probe uses the actual endpoint rejection as a mocked HTTP response; it does not claim a browser end-to-end run. Both probes pass their assertions of the current defects and controls; they are audit reproducers, not repairs. All 1,391 physical-journal checks and scoped Checklist ESLint pass; the canonical Checklist snapshot is updated and the board validates. The F0 claim is released with the audit in review. No production code, existing tests, frozen archives or thermodynamic source was changed.

| Finding / boundary | Exact reproducer and observed behavior | Consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F0-6: strict reading gates structural inspection on initialization presence | From `test_common_admission.model(horizon='1')`, remove `volumenes[0].inicial.energia`. `/modelo/validar`, `/formulation-summary` and `/compilation` all return 422 with `ruta=volumenes[0].inicial`, `mensaje=faltan energia` (`core/modelo/lector.py:292`; shared `_modelo` in `backend/api_modelo.py`). | This is correct rejection of the current strict runnable-document schema, not proof that the balance structure is incomplete. There is no independent structural verdict for this pending-initialization draft; the proposal must preserve strict reading while adding staged assessment. |
| F0-7: compilation success has inconsistent initialization meaning | In the same two-volume network, set `datos['volumen.A.T0'].declaracion.texto='1000'`. Reading and formulation summary succeed; `compilar` constructs six physical state slots; `/compilation` calls `system.inicial()` (`core/compilador/report.py:72`) and rejects with `initialization_fuera_de_dominio`, `initializationStatus=incompatible`. For the existing `explicito-agotamiento.json` model, remove the initial condition: compilation and Solve return generic `no_soportado`, empty diagnostics. Remove the rate equation instead: the same code/empty diagnostics represent a genuinely missing equation. With initial mass -1 kg and the existing nonnegative domain, explicit compilation succeeds but Solve rejects the initial state (`core/compilador/explicito.py:269`). | Initialization failure hides equilibrium structure metadata, while an explicit compilation report does not establish compatible initialization. Neither a report nor generic `no_soportado` is a reliable stage verdict. |
| F0-8: contradictory explicit initial conditions silently overwrite each other (high correctness finding) | In the existing depletion fixture, retain m(0)=m0=2 kg and append a separately identified condition m(0)=1.5 m0=3 kg. Both readers and compilation accept it; Solve returns `resuelto` starting at 3 kg. Reverse the two conditions and the same request starts at 2 kg. Assignment to `self.g[index]` at `core/compilador/explicito.py:152` keeps only the last condition. | A structural ODE and populated initial-state vector are being treated as sufficient for execution without checking compatibility of all declared initial constraints. This is an invalid initialization accepted as numerical success. It is **not** a false physical certificate: this explicit result has no `physicalCertification` and has empty `verificaciones`. Proposed repair must check every condition and retain their identities, including redundant compatible conditions. No repair is implemented here. |
| F0-9: Solve transport erases the available stage diagnosis | Feed the actual 1000 K rejection above to `requestCompilation` and `transporteHTTP` in `frontend/motores/modelo/servicioModelo.ts:21,41`. Both throw a plain `Error` with only `stack` and `message`; `initialization_fuera_de_dominio`, the path, native code and initialization status disappear. `CompilationPanel` retains only a message and offers Retry compilation; `DerivedSolve` likewise stores only text. | The UI cannot reliably distinguish structural rejection, unsupported scope and incompatible initialization, or carry a typed rejected attempt to Results. Numerical/initialization failure should not erase a valid structural assessment. |

**What is already separate, and what Results still lacks.** The same two-volume common request initializes to exactly the same state vector in three controls: no boundary temperature, 1000 K boundary temperature, and 100 K boundary temperature. All finish numerically as `resuelto`; certification is respectively `no_evaluable`, `cumple`, and `no_cumple`. Python and TypeScript readers preserve these verdicts, and the existing certification-label helper displays them independently. No evidence was found here of a certificate granted solely because initialization succeeded. `analizarHoja` also keeps structural DOF at zero for the ordinary filling fixture with missing, negative and valid initial mass; it counts the missing initial separately and the Solve guard blocks negative initial mass. These are controls, not defects. The remaining gap is a common typed assessment carried across every path: non-equilibrium Results has optional numerical checks but no explicit certification-unevaluable verdict; rejected attempts never reach Results as typed assessments. Network Solve still uses its native route, while `/models/.../solve` mounts common compilation inspection above it; this audit does not assert that the common v2 adapter is already the product execution route. No parity or MD-2 review finding is inferred from this F0 evidence.

**Proposed contract (not implemented).** Introduce an opt-in `rage.model-assessment` v1 envelope with three required discriminated verdicts. Each verdict has `kind`, `status`, stable `code`, source signature, scope, and located `diagnostics` with existing raw/native codes retained. An unchecked or stale stage is never synthesized as a pass. Structure means closure of the admitted equations and required constitutive/boundary bindings, not a proof of numerical rank or a thermodynamic certificate. Initialization means compatibility of all initial constraints at t=0, including package/domain/phase policy; a successful initial flash certifies only that initial state. Certification describes actual published numerical and physical evidence, its declared aspects and time/sample scope; a partial run may pass within its published prefix without being complete.

| Typed verdict | Proposed stable status/code pairs |
| --- | --- |
| `kind: structure` | `complete / STRUCTURE_COMPLETE`; `incomplete / STRUCTURE_MISSING_RELATION`; `incompatible / STRUCTURE_CONFLICT`; `unsupported / STRUCTURE_UNSUPPORTED`; `not_evaluated / STRUCTURE_NOT_EVALUATED`; malformed syntax/references use `invalid / STRUCTURE_INVALID_DOCUMENT`, without claiming a physical count. |
| `kind: initialization` | `compatible / INITIALIZATION_COMPATIBLE`; `pending / INITIALIZATION_MISSING_DATA`; `incompatible / INITIALIZATION_CONSTRAINT_CONFLICT` or `INITIALIZATION_OUT_OF_DOMAIN`; `unsupported / INITIALIZATION_UNSUPPORTED`; `not_evaluated / INITIALIZATION_NOT_EVALUATED`. Per-owner diagnostics retain every conflicting condition ID; saturation ambiguity remains pending, not an unsupported equation structure. |
| `kind: certification` | `passed / CERTIFICATION_PASSED`; `failed / CERTIFICATION_FAILED`; `not_evaluable / CERTIFICATION_NOT_EVALUABLE`; `not_evaluated / CERTIFICATION_NOT_EVALUATED`. Aspect diagnostics distinguish unavailable physical models, missing boundary evidence and failed residuals; no tolerance is changed. Existing `physicalCertification.estado` is the authoritative physical verdict when present, and missing physical evidence must not become a pass because numerical checks passed. |

**Transport, persistence and UI proposal.** Preserve existing `formato`, `version`, `estado`, `codigo`, `diagnostics`, `recuento`, `inicializacion`, `physicalCertification`, `equilibrium` and `verificaciones` keys and enum meanings. `estado` continues to mean numerical execution. Add a versioned assessment endpoint/envelope instead of inserting unknown fields into strict `rage.compilation` v1; it can return a partial structural report plus initialization diagnostics. Keep `/modelo/validar` strict: assess a draft through a separate explicitly typed request, with required initialization declarations treated as pending by that assessment only, never by injecting defaults or a fake initial state into the runnable model. Use optional versioned `procedencia.assessment` for new results and `detail.assessment` for new rejections, retaining the old fields. Update readers before enabling these additions; mismatched signatures/evidence must reject, and older stored records display “Not evaluated · previous record” rather than a fabricated pass. Extend the existing result/attempt storage additively so a rejected attempt can be inspected in Results without inventing a solved trajectory or overwriting an earlier saved run. All common-to-UI result adapters must preserve the assessment; the native network adapter is outside the current implementation authorization and awaits its independent review.

Solve should display Structure, Initialization and Certification independently, alongside the existing numerical run status; disable execution on missing/incompatible required initialization without changing a complete structural verdict. Results should retain the three assessments, raw codes, source identity, residual evidence, failed/unevaluable aspects and actual prefix scope. Certification before a run is `not_evaluated`, not `passed`. Physical graph/closure edits invalidate all three assessments; initial-value edits invalidate initialization and certification; data edits affecting equation scope/dimensions also invalidate structure; solver settings invalidate execution/certification and any time-domain assessment that depends on them. Conservative invalidation is preferable where dependency tracking is not yet explicit. Never reuse an assessment merely because the model label or ID matches.

**User decision (27 October 2026): approved in three ordered steps, stopping with evidence after each.** (1) Reject contradictory explicit initial constraints with structured initialization diagnostics and a regression that fails before the fix. (2) Implement the three versioned verdicts and optional provenance while preserving every persisted key and the numerical meaning of `estado`; make structural inspection independent of missing/incompatible initialization. (3) Preserve typed diagnoses in the frontend compilation/Solve clients and show them in the existing Model/Solve/Results layout. No thermodynamic change is presently required. The F0 separation checkbox stays open until all three steps are delivered and reviewed; other unchecked F0 work remains open. Claude-assigned v2/R7 repairs take priority when the independent verdict appears on the board.

**F0 step 1 delivery (Codex, 27 October 2026; independent review pending).** `core/compilador/explicito.py` retains every declared explicit initial condition for each state and checks repeated constraints after the existing SI parameter binding. Conflicting evaluated values now raise `AdmissionError` with stable code `INITIALIZATION_CONSTRAINT_CONFLICT`, `stage=initialization`, `initializationStatus=incompatible`, the state ID, sorted condition/equation IDs, SI values and unit, and `path=matematico.condiciones`. This prevents last-row-wins initialization and is independent of declaration order. A repeated constraint that cannot be evaluated to a finite value gives `INITIALIZATION_OUT_OF_DOMAIN` without placing non-finite numbers in the diagnostic. Existing common compilation and Solve endpoint handlers retain these diagnostics under HTTP 422 and their unchanged legacy `codigo=no_soportado`; no new envelope or frontend behavior is introduced in this step.

The comparison uses exact equality of evaluated SI floating-point values, not the integrator's tolerances: even adjacent representable values are rejected as contradictory. This is a conservative compatibility check, not a symbolic equality proof; algebraically equal expressions that round differently may be rejected. Identical evaluated values such as 2 kg and 2000 g remain accepted, with a deterministic representative. Single-condition execution retains its existing path, including the separate initial-domain check; general structural/initialization separation and the equilibrium 1000 K / explicit negative-mass consistency issue belong to approved step 2.

**Regression evidence.** `python tests/test_explicit_initialization.py` exercises all six permutations of three initial declarations (2 kg, 3 kg, 2000 g), RK45/BDF, direct compiler construction, compilation reports and Solve; both common endpoint handlers preserve structured rejection. The three-row case prevents a first/last-only comparison from missing a contradictory middle row. The compatible redundancy control is checked against independent mass accounting m(t)=2-t/3600 kg for the existing 1 kg/h depletion fixture. A one-ULP difference must reject, and division-by-zero/overflow in a repeated condition must produce finite structured diagnostics. Before the repair, the first four tests produced 41 failures because the contradictory requests were accepted; compatible redundancy already passed. After the repair, all five tests pass. The existing explicit compiler validator passes 27 checks with its archive write suppressed, and all nine transformed-execution tests pass. All 26 derived-execution tests pass (501.504 s), including frozen A/B/C and the unchanged MD-2 regressions; A/B/C hashes and 43/31/25 operation counts remain unchanged. All 1,391 physical-journal checks, scoped Checklist ESLint and the board check pass. The claim is released with F0 step 1 in review for Claude. Approved steps 2-3 are deliberately not started in this turn; continue with the typed assessment envelope only after this required step boundary. The v2/R7 verdict was still pending on the board at release, so no assigned repair was available to apply. No solver tolerance, frozen archive, MD-2 source/test, v2 adapter/matrix/test or thermodynamic source was changed. The new test only changes numerical initialization constraints of an existing internal fixture; it introduces no new physical law or product example.

**F0 step 1 independent review (Claude, 27 October 2026): accepted.** Own probe through common `resolver` (the fixture builder of the test file is reused only to build inputs): m(0) = 2 kg with m(0) = 3 kg rejects with `INITIALIZATION_CONSTRAINT_CONFLICT` in both orders, carrying `conditionIds`, `equationIds`, `valuesSI`, `unitSI`, `variableId`, `stage` and `initializationStatus`; 2 kg with 2.000001 kg also rejects. Compatible controls 2 kg / 2000 g (both orders) and 2.5 kg / 2500000 mg solve with m(t) = m0 - t/3600 kg exactly (maximum absolute error 0.0). Negative control: the current tree with only `core/compilador/explicito.py` restored from HEAD (clean worktree, removed afterwards) solves both contradictory orders as `resuelto` starting at the last declared row (3 kg, then 2 kg; error 1 kg against the first condition), accepts 2.000001 kg, and fails 4 of the 5 new tests. `tests/test_explicit_initialization.py` passes 5/5 on the current tree. Scope note: exact SI floating-point equality is fail-closed; algebraically equal expressions that round differently are rejected, as documented, not silently merged.

**F0 step 2 delivery (Codex, 28 October 2026; review requested).** The new versioned `rage.model-assessment` v1 endpoint carries distinct typed structure, initialization and certification verdicts, stable stage codes, located raw/native diagnostics, model signatures and actual publication scope. Common Solve can opt into `procedencia.assessment` or `detail.assessment` without changing any existing persisted key or numerical `estado`; plain requests keep their result shape. The strict runnable reader is unchanged. A separate inspection reader admits absent initial declarations/references as pending without inserting any values; malformed supplied objects still reject. Explicit and generated-balance inspection reuses the same rate lowering without initial constraints or diagnostic budgets; equilibrium inspection reuses admission with only initialization disabled, plus recorded energy lowering. Public compilation now checks the explicit initial finite/domain constraints, so negative mass and equilibrium T0=1000 K both reject as initialization failures without hiding valid structure. The accepted step-1 conflict checker remains intact.

Certification remains tied to the published physical evidence, never to successful compilation or numerical `resuelto`. The three same-initial-state network controls retain `not_evaluable`, `passed` and `failed`; explicit results without physical evidence stay `not_evaluable`, or `failed` if a published numerical check fails. A partial result describes only its published prefix. Python readers reject stale model signatures, changed result samples, mismatched stage signatures/status/code pairs and substituted certification. Signatures are correlation checks, not authenticated proofs. The contract and optional transport are documented in the existing common-model and results-format contracts.

**Scope and remaining boundary.** Independent structure inspection is delivered for explicit ODEs, generated balances and admitted equilibrium networks. Derived-graph and hydraulic constructors still combine lowering and initialization: their independent structural stage is explicitly `not_evaluated` until successful full compilation supplies complete/compatible verdicts. No fake initial state is used to bypass that limitation. Existing compilation reports keep their v1 schema; internal equilibrium constructor allocation is not a successful public compilation report. General rank/DAE proofs remain outside scope. Frontend typed clients, existing Model/Solve/Results display and rejected-attempt persistence remain approved step 3 and are deliberately not started before this requested stop.

**Evidence.** `tests/test_model_assessment.py`: 18 passing tests, including every backend audit reproducer, generated/scaled controls, located initial-unit/nonfinite failures, stale/forged evidence, partial scope and isolated ASGI HTTP transport. The negative-mass compilation regression failed before the change (`NoSoportado not raised`) and passes now. Existing suites pass: explicit initialization 5, transformed execution 9, derived execution 26 (506.539 s; frozen A/B/C and MD-2 unchanged), common admission 12, v2 initialization 6, common network Results 9, generated balances 30 checks, explicit compiler 27, Results reader 34 and vertical slice 71. Validator archive writes were suppressed or disabled; no frozen document was regenerated. Claude's assigned v2 comparison repairs were applied first, as recorded in the compatibility contract; all 25 stored trajectories pass the revised comparisons, which is a replay of solver evidence, not a new final-source 25-request solve. All 1,391 physical-journal checks and scoped Checklist ESLint pass; the canonical Checklist snapshot and board are updated. Review both the stated inspection scope and backend assessment before proceeding to frontend step 3. No commit, push, route switch or deletion.

**F0 step 3 frontend delivery (Codex, 28 October 2026; independent review requested).** Common compilation uses the existing assessment endpoint and preserves the complete typed structure/initialization/certification envelope; common Solve opts into `assessmentVersion: 1`. `ModelDiagnosticError` retains HTTP status, the original detail, legacy/native codes, located diagnostics and any typed assessment rather than reducing the rejection to a message. Native network compilation keeps its existing `/compilation` route and preserves its raw typed diagnostics; the v2 adapter, parity matrix and backend envelope/tests are unchanged while Claude reviews them.

The TypeScript reader enforces the existing version, stage/status/code pairs, model and stage identities, finite evidence, structural inspection shape and optional compilation report. Results verifies the declared certification against the authoritative published physical certificate or failed numerical checks, with the actual time/sample/completion scope; numerical `estado` keeps its existing meaning. Source/result digests are retained and checked for format and stage consistency; the browser does not recompute Python JSON digests or claim authenticated evidence. The server remains responsible for its digest validation. Current request identity, cancellation and the existing scientific model signature prevent an older response/attempt from appearing as current after edits. Older records without assessments explicitly display Not evaluated; no physical pass is inferred.

ModelWorksheet still owns the same left mathematical development and right assumptions layout. Inline assessment text is added inside existing content, without a page, panel, layout or stylesheet. Model asks for structure with `initialize: false`; common-backed drafts bypass only the executable frontend reader for this inspection and retain missing initialization without defaults. Solve displays the typed compilation or run rejection and its paths. Explicit/generated, transformed, derived and hydraulic common-result adapters retain the complete original common publication in optional `commonResult`, so Results and storage retain the assessment and physical evidence rather than a message-only projection. Native network execution and its existing certification display remain unchanged; it has no fabricated common assessment.

**F0 steps 2-3 independent review (Claude, 28 October 2026): accepted.** Own probe through `model_assessment`: missing initial energy gives structure `complete/STRUCTURE_COMPLETE` with initialization `pending/INITIALIZATION_MISSING_DATA`; equilibrium T0 = 1000 K and explicit m(0) = -1 kg give `complete` with `incompatible/INITIALIZATION_OUT_OF_DOMAIN`; m(0) = 3 kg with m(0) = 2 kg gives `incompatible/INITIALIZATION_CONSTRAINT_CONFLICT`; 2 kg with 2000 g is `compatible`; removing the rate equation gives `incomplete/STRUCTURE_MISSING_RELATION` with initialization `not_evaluated`, distinct from a missing initial condition (`complete` + `pending`). Certification stays `not_evaluated` for every rejected or uninitialized attempt. Explicit and network Solve without `assessmentVersion` return the same top-level keys, `procedencia` keys, series and numerical `estado` as the opt-in call minus `procedencia.assessment`, and contain no assessment. Frontend (headless, no screenshots): the backend assessments for all four audit reproducers pass the TypeScript reader unchanged and render in `AssessmentText` SSR with exactly the backend status/code pairs and diagnostic codes/paths; a typed Solve rejection keeps `INITIALIZATION_CONSTRAINT_CONFLICT` and the assessment object. The client only checks shapes and digest formats; no physical quantity or digest is computed. `ModelWorksheet` changes by one inline `ModelAssessmentText` inside the existing left equations section, with no new panel, page or stylesheet. Negative control in a clean HEAD worktree (removed afterwards): no assessment endpoint, missing energy returns only a 422 without a structural verdict, and m(0) = 3 kg then 2 kg solves as `resuelto` from 2 kg. Evidence on the current tree: `test_model_assessment.py` 18 plus `test_explicit_initialization.py` 5 pass; `verificar_model_assessment.mjs`, 1,391 physical-journal checks and TypeScript pass. Derived-graph/hydraulic independent inspection remains explicitly `not_evaluated` before full compilation, as declared.

Compilation and Solve rejections use a separate versioned `rage.model-attempt` record in the existing IndexedDB result store, keyed by model ID and scientific signature. The attempt retains operation, time, HTTP detail and assessment, is readable from the existing Results view even without a trajectory, and never overwrites a previous successful run. Aborted requests are not recorded; edited models cannot reuse an old attempt. Results labels a rejected attempt separately from the saved run and its numerical status. Persisted model/protocol keys are not renamed; new result/attempt fields are additive.

**Headless evidence:** `node frontend/verificar_model_assessment.mjs` consumes live Python assessments/results, verifies the common and legacy compilation/Solve transport, all three physical-certification controls, 12 malformed-envelope rejections, real inline React SSR and isolated result/attempt storage with stale/cancel guards. An in-memory HEAD-client negative control loses the same response's diagnostics and assessment; the new clients retain them. `verificar_physical_journals.mjs`: 1,391 checks; `verificar_equation_workbench.mjs`: readers, operation handoff, invalidation and editable topology pass with 11,655 rendered formulas; `verificar_derived_lifecycle.mjs`: 78 checks; `verificar_common_certification.mjs`: two live results and six corruptions pass. TypeScript and scoped ESLint pass. No screenshot or browser automation was used. Steps 2 and 3 remain in review; independent derived/hydraulic draft inspection retains the backend's explicit not-evaluated limitation. Claude's v2/step-2 verdicts were still pending at delivery, so no protected repair was inferred. Roadmap, Checklist and board are updated in place; no commit, push or deletion.





## F1. UNIFAC first

### F1A. Complete original UNIFAC

- [x] U0: audit the current variants, data, groups, derivatives and couplings.
- [x] U1: adapter for excess free energy and activities, with a reproducible identity.
- [x] U2: generic stability and TP split, with verified groups and parameters.
- [x] U3: consistent caloric properties and identifiable PH/UP/UV/TV inversions.
- [x] U4: dynamics and networks with conserved inventories and energy.
- [x] U5: routes, capabilities, fingerprints and diagrams of the same package.
- [x] U8: reproducible physics delivery and contracts.
- [x] Codex integration: selection, evidence, diagnostics, readers, examples and visual review.

**Output:** choosing original UNIFAC in a sheet changes equilibrium, energy, trajectory and diagram consistently. It is not limited to calculating gamma in an isolated query.

### F1B. Dortmund

- [x] U6: subgroup set, formulation and parameter table explicit and distinct from the original.
- [x] Repeat the same criteria of consistency, stability, energy and dynamics.
- [x] Publish a comparison by case, without presenting one variant as universally better.

**Output:** the user chooses the variant; changing the variant invalidates its earlier calculations and caches. There is no automatic substitution.

### F1C. Parameters dedicated to LLE

- [x] U7: audit the coverage and chemical equivalence of the groups of the existing LLE set.
- [x] Integrate only if there are enough data and evidence, with a separate package identity.
- [x] Keep a single Gibbs model in each LLV calculation; never solve LV with one table and LL with another within the same state.

**Closure with limited scope:** LLE table integrated for its six admitted species, with methanol blocked for lack of the CH3OH subgroup. It is not approximated with other groups. Methanol support and more data remain pending; universal LLE coverage is not declared.

Detailed assignment: [UNIFAC contract](../history/frontend/contract-claude-unifac-engine.md).

## F2. Catalog and properties

Sequence of work within this phase, without opening separate assignments for each step:

| Milestone | Claude: physics delivery / contract | Codex: integration | State |
|---|---|---|---|
| F2.1 | Audit and an extensible substance registry; separate identity, pure-component data and coverage per operation | Read the published catalog; keep IDs and symbolic components; do not keep a parallel chemical list | Integrated: eight published substances; acetone discovered without a local chemical list |
| F2.2 | Properties and correlations with unit, source, reference, domain and fingerprint | Show declared and actually used data separately, with their sources; do not infer admission from having groups | Integrated for Psat, ideal Cp and liquid volume of the mixture packages; it does not replace evaluated properties or the IAPWS formulation |
| F2.3 | Adding species when data suffice, with structured rejections | Selection from real capabilities and navigation to the missing datum; do not raise limits locally | Integrated per package/version, with real acetone and F9-R3 fixed |
| F2.4 | Consistent overrides per species/property and their effect on the potential, or a manual closure | Explicit editing, keeping the original and the choice; invalidate results and cache when the physical datum changes | Integrated: liquid volume, ideal Cp and Psat; source required, originals kept, restoration and expiry checked |
| F2.5 | Compatible references between packages and an audit of formation data | Show execution provenance; do not convert references or correct energies in the client | Integrated: streams with the same reference; IAPWS ↔ mixture keeps the material rejection and accepts thermal contact. Formation data pending, for F5 |
| F2.6 | Examples and regression of catalog, energy, phases and compatibility | Strict reading, units, persistence, service and visual path; update this record and the checklist | Integrated within the cross-checked scope; eight substances in the catalog do not mean eight components per volume or a dynamic cross-check of every NRTL set |

### Running F2 for Claude

**Continuity after closing F2:** F2.1–F2.6 are delivered and integrated within the limits of [the cumulative delivery](property-catalog.md). Acetone is already in the real registry; its simulated addition belonged to the first delivery. Do not repeat F2 or open another assignment for what is finished. The criteria below are kept as an acceptance reference; the next work is ordered by F3 and its dependencies.

The sequence applied was F2.1 → F2.2/F2.3 → F2.4/F2.5 → F2.6; the audit of references conditioned the substitutions and connections accepted. Another EOS, reaction, momentum and new solvers still belong to their own phases.

**F2.1 — audit and centralize.** Review the fixed registry `_ESPECIES` and the pure potentials of `core/mezclas_datos.py`, their use in `core/mezclas.py`, `core/paquetes_volumen.py` and the capabilities of the `backend/motor_*` services. Keep a single substance identity and references by ID from volumes/streams. Separate the pure-component data registry from UNIFAC assignments, NRTL parameters and package capabilities. A registered substance is not automatically admitted in every operation. Symbolic components keep working in balances that do not need real properties.

**F2.2/F2.3 — publish executable coverage.** Audit per package/operation which data and derivatives the calculation needs, where they come from and in which domain they are valid. Publish the identity, name, unit, source, reference and fingerprint of the data used; record what is missing per substance/property/operation. Do not demand from IAPWS the fields of an incompressible-liquid correlation, and do not force every model to use groups. Do not load mutable data from a shared provider during each flash, and do not fill gaps with zeros or substitutes. Respect the explicit choice of method when there are alternatives.

The canonical contract must allow discovering and selecting the substances actually available and their operations, without a parallel list of seven names in the frontend. Claude defines compatible types, routes and semantics in `docs/contratos/`; existing ones are reused where they suffice. Codex does not guess those fields. Include the pure limit and the mixtures in the same admission rule to close the F9-R3 discrepancy as part of this audit; do not hide it with a client filter. Do not remove component/phase/volume limits as a substitute for a numerical cross-check.

**F2.4 — overrides limited to the physics that supports them.** Distinguish inputs of the model/correlation from properties derived from the state. For each accepted override, keep the original, the chosen value or correlation, unit, source, reference and domain. Show how it modifies the same potential and its derivatives; if that cannot be done with that package, return a rejection with a reason or require an explicit manual closure. Do not overwrite h, Cp, density or chemical potential of an independently certified state. Changing a physical datum changes its fingerprint and expires results/cache; it does not globally mutate other sheets or packages.

**F2.5 — references and conservation.** Audit molar mass and energy/entropy references between connectable packages, including IAPWS against the mixtures' pure-component data. An internal stream carries the same amount and energy with opposite signs; it is not recalculated with two incompatible references on each side. Formulate an explicit conversion when valid, or reject the connection. Do not resolve a discrepancy by modifying inventories/flows or silently adjusting a molar mass. Audit the availability of formation data as preparation for F5, without bringing reaction into F2.

**F2.6 acceptance:**

- Add one more substance by registering complete, traceable data, without a new branch in the flash/assembler. If there are not enough data, deliver what is missing; do not invent parameters to complete an example.
- Cross-check properties and derivatives against independent references within the domain; check the identities of the potential and the pure limits. Comparisons between implementations with the same parameters do not replace experimental evidence.
- Test consistent admission and rejection in point state, inversion, dynamics and diagrams, according to what each operation publishes. Do not announce an operation because another one can use the substance.
- Test zeros/traces, component order, missing parameters/groups, equivalent units, concurrency and registry immutability. Keep the cross-checked sizes separate from the admitted maximum.
- Test compatible and incompatible overrides, references between packages and the invalidation of old results after changing identity/data. Keep the distinctions component concentration / mixture density / phase density.
- Deliver original examples and negative controls, a comparison with the F1 base and the regression tests actually run. A changed value needs a physical or numerical explanation; a green suite is not enough.

The data matrix, physical decisions and results go into the cumulative F2 delivery under `docs/`; their links and state are added here. Do not create another priority or assignment document. The canonical types and example folders are artefacts of the delivery, not alternative plans.

Inspecting an existing field does not close the corresponding physics milestone. The molar mass used is not deduced from the name or filled in on editing; it comes from the package's evaluation. Endpoints and catalog types are not anticipated until Claude publishes the contract. Keep the following ten acceptance requirements:

**Codex advance · F2.2, 16/10/2026:** in `Detalles técnicos → Referencias de certificación` the following are shown per volume and ID: the declared CAS/provider, the CAS used, the declared molar mass with its unit/source and the molar mass actually used in kg/mol with its source. They are read from the saved request and first sample, without querying the current catalog, filling in data or replacing the model. [Code](../../frontend/app/motor/PaquetesEjecutadosRed.tsx), [visual evidence](../../frontend/qa_integracion_unifac_2026_10_16/procedencia-componentes.png). Targeted tests: 44 checks of selection, identity, cache and provenance, including a different component order and a run without evaluation data; TypeScript and ESLint clean. This prepares the traceability of the future catalog; it does not implement new species, overrides or properties.

Regression of this advance: `npm run test:motor-fundamental`, exit code 0; log `.tmp/continuidad-f2-20261016.log`. 45 local links of the continuity documentation were also checked. Claude's backend was not modified or restarted; its physics battery was not rerun for this presentation change.

**Codex advance · F2.3/F2.6, 16/10/2026:** the state query keeps the backend's structured rejection (code, species, owner and path) instead of replacing it with a generic local catalog-membership error. An `evaluado` response must still satisfy the catalog and all the reader's checks. The explicit choice of model/version and the local validation of incomplete data are kept.

The client's timeout for evaluating a state goes from 20 to 120 s; capabilities keep 20 s. Real test against the existing service: UV of seven original-UNIFAC components in 24.03 s, T = 351 K and P ≈ 101325 Pa, from U/V rebuilt from an original TP state. It is an inversion/transport test, not an independent cross-check of the EOS. `Cancelar espera` (cancel the wait) was added to the query, reusing the operations control: an old response or completion cannot publish over the next one. It does not announce cancelling the calculation in Python.

`Estado → Propiedades del paquete` allows substituting v_L, ideal Cp and Psat per species, keeping the rest of the package. The substitutions are sent in the point query and `paquetes[].datosPuros`; the executed sample carries to the diagram only those of its components, together with the real fingerprint. - [x] Define references between connected packages: the same reference accepted; IAPWS ↔ mixture keeps an explicit material rejection.
- [x] Audit the availability of formation data before bringing in reaction; adding and cross-checking per species stay in F5.

**Output:** adding a substance does not require adding special cases to the assembler. A missing datum identifies species, property and path. Data changes alter the physical fingerprint.

## F3. Equation engine and solution methods

**Generic template reuse validation (4 November 2026; review pending).** Independent replay and compiled-plan comparisons pass 30 combined tests in 480.82 s (template instances, preparation cache, indexed lowering, strict compilation and HTTP). A subsequent exact cold-replay receipt test found stale rendered predicate names; regenerating receipt conditions from the rebound typed predicates repairs it (1 test, 87.50 s). Generated steady scale identities and magnitude metadata now match the independent Transform exactly. Existing Transform-owned data trigger ordinary preparation rather than guessing collision/identity semantics. Mutable closures, seeds, caches and runtime wrappers are allocated per instance; only admitted symbolic content and pure AST kernels are reused. The request-local uncached policy remains the reference. Global network incidence, Jacobian assembly and numerical regularity remain active.

The isolated benchmark separates document replay, lowering and numerical comparison. Both paths use identical deterministic instance documents and verify rates/checks and initial RHS to 1e-13. No source deduction is re-recorded. Times are wall seconds on the local Windows host; the cold reference disables preparation caches.

| Instances | Instance-document replay/binding | Generic plan + binding | Independent cold lowering | Reference comparison |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 5 | 21.232 | 8.756 | 74.845 | 0.202 |
| 10 | 29.649 | 13.861 | 157.855 | 0.349 |
| 20 | 58.534 | 25.998 | 316.817 | 0.691 |
| 40 | 111.380 | 56.945 | 984.295 | 2.371 |

The empirical log-log lowering exponent is **0.901** for generic reuse versus **1.216** for independent cold lowering. At 40 instances the 17.28 ratio covers lowering alone. Including the same instance-document replay/binding on both paths, the preparation gain is 3.2x at n=5 and 6.5x at n=40 (excluding the separately measured reference comparison). These measurements cover the recorded drum template, not every possible authoring operation. End-to-end drum regression after generic reuse passes all 10 cases in 9326.64 s (n=1,2,3,5 dynamic and steady; 11 warnings from rejected diagnostic trials). CSTR acceptance is tracked separately; no new sparse crossover is inferred. Source files are core/modelo/template_replay.py, template_instance.py, preparation_cache.py; core/compilador/template_lowering.py, indexed_network.py, indexed_steady.py; tests/test_template_lowering.py and benchmark_template_lowering.py.

**Generic template binding checkpoint (Codex, 4 November 2026; part C under validation).** The indexed runtime now has a candidate generic explicit-plan binder: verify the signed source recipe, alpha-rename its replay and operation signatures through the deterministic identity map, then reuse the admitted symbolic rates and numeric AST while allocating independent closure contexts. Zero-accumulation defaults are specialized once per generic template and rebound per instance. The uncached preparation policy retains independent replay/lowering. A cold-reference test caught unmapped equation identities in generated steady scale keys and magnitude metadata; both are repaired, and the steady default/report comparison passes (1 test, 48.82 s). Source recipes, data and assumptions still participate in exact replay keys; imported provenance cannot populate the internal cache. Global network Jacobian and numerical regularity checks remain; no coupled-rank inference is made from local proofs. Full regression and final timings are pending, so this checkpoint does not close part C.

The preliminary n=5,10,20,40 experiment measured generic lowering/binding at 6.363, 8.589, 15.838 and 29.386 s, against 76.819, 155.447, 300.011 and 654.633 s for independent cold lowering **including reference comparisons**. Replay/binding of the instance documents cost 20.266, 26.698, 67.983 and 101.815 s separately. The respective empirical log-log exponents were 0.751 and 1.022. These unequal timing scopes are diagnostic only, not the final speedup claim: the benchmark now separates validation from cold lowering and must be rerun after the receipt consistency repair. The CSTR and its train remain outstanding.

**Symbolic-preparation investigation (Codex, 3 November 2026; in progress, not accepted).** Primary workspace: Solve, including preparation of the zero-accumulation Transform. Inputs are the unchanged signed journals, selected equations and SI data; outputs remain the same lowered equations, guards, certificates and common results. `tests/profile_symbolic_chains.py` profiles the shared-authored chain before any cache changes. The baseline below includes cProfile overhead and is not directly comparable to the uninstrumented solve table below. Phase costs overlap; they must not be added across nested calls.

| Volumes | Dynamic preparation (s) | Steady Transform (s) | Steady preparation (s) | Dynamic solve/publication (s) | Steady solve/publication (s) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 5 | 11.926 | 15.852 | 7.140 | 0.174 | 0.030 |
| 10 | 24.237 | 48.033 | 21.228 | 0.422 | 0.088 |
| 20 | 76.303 | 142.009 | 62.899 | 1.365 | 0.310 |
| 40 | 260.309 | 494.831 | 216.248 | 5.673 | 1.595 |

At n=40, dynamic preparation spends 185.147 s cumulatively in graph build (including 157.047 s in journal replay), 46.413 s in substitution, only 0.140 s in AST lowering and 7.425 s configuring the Jacobian. Steady preparation spends 187.386 s in build (159.028 s replay), 2.378 s in substitution and 20.655 s in regularity/proof auditing. Steady Transform rebuilds both systems: 372.825 s build (316.267 s replay), 65.090 s substitution, 0.125 s AST lowering, 20.452 s regularity auditing and 7.455 s Jacobian configuration. During final solves/publication, substitution costs 5.266 s dynamic and 1.382 s steady; Newton factor/regularity costs 0.004 s. The log-log least-squares exponent for the sum of the three preparation phases is **1.598** over n=5..40. This is an empirical instrumented-host fit, not an asymptotic proof.

The candidate cache retains an explicit request-local uncached reference (`preparation_policy(cached=False)`). It caches exact document/recipe/identity/revision replay results with independent mutable copies, immutable quantity identities, and alpha-normalized typed numerical AST kernels with exact per-instance symbol/index/constant bindings. Failed or unsigned replay is not cached. Five initial tests pass, including exact full dynamic/steady publications on a freshly shared-authored two-volume case and forty distinct AST bindings. This is **not yet** a verified generic-stage symbolic replay cache: first-build journal replay still runs per volume. Global regularity checks remain because block regularity alone does not prove coupled-system regularity. The later measurements, regression result and indexed publication delivery are recorded below. Generic-stage symbolic reuse and a reproducible sparse crossover remain open; do not infer their completion from this checkpoint.

**Preparation candidate measurements and policy revision (3 November 2026).** Exact-context domain queries remove repeated SymPy assumption solving without weakening predicates; all global coupled-system regularity checks remain. The cache includes complete global assumptions for both proof queries and replay. The first mathematical run caught an architectural dependency from the pure kernel into `core.modelo`; moving the request-local policy into `core/mathematics/preparation.py` repaired it. The combined mathematical-domain/kernel/cache/publication suite then passed **73 tests in 41.20 s**. A broad numerical run passed 115 but failed 5 with 16 setup errors: an unintended UTF-8 conversion changed punctuation in physical-assumption text and therefore correctly invalidated signed journals. The original bytes were restored; the repaired affected run passes **44 tests with 2 expected failures in 163.30 s**, and the final focused cache/policy run passes **10 tests in 17.72 s**. No signature was waived or re-signed. The clean combined run passes **134 tests with 2 expected failures in 669.93 s** across cache, sparse, steady, nonlinear, affine, index-one DAE, inverse, hydraulic, derived execution, package closure and indexed preparation suites. The final cache-context suite passes **10 tests in 18.72 s**. TypeScript passes; the physical-journal audit passes **1476 checks over 45 fixtures**. Whitespace validation and the project board pass. No commit, browser, server or protected physical-source edit was made.

| Volumes | Dynamic preparation before / candidate (s) | Steady Transform before / candidate (s) | Steady preparation before / candidate (s) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 5 | 11.926 / 8.978 | 15.852 / 6.913 | 7.140 / 1.097 |
| 10 | 24.237 / 14.461 | 48.033 / 19.797 | 21.228 / 2.792 |
| 20 | 76.303 / 41.933 | 142.009 / 57.148 | 62.899 / 8.310 |
| 40 | 260.309 / 134.756 | 494.831 / 191.831 | 216.248 / 30.789 |

Same cProfile phase protocol, local Windows host. The candidate preparation-sum log-log exponent is **1.472**, versus baseline **1.598**. At n=40 replay in dynamic preparation falls from 157.047 to 29.831 s, graph build including replay from 185.147 to 58.881 s; substitution still costs 45.426 s and Jacobian configuration 7.530 s. AST lowering is only 0.317 s. Consequently a numerical AST cache alone cannot close the symbolic bottleneck. These measurements preceded restoration of the punctuation bytes; the subsequent clean regression run passes. They establish the measured cache candidate, not completed generic-stage lowering. The required generic symbolic stage cache is still outstanding; no once-per-stage lowering claim is made.

**Preparation files and remaining implementation:** `core/modelo/{preparation_cache,equation_workbench}.py`, `core/mathematics/{preparation,dimensions,domains,validity}.py`, `core/compilador/{lowering_cache,explicito}.py`, `tests/{profile_symbolic_chains,test_preparation_cache}.py`. The model layer consumes the pure mathematical policy; the mathematical kernel has no reverse dependency on models or physical producers. The remaining generic-stage work must cache a validated typed symbolic block using the recorded template recipe signature, bind each deterministic identity map, and preserve equation/condition signatures and located failures. The existing alpha-normalized numerical AST cache does not replace that work. Do not bypass physical replay or infer a coupling-rank proof from independent blocks. Step 2 files are `core/solvers/sparse_blocks.py` and `tests/test_sparse_blocks.py`; policy v2 changes only the recorded automatic path, not persisted model keys or available explicit overrides.

**Sparse remeasurement after candidate preparation** (three-solve medians, seconds; solve plus publication only):

| Volumes | BDF dense | BDF sparse | Newton dense | Newton sparse |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 10 | 0.141078 | 0.144613 | 0.023910 | 0.024832 |
| 20 | 0.441445 | 0.441865 | 0.112168 | 0.118066 |
| 40 | 1.841057 | 1.781584 | 0.618449 | 0.609206 |

Dense/sparse scaled dynamic disagreement is at most 9.84e-16; steady disagreement at most 1.64e-11. Default seeds converge without continuation. The first measured win is n=40, only 3.2% BDF and 1.5% Newton; the preceding measurement had sparse Newton slower at n=40. Therefore **no reproducible crossover is established**, and policy v2 replaces the unsupported size-16 automatic threshold with automatic dense. Explicit sparse remains selectable and recorded; no failure fallback is added. A repeatable crossover and generic-stage preparation remain open acceptance work.

**Runnable-model sparse delivery (Codex, 3 November 2026; review pending).** The user explicitly separated sparse solving from the blocked drum transport. `core/solvers/sparse_blocks.py` assembles lowered symbolic incidence and one local output-Jacobian block per package volume, then multiplies the symbolic outer derivatives by those blocks. A ten-volume test performs 40 local central probes (two inputs per volume), rather than probing all volumes for every global column. Newton uses CSC `splu` with COLAMD ordering; the common integrator supplies the CSC Jacobian to SciPy BDF. The original policy v1 selected sparse at 16 or more coordinates; policy v2 above now retains automatic dense pending a reproducible crossover; Python verification overrides are explicit in compilation descriptions and there is no factorization-failure fallback. Newton preserves bounded backtracking, primary-seed semantics, competing-root probes, residual/step tests and local regularity gates. The sparse condition-infinity calculation accumulates inverse column norms through LU solves, and an inverse `LinearOperator` supplies the smallest-singular-value test without a dense inverse. These regularity checks still cost work proportional to the system size and do not promise column-scale asymptotic performance. Nested nonlinear/DAE/scaled dynamic and inverse-specification assembly retain their prior path and dense Jacobian access.

**Files in this numerical increment:** Step 1: `core/solvers/sparse_blocks.py`, `nonlinear.py`, `integrador.py`; `core/compilador/derived.py`, `steady.py`, `derived_closure.py`; `tests/test_sparse_blocks.py`, `sparse_chain_inputs.py`, `benchmark_sparse_chains.py`. Step 2: `core/compilador/indexed_network.py` and `tests/test_indexed_network_lowering.py`. Canonical maintenance: `docs/contracts/common-model.md`, `docs/engine/codex-queue.md`, this roadmap, `docs/engine/model-workspaces.md`, `docs/engine/project-board.json`, `frontend/app/checklist/datos.ts` and generated `docs/engine/engine-checklist.md`. These files also contain earlier uncommitted deliveries; no staging or commit was performed. Both claims were released after the board check passed.

The focused suite passes 15 tests in 155.82 s; later M1-A/B/C tolerance refinement passes 3 tests in 118.48 s, and the final policy/nonfinite-block/regularity-failure rerun passes 11 tests in 3.45 s. One package steady test compares both paths and validates closure certificates through the strict result reader. The headless steady Python/TS publication, tampering, storage and invalidation verifier passes. Physical journals pass 1476 checks across 45 fixtures. No thermodynamic, balance or reaction source is edited.

**Shared-author chain and F9-R8 protocol:** `python tests/benchmark_sparse_chains.py 5 10 20 40`. Closed thermal volumes use the existing physical journals, recorded first-law boundary substitutions and caloric reconstruction. The test recipe batches operations, replays them through the shared operation service, pins the actual calculated input signatures and replays again; it does not insert a terminal RHS. These are local host measurements, not a controlled cross-machine speed claim. All publications pass strict readers and their verification rows.

**Prepared indexed lowering (Step 2; physical prerequisite remains):** `core/compilador/indexed_network.py` reuses replayed DerivedSystem stages and the deterministic expansion. It scopes states, authored symbols and constants per stage; admits recorded outgoing component flows and specific enthalpy; substitutes donor expressions for the receiving package exterior-state bindings; assembles shared closure blocks; and uses the Step 1 Jacobian path. ### F3 preparation, 28 October 2026 — decision required

The following preparation inventory preceded the user's 28 October approval. Its proposed first slice is implemented below; the remaining F3 scope is still pending. F2 is complete on the board. M1 is complete only for its [fixed explicit-ODE suite](#m1--minimum-usable-multiphysics-engine); general residual/DAE and index reduction remain explicitly [outside M1](#scope-limits-evidence-and-immediate-order). The [M1 bridge contract](../contracts/common-model.md#7a-replayed-graph-to-explicit-ode-bridge-m1-23-october-2026) defines the existing execution boundary. No frozen document, operation count or hash changes. During this preparation Claude recorded acceptance of F0 steps 1–3 and the final v2 parity run on the board, with no repairs assigned here. Those implementations and tests were left unchanged. F0's remaining consolidation work is separate from this proposal; parity does not authorize switching routes or deleting v2.

**Existing scope and remaining work.** The evidence below is source/test inspection, not a fresh full-suite run. Existing tests identify executable coverage; their presence is not evidence for unimplemented F3 scope.

| F3 concern | What exists, with evidence | What remains |
|---|---|---|
| Typed equations and execution identity | `core/mathematics/{graph,session,dimensions,validity,domains}.py` retains typed expressions, units, guards and operation ancestry. `core/modelo/derived_execution.py` pins selected equation/quantity identities. `tests/test_derived_execution.py` checks exact Transform rows, stale-source rejection, frozen references and additional density–volume cases. | General assembly from arbitrary selected balance/constraint sets; generic equation-to-unknown matching and residual dependencies. Existing symbolic authoring and dimensional matrix rank do not establish numerical Jacobian rank. |
| Explicit versus implicit equations | `core/compilador/derived.py` expands reconstructions and isolates one linear first derivative per selected state (`unsupported_rate` for coupled derivative constraints). `explicito.py` accepts first-order explicit RHS trees; `transformed.py` supports the admitted multiplicative scaling. `tests/test_m1_u1_acceptance.py` compares RK45/BDF refinements with independent references. | Coupled mass matrices, general `F(t,y,dy/dt)=0`, nonlinear derivative branches and differential/algebraic partitions. BDF's implicit numerical time step does not implement an arbitrary implicit physical residual. |
| Algebraic loops and nonlinear closures | `derived.py` reconstructs oriented, uniquely isolated quantities; `_expand` rejects `cyclic_reconstruction`. `graph.py::solve_unique` supports a single admitted polynomial branch with retained guards. `compilador.py` solves supported thermodynamic UV/PH closures inside RHS evaluation; `report.py` explicitly describes this nesting. `core/equilibrio.py`, `core/balance_materia.py` and `core/adimensionalizacion/dinamica.py` also contain scoped nonlinear root/least-squares solvers. | A common generic coupled algebraic block selected from the replayed graph, with units, residual scaling, rank/singularity diagnostics, branch conditions and initialization. Existing package-specific closures and laboratory root finders are not this capability. Thermodynamic solvers remain Claude-owned and are reused unchanged. |
| Structural solvability and initialization | `core/modelo/dynamic_definition.py` distinguishes inventories, dependent totals, closure unknowns and fixed constraints; its report explicitly disclaims rank and feasibility proofs. `core/compilador/initialization.py`, `tests/test_explicit_initialization.py` and `tests/test_model_assessment.py` cover contradictory initial data, declaration order, missing data and separate structure/initialization verdicts. | General structural matching, numerical/structural rank distinctions and consistent DAE initial values/derivatives, including redundant or incompatible constraints. Derived/hydraulic structure remains `not_evaluated` before full compilation in the accepted F0 scope; do not reinterpret that status as complete. |
| Index reduction | Shared differentiation/substitution and typed interval/nonzero premises exist in `core/mathematics/`; they operate on selected equations. | DAE index analysis, justified constraint differentiation, hidden constraints, consistent derivatives and drift control. No general index-reduction execution path was found in the common compiler/solver. Manual symbolic differentiation is not index reduction. |
| Steady states and inverse problems | Material/energy journals can retain zero-accumulation equations. `core/adimensionalizacion/dinamica.py::estados_homogeneos` searches bounded homogeneous equilibria of its explicit system, and `jacobiano`/`estabilidad` analyze them; a sampled multiplicity heuristic is not a uniqueness proof. Common `admission.py` rejects stationary accumulation constraints with `ESTACIONARIO`; `report.py` requires a temporal study. | A common steady-state study using the same physical residual, declared zero accumulation, unknown/data selection and conserved totals; detection/reporting of nonunique or infeasible states. General inverse targets and constrained time integration remain pending. Do not infer steady-state support from an initially stationary trajectory or a local equilibrium flash. |
