Accepted F0 assessments transport distinct structure, initialization and certification codes; numerical `estado` is independent. | Residual-class-dependent method admission, generic algebraic/nonlinear convergence and singular-Jacobian diagnostics, DAE-consistent events/restarts, sparse assembly and solver statistics. There is no general automatic stiffness-based method selector or universal matrix inspector. Preserve structured stops and separate physical certification for every extension. |

**Recommended first slice: constant affine algebraic blocks coupled to explicit ODEs.** Admit `dy/dt=f(t,y,z)`, `0=A(p)z-b(t,y,p)` only when the selected square algebraic block is affine in `z`, its coefficients depend solely on declared constant parameters, and its nonsingularity is established in the declared regular regime. Solve the block simultaneously in shared Python mathematics, retain the exact elimination identities and conditions, then lower the reduced RHS to the existing common RK45/BDF path. This adds a narrow index-1 reduction capability; it does not introduce a general DAE integrator, nonlinear loop solver, state-dependent mass matrix or index reduction. A nonsingular block supplies unique `z` for a given differential state; it does not prove global validity of the full model. Never use a pseudoinverse, drop equations or replace the selected system with an earlier formulation.

A future implementation must validate row dimensions, preserve guards before cancellation, check all original algebraic residuals and initial declarations, and distinguish non-affine/unsupported, missing/duplicate constraints, singular blocks and inconsistent initialization through located diagnostics. Numerical conditioning and residual scales must be reported without treating a symbolic rank proof as a floating-point accuracy guarantee. Fixed differential inventories, implicit derivative rows, nonlinear blocks and arbitrary branch choices remain explicitly rejected. No new thermodynamic closure is needed.

**Acceptance case and independent reference (proposed, not yet an authored executable fixture).** Create a fresh internal two-body thermal model through the existing shared operations, with arbitrary region/quantity IDs. Start with component conservation and each body's first law; record closed material boundaries, rigid bodies, negligible kinetic/potential storage and mechanical work, single-phase uniform states, fixed positive masses, linear caloric premises and exhaustive equal/opposite internal heat exchange. The caloric and conductance relations are classified constitutive premises, not consequences of conservation. Derive constant masses and `dU1/dt=-q`, `dU2/dt=q` from those laws; derive the caloric inventory equations by substitution. No library entry and no changes to frozen M1-C are authorized.

Differentiate each caloric inventory, substitute its derived zero mass rate and first-law rate, then isolate the temperature rate through the shared operations. Retain component inventories with their zero rates and `T1,T2` as differential states; reconstruct `U1,U2` from the caloric inventories and verify the original first laws. This coordinate choice does not discard either conservation law. Define the mean temperature `theta=(T1+T2)/2` and temperature difference `delta=T1-T2` as mathematical definitions. Use recorded combination/isolation/substitution operations to obtain the two coupled selected rows:

- `T1=theta+delta/2`;
- `T2=theta-delta/2`.

Retain `theta,delta` as the simultaneous algebraic unknowns; derive `q=G*delta` by substituting the definitions into the declared heat law and use that heat rate in the derived temperature-rate rows. The block has constant dimensionless coefficients independently of the retained mass states. This deliberately reuses a familiar physical regime to isolate the generic assembly capability, rather than claiming new thermal physics. The selected algebraic rows must stay coupled: do not author final uncoupled reconstructions to make the existing compiler pass. Every selected row must retain its actual equation inputs, before/after algebra, dimensions and conditions. If replay cannot express a step, record that specific shared-operation gap and resolve it before claiming this case; do not insert terminal ODEs or substitute a special thermal evaluator.

Choose `m1=2 kg`, `c1=300 J/(kg K)`, `m2=3 kg`, `c2=300 J/(kg K)`, `G=6 W/K`, `Tref=300 K`, `T1(0)=360 K`, `T2(0)=300 K`, temperature validity 290–370 K and horizon 0–300 s. Independently, summing the first laws conserves 36,000 J; subtracting the temperature rates gives decay time `C1*C2/(G*(C1+C2))=60 s`. Thus, with `e=exp(-t/60 s)`, the hand-derived reference is `T1=324+36e K`, `T2=324-24e K`, `theta=324+6e K`, `delta=60e K`, and `q=360e W`. For this reference `C1=m1*c1=600 J/K` and `C2=m2*c2=900 J/K`. The algebraic residual block has coefficient matrix `[[-1,-1/2],[-1,1/2]]` for `theta,delta`, with determinant `-1`; both algebraic rows have temperature dimension. These reference formulas belong only to independent verification, never to the model history or production evaluator.

Proposed acceptance: 61 requested samples; both existing methods and a tenfold tolerance refinement; maximum temperature error divided by 60 K and energy-conservation error divided by 36,000 J each at most `1e-6`; original algebraic temperature residuals divided by 60 K at most `1e-10`. Declare solver tolerances before the run and report measured errors for both refinements. Check initial temperature data against reconstructed algebraic values. Perturb capacity/conductance with independently recomputed references; permute equation/unknown order, rename all IDs and change compatible units; remove a closure, duplicate a row to make the block singular, introduce a nonlinear row, contradict an algebraic initial value and invalidate a source through Undo. Every negative case must reject or stop structurally with its source path, never publish a fictitious valid result. Existing M1, MD-2, lifecycle and F0 suites remain regression boundaries.

Preparation evidence: an in-memory call to the current `DerivedSystem._expand` with the two mutually dependent temperature-coordinate reconstructions raises its existing cyclic-reconstruction error. This is a helper-level negative control, not a full model/HTTP regression. Independent reference arithmetic at 0, 30, 60, 120 and 300 s gives the block determinant `-1` and maximum energy residual `5.10e-11 J` from floating-point arithmetic. No future compiler implementation or full acceptance result is claimed.

**Workspace contract and prospective files.** Model owns the fundamental-law journals, typed definitions, constitutive premises and validity conditions. Transform selects original differential/algebraic equation IDs and exposes the elimination with provenance; it cannot store an unrelated RHS. Solve binds units/data, checks rank and compatible initial values, and selects an admitted existing time integrator. Results reconstructs the algebraic variables, publishes original residuals and conservation evidence, and keeps numerical completion distinct from physical certification. Physical/history/closure edits invalidate the formulation; data/unit/initial-value changes invalidate initialization and results; method/tolerance changes invalidate numerical results. Display aliases do not change scientific identity. No new page/panel, alternate Model layout or client-side mathematics is proposed.

The prospective implementation surface, to be re-read and claimed only after approval, is:

- `core/mathematics/linear_system.py` (new): reusable typed affine-block extraction/elimination with retained conditions; `core/compilador/derived.py`: selection admission, reduction, original residual checks, initial consistency and report provenance.
- `core/modelo/derived_execution.py`, `frontend/motores/modelo/derivedExecution.ts`: an explicitly versioned selection extension for coupled algebraic blocks, retaining version-1 readers and meanings; `frontend/app/models/DerivedExecution.tsx`, `frontend/app/models/derivedSelection.ts`: select existing rows/unknowns in the existing Transform controls and preserve invalidation.
- `core/compilador/presentation.py`, `frontend/motores/modelo/compilation.ts`, `frontend/app/models/CompilationPanel.tsx`: describe the algebraic elimination and original rows without claiming a DAE time integrator. `core/compilador/assessment.py`: integrate block diagnostics with the accepted stage-specific verdicts, without changing numerical `estado` or relabeling unsupported standalone structural inspection. This is prospective work only; no assessment files were edited during review.
- `tests/test_f3_affine_algebraic.py` (new): shared-operation authoring, independent reference, failing-before control and negative cases; `frontend/verificar_affine_algebraic.mjs` (new): browser-free versioned reader, selection and stale-result checks. Re-run existing suites rather than rewrite their frozen expectations.
- Canonical shared documentation/registries when delivering the capability: `docs/contracts/common-model.md`, `docs/engine/model-workspaces.md`, this F3 section, `docs/engine/project-board.json`, `frontend/app/checklist/datos.ts` and `docs/engine/engine-checklist.md`. No new report document. The present preparation updates only this roadmap and the board; the Checklist remains unchanged because nothing executable was delivered.

The common integrator, v2 compatibility adapter/parity tests, thermodynamic files, frozen reference documents and product library are outside this first slice. Additional file needs or an unexpressible authoring step require an updated narrow claim and scope explanation, not silent expansion. After this slice, separately scope nonlinear algebraic blocks and consistent index-1 initialization, coupled implicit/mass-matrix systems, higher-index reduction, and common steady/inverse studies. **Decision taken, 28 October:** the user approved this constant-affine-block slice with runtime RHS solves, structured rejections and independent RK45/BDF acceptance. No broader F3 implementation is implied.

### F3 first-slice delivery — 28 October 2026

**Scope delivered; independent review requested.** The generic compiler admits one selected square affine algebraic block alongside its existing differential states. Version 2 of `rage.derived-execution` adds `algebraic` equation/quantity pairs; version 1 keeps its previous semantics and cycle rejection. The matrix is extracted from the replayed equations, never supplied by the client or inferred from an equipment name. See [the implemented contract](../contracts/common-model.md#7a1-constant-affine-algebraic-blocks-f3-28-october-2026).

`core/mathematics/linear_system.py` requires an exact, provably nonzero SymPy determinant after SI conversion and constant parameter binding. Before numerical admission it equilibrates rows and columns and requires `cond_inf * binary64_epsilon <= 1e-8`. Nonrepresentable/lost coefficients are rejected. Each RHS evaluates the forcing with the shared AST evaluator and calls `numpy.linalg.solve`; original SI componentwise backward error must be at most 64 machine epsilons. The solve feeds the actual differential RHS. Exact symbolic reconstruction is used for equivalence, initial compatibility, guards and conservation bookkeeping, with no runtime fallback to the reduced RHS. The report exposes the actual differential expressions, original coupled rows, SI matrix, determinant, conditioning test and solution method.

Structured admission codes include `nonlinear_algebraic_block`, `singular_algebraic_block`, `nonconstant_algebraic_matrix` and `ill_conditioned_algebraic_block`, with the selected equation IDs and `motores.derivedExecution.algebraic` path. Runtime solve/residual failures use structured stops and the common integrator's accepted-prefix hook. General nonlinear algebraic systems, time/state-dependent matrices, algebraic derivatives, index reduction and steady studies remain unsupported. This increment admits identity formulations only; combining the block with scaling explicitly rejects. Time/state-dependent forcing remains allowed within the existing expression grammar. No tolerance of an existing solver/test was changed to obtain acceptance.

**Acceptance derivation.** `tests/test_f3_affine_algebraic.py` freshly authors two bodies from component conservation, each first law and declared linear caloric/heat-transfer premises through the same journal and typed operation APIs as Model. It adds mean/difference definitions, isolates/substitutes their actual equations to retain the coupled rows `T1=theta+delta/2` and `T2=theta-delta/2`, and substitutes them into the heat-transfer balances. The implementation retains the component masses and internal energies as differential inventories; temperatures use the already admitted caloric reconstruction. This is equivalent to the proposed temperature coordinates while preserving the existing physical-inventory and conservation path. The constant block matrix is `[[-1,-1/2],[-1,1/2]]`; determinant is `-1`, and the equilibrated infinity-norm condition number is `2`.

The model uses masses 2/3 kg, both specific capacities 300 J/(kg K), conductance 6 W/K and initial temperatures 360/300 K. For 61 samples over 300 s, the independent hand reference is `delta=60*exp(-t/60) K`, `theta=324+6*exp(-t/60) K` and `U1+U2=36000 J`. This reference is not the compiler's RHS or an authoring shortcut. The test patches the reduced `numeric.rhs` to fail if called and counts actual block solves. Frozen M1-A/B/C documents, hashes and 43/31/25 operation counts are unchanged; no product-library example was added.

| Method | Tolerance factor | Maximum difference error / 60 K | Maximum energy drift / 36,000 J |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| RK45 | 1 | 1.710e-10 | 4.043e-16 |
| RK45 | 0.1 | 2.476e-11 | 2.022e-16 |
| BDF | 1 | 3.850e-9 | 6.064e-16 |
| BDF | 0.1 | 7.153e-10 | 6.064e-16 |

All are below the user's `1e-5` scaled-error and `1e-6` conservation limits. The declared baseline is `rtol=1e-9` and state `atol=1e-8` in SI, each reduced tenfold for refinement. Algebraic backward-error checks also pass at published samples. Numerical completion remains distinct from physical certification; no thermodynamic certificate is invented for the assumed caloric model.

**Regression and lifecycle evidence.** The six thermal/adverse tests pass (359.650 s): the equivalent version-1 reconstruction cycle still rejects; full shared-operation models reject singular, nonlinear and time-dependent blocks; generic controls include matrix underflow; row/unknown permutation, compatible unit conversion, parameter perturbation with an independent reference, contradictory initial data and Undo/stale-source rejection pass. A separate accepted-prefix regression passes (0.004 s): an injected block failure at 0.5 s stops the common integrator with its retained prefix and matching `dy/dt=1` reference, while unclassified programming exceptions are not converted to success. The existing 26 derived tests pass (365.558 s), including A/B/C, optional existing scaling, MD-1 and MD-2; 27 mathematical-kernel tests pass. TypeScript, scoped ESLint, the 78-check derived lifecycle, workbench readers/handoff (11,655 rendered formulas) and 1,391 physical-journal/layout checks pass. A browser-free live-publication verifier passes versioned readers, eleven malformed payloads, v1 compatibility and selection/data invalidation. The same browser-free verifier also passes with a publication from the public Solve handler and assessmentVersion=1. All 18 existing F0 assessment tests pass (5.818 s) outside the sandbox: the sandbox run stalled before any request in Windows asyncio socketpair creation, confirmed by a bounded traceback probe. Only the verified stalled test PID was stopped; no server or other agent process was touched.

The existing Transform selector, Solve mathematical strategy and common Results series expose the capability. Model layout, the product library, thermodynamics, the common integrator implementation, v2 adapter/parity matrix and F0 assessment implementation remain unchanged. Source/data/selection edits use the existing invalidation machinery. The Checklist registry and canonical `engine-checklist.md` snapshot now describe this limited delivered scope and the remaining extensions. Claude: independently review the constant-matrix admission, runtime solve/no-fallback control, conservation and reference evidence, structured negative cases and v1 compatibility before accepting the slice. Broader F3 work requires a separate decision.

**Independent review (Claude, 28 October 2026): first slice accepted.** Read-only review of the working tree; probes are in `.tmp/claude-f3-review/` (`probe_block.py`, `probe_acceptance.py`). No source, test or Codex-owned file was changed.

- *Generic admission and solve* (`ConstantAffineBlock`, 20/20 probe checks): a 2×2 block (det −7) at three forcing vectors and a 3×3 block (det 56) match hand/exact solutions within a few ulps, with backward error ≤ 1.5e-16. A badly scaled but well-conditioned diagonal (1e12, 1e-9) is admitted with equilibrated cond 1. The threshold edge was bisected on `[[1,1],[1,1+e]]`: `e=8.8818e-8` (cond·eps just above 1e-8) rejects with `ill_conditioned_algebraic_block`; `e=8.9706e-8` (cond·eps 9.90e-9) is admitted. Exact singular blocks, including one singular only after parameter binding, reject with `singular_algebraic_block`; bilinear and quadratic blocks with `nonlinear_algebraic_block`; time-dependent, state-dependent and unbound-parameter matrices with `nonconstant_algebraic_matrix`; a nonsquare block with `invalid_algebraic_block`; NaN forcing at runtime with `algebraic_nonfinite`. Time/state-dependent forcing is admitted, as documented. A `1000 J` versus `J` coefficient binds to the SI matrix `[[1000,−1000],[1,1]]`; solved quantities are published with the SI unit derived from their dimension.
- *Acceptance case, rederived by hand:* `C1=600 J/K`, `C2=900 J/K`, `G=6 W/K` give `d(T1−T2)/dt=−G(1/C1+1/C2)(T1−T2)=−(T1−T2)/60 s`, hence `delta=60·exp(−t/60) K`; `C1T1+C2T2` is invariant, so `T_eq=324 K`, `theta=324+6·exp(−t/60) K` and, with the 300 K / 0 J/kg caloric reference, `U1+U2=600·60=36,000 J`. The compiled SI matrix is `[[−1,−0.5],[−1,0.5]]`, det −1, cond 2. Independent rerun with the reduced `numeric.rhs` instrumented (0 calls) and own error formulas: RK45 delta 1.709e-10, theta 1.709e-11, energy 4.04e-16; BDF delta 3.849e-9, theta 3.85e-10, energy 6.06e-16; mass drift 0; both `resuelto` with 61 samples to 300 s and both verifications passing. Frozen M1-A/B/C hashes are unchanged.
- *Codex's seven tests:* all pass on the working tree (321.5 s) with the reported figures. Negative control in a clean HEAD worktree (removed afterwards), overlaid with the current tree except HEAD's `core/compilador/derived.py` and `core/modelo/derived_execution.py`: all seven fail (the six execution tests at class setup because HEAD rejects the version-2 `algebraic` selection; the prefix test because `stop_from_exception` is absent). A plain HEAD checkout fails earlier at import for an unrelated uncommitted thermodynamics module, so it is not a meaningful control.
- *Non-blocking findings:* (1) Codex's suite tests only the underflow side of the conditioning gate; the 1e-8 edge is covered only by this review's probe. (2) The full-model rejection test covers singular, nonlinear and time-dependent blocks but not a state-dependent matrix; that case is covered here at block level only. (3) The `derived.py` diff also contains the MD-2 prescribed strict-validity-boundary change (`prescribed_boundary`, four tests in `tests/test_derived_execution.py`), accepted separately under matter deduction and not re-reviewed here.

### F3 second slice — nonlinear algebraic blocks

**Approved and implemented, 28 October 2026; independent review pending.** The user approved this generic dense damped-Newton block for `dy/dt=f(t,y,z,p)`, `g(t,y,z,p)=0`, nested inside common RK45/BDF, with scaled error <= `1e-5` and conservation <= `1e-6`. It advances F3 and the existing Solve & Results capability inventory after F2 and the independently accepted constant-affine slice. Frozen M1-A/B/C, their hashes and acceptance expectations remain unchanged. General residual DAE integration, index reduction, implicit derivative/mass-matrix systems, steady/inverse studies, spatial work and thermodynamic package development remain separate decisions. A local regular algebraic solve is not a general DAE solver.

**Ownership and review dependency.** Claude accepted `v2-route-switch` on the board during this implementation and assigned no repairs. Its routes, adapter, executionProvenance and tests were left unchanged, as were Claude-owned thermodynamics. The two affine-review coverage findings now have regressions: the conditioning threshold is tested clearly on both sides (the rounded published probe value was too close to the floating-point edge), and a freshly replayed full model with a state-dependent matrix rejects in version 2. No library entry or new report document was created. Detailed arithmetic and run logs are ignored under `.tmp/f3-nonlinear/`.

**Workspace contract.** Primary workspace: Solve. Model supplies replayed fundamental laws, constitutive premises, typed guards and units; Transform selects the exact differential and algebraic equation/quantity identities with the identity formulation. Solve supplies numerical guesses, scales, tolerances and the nonlinear method, initializes the selected constraints and evaluates that same block at every RHS and published sample. Results receives algebraic values, original residuals, conservation checks and structured convergence/termination evidence. Numerical success does not supply a thermodynamic certificate. Equation/history/condition changes invalidate formulation, initialization and runs; changing data, units, seeds, scales or tolerances invalidates numerical artifacts; display renaming does not. Keep all controls in the existing workspaces and all science in Python.

**Approved admission and method.**

- Introduce an explicit version-3 derived-execution selection for nonlinear blocks and their numerical settings. Versions 1 and 2 retain their current meanings and rejections, including v2's rejection of nonlinear and nonconstant matrices. Select one finite square block, distinct algebraic unknowns and replayed residual rows, explicit first-order differential rates, complete SI-compatible data and fully specified differential initial values. No derivative of an algebraic unknown, hidden constraint differentiation, conservation-row deletion or symbolic root substitution is admitted.
- Initially admit the shared arithmetic expression subset (sums, products, integer powers and guarded division) with an evaluable symbolic Jacobian in the algebraic unknowns. Time/state dependence is allowed within that subset; unsupported functions, unevaluated spatial operators, phase/complementarity switches and arbitrary nonsmooth residuals reject. Every selected condition must have supported runtime evaluation. Equation count and structural matching are necessary checks, not a numerical rank or existence proof.
- Require finite initial guesses for every algebraic unknown, declared admissible intervals and positive dimensioned variable/residual scales. Guesses are Solve inputs, never fixed physical initial constraints. Solve `g(t0,y0,z0)=0` while holding the prescribed differential state fixed, then verify all separately declared algebraic initial values/relations. Do not silently adjust an inventory or overwrite contradictory data. This is algebraic initialization for the admitted semi-explicit system, not general consistent initialization of `F(t,y,ydot)=0`.
- Compile residual and Jacobian from the same selected graph. Use a dense direct Newton correction with domain-preserving Armijo backtracking on the squared scaled residual norm. Trial points outside guards or with nonfinite values are rejected before acceptance; no clipping, pseudoinverse, least-squares replacement, equation dropping or cached-root success. Require both acceptable original scaled residual and a regular, sufficiently conditioned scaled Jacobian at the published root. A tiny step with a large residual is stagnation, not convergence. Initial proposed limits: 30 Newton iterations, 20 backtracks per iteration, Armijo coefficient `1e-4`, and `cond_inf(J_scaled)*eps <= 1e-8`; report them and never relax them silently.
- First implementation uses the same declared seed at each `(t,y)` evaluation, making rejected integrator stages and BDF finite-difference probes independent of call order. Do not add mutable warm starts in this slice. Each BDF probe must recompute the complete nonlinear closure. A supplied domain/seed selects a local numerical search; convergence and pointwise regularity do not prove global uniqueness or enumerate branches. Report that limitation. An ambiguous branch specification, detected distinct admissible roots or a requested global uniqueness guarantee without evidence blocks execution; never select a branch by equation order. The acceptance case below has an independent uniqueness proof. Generic global branch certification is deferred.
- Identity formulation only. Numerical row/variable scaling inside Newton is not a Transform nondimensionalization. Keep original SI residuals and the declared scales visible; success requires their norm below the requested tolerance. Retry/stop behavior uses the existing classified accepted-prefix hook. On final publication, retain only samples whose algebraic solve and guards pass, and report the failed location; never publish stale algebraic values or an earlier model.

The numerical design is consistent with the separation of scaled residual convergence, small-step stagnation and line search in [SUNDIALS KINSOL mathematical considerations](https://sundials.readthedocs.io/en/latest/kinsol/Mathematics_link.html). This is a method reference, not a proposed dependency or independent physical benchmark.

**Located diagnostics.** Keep admission, initialization, numerical termination and physical certification separate in the existing F0 assessment. Reject missing/duplicate/non-square rows, overlapping unknown ownership, unresolved data, dimension mismatch, unsupported derivatives/expressions/guards and stale signatures before execution. Distinguish singular/ill-conditioned Jacobian, nonfinite evaluation, invalid domain/seed, incompatible initial constraints, line-search failure, stagnation and iteration exhaustion. A failure to converge is not a proof that no root exists. Each diagnostic must identify the stage, selection path, equation/quantity IDs, model/formulation signature, time if applicable, iteration/evaluation counts, residual norm, step norm, conditioning estimate and termination reason. Runtime failures retain the last accepted certified numerical prefix; zero publishable samples are failed. Tests must distinguish these conditions rather than match one generic exception string.

**Internal acceptance derivation (authored through shared operations).** Use two arbitrary region IDs, each with closed component boundaries and its own local material conservation and first law. Through the existing shared journals and operations record fixed geometry, no material transfer/reaction, negligible kinetic/potential storage, no mechanical work and exhaustive equal/opposite heat transfer. Record single-phase uniform states and constant positive specific capacities as explicit assumed thermodynamics, with validity `290 <= T <= 370 K`; do not claim equilibrium-package certification. Derive component zero rates, retain material and internal-energy inventories, and derive `dU1/dt=-q`, `dU2/dt=q`. Summing component mass equations gives the dependent total; it is not another independent residual.

Declare caloric premises `Ui=mi*ci*(Ti-Tref)` with zero reference energy, and two constitutive contact relations `q=G1*(T1-theta)` and `q+a*q^3=G2*(theta-T2)`. Here `theta` is an algebraic interface temperature, not a third storage body: the ideal contact stores no energy, and the equal heat rate follows from the recorded equal/opposite transfer constraint. The cubic relation is a declared phenomenological monotone resistance, not a consequence of the first law or a new thermodynamic package. Its units are `G1,G2: W/K`, `a: W^-2`, `q: W`, `theta: K`. Positive `G1,G2` and nonnegative `a` give passive heat transfer. Retain both contact equations coupled in the selected algebraic block `z=(q,theta)`; do not pre-solve the cubic or inject the final temperature ODEs.

Obtain temperature reconstructions by recorded isolation of the caloric inventory equations. The selected differential rows retain `mi,Ui`; their rates use the algebraic heat rate. Every substitution, sign convention and isolation must retain input identities, actual before/after algebra, units and conditions. Reuse the shared authoring APIs, not the frozen M1 document. If any step or numerical lowering is missing, record the exact Engine finding and update the narrow scope before claiming execution; never append a terminal formula or fabricate its history. In particular, current affine lowering relies on exact symbolic reconstruction for guards, initialization and balance coverage: the nonlinear implementation must evaluate these against the retained block instead of pretending a symbolic inverse exists.

**Independent reference and fixed data.** Use `m1=2 kg`, `m2=3 kg`, `c1=c2=300 J/(kg K)`, `G1=G2=12 W/K`, `a=1/57600 W^-2`, `Tref=300 K`, `T1(0)=360 K`, `T2(0)=300 K`. Thus `C1=600 J/K`, `C2=900 J/K`, initial total energy is `36000 J`, and the equilibrium temperature is `324 K`. Take `q` in `[0,300] W`, `theta` in `[290,370] K`; a deliberately inexact seed is `(100 W,330 K)`. No initial algebraic constraint is silently inferred from that seed. The reference root is `(240 W,340 K)` at time zero.

Independently eliminating the contact temperature for verification gives `Delta=T1-T2=R*q+B*q^3`, with `R=1/G1+1/G2=1/6 K/W`, `B=a/G2=1/691200 K/W^3`, and `s=1/C1+1/C2=1/360 K/J`. Conservation gives `dDelta/dt=-s*q`, so, for `q>0`,

`t=(R/s)*ln(q0/q)+(3*B/(2*s))*(q0^2-q^2)`, where `q0=240 W`.

In SI numeric values this is `t=60*ln(240/q)+(57600-q^2)/1280` seconds. It is strictly decreasing in positive `q`; bracketed scalar bisection on `(0,q0]` supplies each reference sample without Newton, the production residual evaluator or either ODE integrator. Reconstruct `T1=324+0.6*Delta`, `T2=324-0.4*Delta`, `theta=T1-q/G1`, and the two caloric energies. Since `R+3*B*q^2>0`, the real contact root is unique for each temperature difference. For residuals `g1=q-G1*(T1-theta)`, `g2=q+a*q^3-G2*(theta-T2)`, the algebraic Jacobian determinant is `-G2-G1*(1+3*a*q^2)`, never zero for these parameters. These formulas belong only to verification; they must not become a compiler branch or an authoring shortcut.

Preparation arithmetic at 61 times from 0 to 300 s gives maximum energy roundoff `3.64e-11 J`, original contact residual `7.82e-13 W`, and implicit-time residual `1.14e-13 s`. At 300 s, `q=3.42289391969 W`, `T1=324.342324204 K`, `T2=323.771783864 K`, `theta=324.057083044 K`. This checks the reference, not future compiler accuracy or an executable derivation.

**Acceptance gate and verification.** Run RK45 and BDF on 61 samples over 300 s with `rtol=1e-8`, mass absolute tolerance `1e-10 kg`, energy absolute tolerance `1e-6 J`, then reduce these tenfold. Newton residual/step tolerances start at `1e-10`/`1e-12`, also refined tenfold; residual scales are `240 W` per contact row and variable scales are `240 W` and `60 K`. Require maximum temperature error/60 K and heat-rate error/240 W each <= `1e-5`, total-energy drift/36000 J <= `1e-6` (the user-approved acceptance budgets), and original algebraic scaled residual <= the requested Newton tolerance. Report both measured refinements; errors must decrease until the independently quantified reference/roundoff floor. Instrument actual nonlinear solves and fail if a closed-form/reduced-RHS fallback is used. Fresh graph publication through the common Solve handler, Results readers, F0 stage assessment and lifecycle invalidation must pass without route changes.

Repeat with renamed IDs, permuted rows/unknowns, equivalent units, altered positive capacities/conductances/nonnegative `a`, reversed initial heat direction with signed domains, and several admissible inexact seeds. The independent reference is recomputed from the same conservation derivation, with the `a=0` exponential limit as an additional control. A separate small dimensioned algebraic polynomial system must exercise the same solver without thermal metadata and compare against exact hand roots. Exercise missing/duplicate closure, unit errors, singular and ill-conditioned Jacobians, incompatible fixed algebraic initial data, invalid seed/domain, nonfinite trials, exhausted iteration/backtracking budgets, stagnation, branch ambiguity, source Undo/staleness and a late failure retaining a valid prefix. Repeat an identical `(t,y)` after rejected/out-of-order probes to verify deterministic evaluation. Existing affine v2 controls must still reject nonlinear/state-dependent matrices; add full-model coverage of the affine review findings within the new regression suite. Re-run frozen M1, derived execution, affine, F0 assessment, workbench and physical-journal/layout suites without changing their tolerances or reference expectations.

**Delivery evidence and review request.** Version-3 strict Python/TypeScript readers retain v1/v2 semantics. `NonlinearBlock` compiles the actual residual/Jacobian, keeps algebraic unknowns in the differential AST, checks original balances against the retained equations and evaluates their domains. A polynomial-ideal consequence check binds constant data exactly and keeps time/states as polynomial variables, avoiding unproved division by state coefficients. It is not an executable inverse. The generic solver always starts from the declared seed, uses the fixed conditioning/Armijo rules, and rejects detected competing roots. Its midpoint/end/coordinate-face probes are finite: global uniqueness and exhaustive root discovery are explicitly **not certified**. Failed probes never rescue a failed primary solve. Original SI algebraic residuals and located diagnostics are published through common Results, with strict readers and valid-prefix truncation.

The fresh internal thermal model records component conservation, first laws, caloric assumptions, passive constitutive contacts and equal/opposite transfer through shared journals/operations. Its states remain the two masses and energies; temperature is reconstructed, while contact heat and contact temperature remain simultaneous algebraic unknowns. The independent bisection reference uses the implicit time relation above, never the production residual or integrator. Both methods publish 61 samples to 300 s; the tests instrument the runtime and fail if the reduced `numeric.rhs` is called.

Perturbation includes positive capacities/conductances, changed nonlinear resistance, reverse heat flow, renamed arbitrary region IDs, SI-compatible units, row/state permutations, different seeds and the `a=0` limit. An unrelated dimensioned polynomial system matches hand roots. A late classified failure retains the published prefix. The conditioning edge tests use 8.8e-8 and 8.9706e-8 perturbations, without changing the existing 1e-8 admission threshold.

Regression evidence: all 26 derived-execution tests pass; all seven existing affine tests pass; all 12 frozen M1 U1 method/refinement trajectories pass with unchanged references; all 18 F0 assessment tests pass. Structured codes for an exhausted budget (`nonlinear_iteration_limit`), budget > 30 (`nonlinear_invalid_settings`), rank-deficient Jacobian (`nonlinear_singular_jacobian`), two admissible roots in 1D and 2D (`nonlinear_multiple_roots`), a root outside the bounds or outside an `admissible` guard (`nonlinear_domain`), a seed outside the domain (`nonlinear_invalid_seed`) and a nonfinite residual (`nonlinear_nonfinite`); repeated calls are bitwise deterministic. With three roots on `[0,4]` the probes detected the competing roots.

- **Required repair (generic regularity gate):** convergence is accepted when the scaled residual is below tolerance and `cond_inf(J_scaled)·eps ≤ 1e-8`, but cond is scale-invariant and identically 1 for a 1×1 block, so a nonregular root can be published. Reproduction: `solve(lambda z:[(z[0]-1)**2], lambda z:[[2*(z[0]-1)]], [2.], [[1,3]], [1.], [1.])` returns `x=1.0000076` as converged (residual 5.8e-11, Jacobian 1.5e-5, cond 1, error 7.6e-6 ≫ the 1e-12 step tolerance); the seed is the midpoint and the lower-bound probe fails as singular, so no competing root is detected. The existing singular test starts exactly at `J=0`. This violates the approved "regular Jacobian at the published root" rule. Suggested fix: also require the final Newton correction `‖J_scaled⁻¹ r‖∞` to be below the step tolerance (or bound the smallest singular value of `J_scaled`), and add this double-root case as a regression. The thermal acceptance case is unaffected (`|det| = 60`, cond near 1 by construction).

The existing relative-conditioning threshold is unchanged; an additional `epsilon/sigma_min(J_scaled) <= 1e-8` gate detects absolute inverse amplification that a scalar or uniformly small matrix's condition number misses. It rejects with `nonlinear_ill_conditioned` and reports the smallest scaled singular value in the message. This is a numerical gate in the declared scaled units, not a global mathematical regularity proof. It also rejects a near-double-root seed whose correction is already small; a correction-only fix would miss that case. Small physical slopes with consistent explicit residual scaling remain admissible. `stepNorm` now reports the final tested correction, rather than a previous iteration's step. The common-model contract describes the stricter checks; no tolerance was relaxed.

Four new test methods cover Claude's exact double-root reproducer and a near-root seed, small residual with excessive correction, a uniformly small 2x2 Jacobian, and a properly scaled small-slope control. ### F3 third slice — index-1 DAEs with consistent initialization

**Revised user decision, 28 October 2026; implemented and awaiting independent review.** The user approved index/drift admission, rejection rules and the law-derived thermal reference, but explicitly rejected adding SUNDIALS or any dependency. The implementation uses the existing SciPy BDF and second-slice nonlinear solver, solving algebraic variables inside each RHS and BDF finite-difference probe. The explicit instruction to initialize algebraic variables from given states narrows the earlier mixed-initialization proposal: differential states are fixed inputs, not adjustable guesses. Coupled residual BDF, mixed free-state initialization and a complete DAE starting tangent remain deferred. No IDA binding was installed, no requirement file changed, and no DAE residual integrator was introduced. Claude is unavailable; F3 and the preceding regularity repair remain in review, not done.

**Workspace and ownership.** Primary workspace: Solve. Model supplies replayed component conservation, first laws, constitutive assumptions and typed validity conditions. Transform selects exact derivative/algebraic rows and an identity formulation. Solve binds states, guesses, bounds, scales and tolerances; Results receives the original SI algebraic residuals, index proof, initialization and checked-prefix diagnostics. Equation/history/condition/partition changes invalidate formulation and runs; numerical data, state/algebraic bounds, guesses, scales and tolerances invalidate numerical artifacts. Labels do not change physics. Existing Model/Solve controls and readers are reused. Thermodynamics files, the second-slice nonlinear solver, routes, v2 adapter/executionProvenance and frozen M1-A/B/C remain unchanged. No library item was created.

**Implemented admission.** Explicit version 4 retains v3 nonlinear settings and adds `dae: {stateBounds: {quantityId: {lowerDataId,upperDataId}}, driftTolerance}`. State bounds cover exactly the differential states; each bound is finite, ordered and dimensionally compatible. The drift tolerance must be positive and at most `1e-9`, with the Newton residual tolerance no larger. Versions 1/2/3 retain their semantics.

- Admit the semi-explicit class `xdot=f(t,x,z), g(t,x,z)=0` with a square nonempty algebraic block, distinct variable ownership, replayed source identities, complete unit-bearing data and the second slice's smooth arithmetic subset. Separate first derivatives with nonzero constant coefficients can be isolated; coupled/nonlinear derivatives, algebraic derivatives and state-dependent derivative coefficients reject. This is not arbitrary mass-matrix or fully implicit execution.
- `core/compilador/dae.py` forms `g_z` from the actual SI-bound residuals. It factors the determinant and encloses it by exact rational interval arithmetic over the declared state/algebraic box and `0 <= t <= tf`; the enclosure must exclude zero. This supplies a conservative sufficient index-one certificate, not an index inferred from equation count or a numerical success. Unsupported interval expressions and inconclusive enclosures reject. The exact box is enforced during evaluation. The unchanged nonlinear solver separately checks relative conditioning, the smallest scaled singular value, residual and final correction at each solved root. Global uniqueness is not inferred from nonsingularity.
- All differential states require given initial data. Hold them fixed, solve `g(t0,x0,z0)=0`, verify any separately supplied fixed algebraic values and selected initial equations, then validate the physical guards. Guesses are not fixed constraints. Missing state data is a pending initialization finding; conflicting values and out-of-domain initialization are incompatible. No initial inventory is adjusted or least-squares compromise introduced.
- Only BDF is admitted. Higher-index/fully implicit structure gives a located rejection explicitly naming IDA as a future option. There is no alternate method, symbolic cubic inversion, artificial mass, index reduction, earlier-model fallback or new dependency.

**Drift and publication.** Independently re-evaluate original algebraic residuals after each successful closure and require `max(abs(g_i)/scale_i) <= driftTolerance`. The common integrator now has an opt-in accepted-state hook: before retaining an internal endpoint, version 4 freshly solves/checks its algebraic constraints and checks selected conservation inventory changes against integrated budgets with tolerance `1e-6`. A failure occurs before appending the candidate segment, so only the earlier checked prefix survives. Old systems have no hook and keep their behavior. Existing classified integration retry/termination behavior remains; this slice adds no retry loop or tolerance changes.

Publication uses the existing differential interpolant, freshly solves algebraic variables there and independently checks conservation at each published sample; it never interpolates algebraic values, projects states or publishes stale roots. Failed samples truncate the prefix; no valid samples means `fallido`. These checks cover RHS evaluations, internal accepted endpoints and published samples, not every point between them. The nested implementation does not expose an independent full BDF residual/tangent certificate. Results retains separate numerical and physical verdicts. `daeDiagnostics` records accepted-check count, maximum successful algebraic residual, maximum successful endpoint/publication conservation error, tolerance, actual method and scope; strict Python/TypeScript readers validate it alongside the existing nonlinear diagnostics and F0 signature.

**Fundamental-law thermal acceptance.** The test authors a fresh two-region graph through the same shared journals/operations as the second slice, without modifying its tests or frozen M1 documents. Start with local component conservation and the first law; record closed material boundaries, no reaction or work, rigid geometry, negligible kinetic/potential storage, uniform single-phase states, positive constant specific capacities and validity `290 <= T <= 370 K`. Record equal/opposite heat transfer through a contact without storage. The retained differential inventories are `x=(m1,U1,m2,U2)` with `dm1/dt=dm2/dt=0`, `dU1/dt=-q`, `dU2/dt=q`. Dependent totals come from sums of the laws and remain checks rather than extra independent rows.

Retain `z=(q,theta,T1,T2)` in four algebraic rows, with constitutive/caloric assumptions distinguished from conservation:

`q-G1*(T1-theta)=0`, `q+a*q^3-G2*(theta-T2)=0`,

`U1-m1*c1*(T1-Tref)=0`, `U2-m2*c2*(T2-Tref)=0`.

The compiler now accepts retained caloric temperatures as an alternative to explicit reconstruction and enforces the same caloric validity interval. It does not inject a reduced temperature ODE or solve the cubic symbolically. Every physical reduction and contact declaration retains its shared operation history. No authoring operation needed modification.

Given initial states are `m1=2 kg`, `U1=36000 J`, `m2=3 kg`, `U2=0 J`; parameters are `c1=c2=300 J/(kg K)`, `G1=G2=12 W/K`, `a=1/57600 W^-2`, `Tref=300 K`. This deliberately replaces the unimplemented proposal to solve the energies from total energy and initial temperature difference. Inexact algebraic guesses `(100 W,330 K,330 K,330 K)` converge to `(240 W,340 K,360 K,300 K)` while inventories stay fixed. The state certificate box uses masses `[1,4] kg` and energies `[-9000,63000] J`; algebraic bounds are `[0,300] W` and `[290,370] K`. Use energy row scales `36000 J`, contact row scales `240 W`, heat variable scale `240 W` and temperature scales `60 K`.

For `Ci=mi*ci`, the algebraic Jacobian determinant (up to the row/column permutation sign) is `-C1*C2*(G1+G2+3*a*G1*q^2)`, nonzero on that box. The caloric relations determine temperatures from given inventories; positive conductances and nonnegative resistance make `Delta=R*q+B*q^3` strictly monotone, providing an independent uniqueness argument for this case.

**Independent analytic reference.** Conservation gives total energy `36000 J` and equilibrium temperature `324 K`. With `Delta=T1-T2`, `R=1/G1+1/G2=1/6 K/W`, `B=a/G2=1/691200 K/W^3`, `s=1/C1+1/C2=1/360 K/J`, eliminate the contact temperature for verification only: `Delta=R*q+B*q^3` and `dDelta/dt=-s*q`. Integration yields

`t=(R/s)*ln(q0/q)+(3*B/(2*s))*(q0^2-q^2) = 60*ln(240/q)+(57600-q^2)/1280`, with `q0=240 W`.

One hundred scalar bisection iterations on `(0,240] W` supply each independent reference sample, without production Newton, residual evaluation or time integration. Reconstruct `T1=324+0.6*Delta`, `T2=324-0.4*Delta`, `theta=T1-q/12` and `Ui=Ci*(Ti-300)`. Preparation arithmetic bounds raw energy roundoff by `3.64e-11 J`, contact residual by `7.82e-13 W` and implicit-time residual by `1.14e-13 s`. At 300 s, `q=3.42289391969 W`, `T1=324.342324204 K`, `T2=323.771783864 K`, `theta=324.0570830439 K`.

**Acceptance and measured evidence.** BDF publishes 61 samples on `0..300 s`, with `rtol=1e-8`, mass absolute tolerance `1e-10 kg`, energy absolute tolerance `1e-6 J`, Newton residual/step tolerances `1e-10`/`1e-12`, and drift tolerance `1e-9`. Repeat at one tenth of all these tolerances. Algebraic quantities are controlled by their Newton residual/correction settings rather than independent BDF state tolerances. Require each temperature error/60 K, heat error/240 W and energy error/36000 J <= `1e-5`, mass relative drift and total-energy drift/36000 J <= `1e-6`, and original algebraic residuals within requested tolerances. The test instruments the runtime to fail if the old reduced `numeric.rhs` is called.

| 1 | 7.75445e-8 | 2.02110e-16 |

### F3 fourth slice — common steady-state residual solve

**Approved and implemented for review, 29 October 2026 (`dec-f3-fourth-slice` taken).** The common steady study specializes the replayed derived model and reuses the unchanged dense damped Newton. No dependency or library example was added. Native v2 retirement is separately taken as **deferred until Claude reviews the route switch in use**; the fallback remains and no deletion is authorized.

**Pre-implementation findings (preserved baseline).** The common reader admits only `estudio.tipo='temporal'`; current derived selections are versions 1–4. `DerivedSystem` and `NonlinearRuntime` construct temporal initialization, horizon, integrator settings and budget states, so calling them with a fictitious horizon is not a steady-state implementation. `compilation_report` routes derived selections directly to that constructor. The Newton engine already enforces scaled residual plus correction convergence, bounded Armijo trials, conditioning and smallest-singular-value limits, and competing-root probes. Its successful result explicitly says local root only; finite probes do not prove global uniqueness. The common results format already permits scalar series without axes, but `resolverEngine.ts:derivedResult`, `CompilationSummary.tsx` and the saved Engine Results view currently assume time-series data. All these boundaries need an explicit steady variant, not a fabricated time axis.

#### Approved scope and equation semantics

Primary workspace: **Solve**. Model retains fundamental laws, actual reduction history, constitutive premises, bounds and conserved quantities. Transform selects the exact original derivative/algebraic rows and an identity formulation; the steady specialization is an inspectable execution transformation with a source-row map. Solve supplies constant data, free steady unknowns, declared invariant bindings, seeds, scales, bounds and Newton settings. Results publishes one state, original SI residuals, balance checks and separate convergence/regularity/uniqueness evidence.

For the admitted semi-explicit derived system `xdot=f(x,z,p)`, `0=g(x,z,p)`, form the simultaneous residual from the **original selected equations with all retained first time derivatives set to zero**, denoted `R(w)=F(w,0;p)`. Solve all remaining free state/algebraic unknowns together with the existing Newton engine. Do not nest the time-dependent nonlinear runtime, rewrite the model as an arbitrary terminal residual, or copy a separately maintained RHS. Original row units and denominator/domain guards survive rate isolation, substitutions and zero-derivative specialization. Retain each source equation, its pre/post-specialization expression and every explicit invariant substitution. No selected physical balance may disappear merely because it becomes dependent or zero.

The first scope is autonomous, finite-dimensional, nonspatial replayed models with separately isolatable first derivatives having nonzero constant scalar coefficients and smooth rational arithmetic already admitted by the nonlinear slice (integer powers and guarded division). All model parameters and boundary data are fixed; free unknowns are selected state/algebraic quantities. Inverse parameter fitting, continuation, bifurcation/stability analysis, phase/complementarity switching, time-periodic forcing, PDEs, general implicit/mass-matrix residuals, higher-index reduction, and arbitrary conserved-total constraint replacement are deferred. A time-independent solution is not a dynamically stable or attracting solution.

**Conserved inventories and equation count.** The selection must distinguish a Newton seed from a prescribed physical invariant. An individually invariant state may be bound to a supplied datum only when its replayed derivative row proves `xdot=0` identically on the admitted domain. Retain that law and its binding in provenance and check it after solving; it supplies no free column or nontrivial Newton row. An initial value in an old temporal study is neither an automatic steady constraint nor an automatic seed. Coupled conserved totals that leave a family of steady states require an explicit future constraint/reduction design; this slice diagnoses the undetermined family rather than choosing a state by its seed. After justified individual invariant substitution and explicit acyclic reconstruction, require a nonempty square independent residual block. A selected row reducing to `0=0` remains an audited satisfied identity; a nonzero constant row is an inconsistency; redundant nonidentity rows, missing closures and unmatched unknowns reject. Never fix rank with a pseudoinverse, least squares, diagonal regularization, fabricated controller or silently removed balance.

#### Admission and diagnostics

| Gate | Required behavior |
|---|---|
| Source and selection | Replay valid history, pin source/formulation signatures, enforce distinct state/algebraic/parameter/invariant ownership and exact equation IDs. Reject stale/unsigned/unsupported history, conflicting selections and any attempt to execute an earlier valid prefix. Every selected or required active constraint must be solved, proved redundant, or explicitly checked. |
| Steady study | Explicit opt-in versioned steady study, identity formulation, supported derivative class and no unresolved time dependence in residuals, data, boundary conditions or guards. Do not evaluate a time-varying boundary at an arbitrary t0 and call it steady. No automatic method substitution from RK45/BDF. |
| Data, dimensions and domain | Finite constant parameter/invariant values; finite seed and ordered bounds for every free unknown; positive variable and original-row residual scales in compatible units. Retain typed restrictions and all pre-cancellation nonzero-denominator conditions. Check guards before evaluating trial residuals. Invalid units, missing data, unsupported expressions and an inadmissible seed are located rejections. |
| Structure | Check unknown coverage, square size, structural matching and exact identities/duplicates where established; report these separately from numerical rank. A square count or a perfect matching is not a uniqueness proof. Explicitly diagnose unbound invariants, inconsistent constant residuals and missing/redundant relations. |
| Numerical root | Call the unchanged damped Newton with declared settings. Use its existing `cond_inf(Js)*epsilon <= 1e-8` and `epsilon/sigma_min(Js) <= 1e-8` gates, where `Js=diag(1/residualScales)*J*diag(variableScales)`. Require both scaled residual and final Newton correction tolerances, including when the initial residual is zero. No tolerance relaxation or fallback. |
| Independent final validation | Re-evaluate original specialized rows, reconstructed quantities, invariant bindings, all selected conditions and independent balance checks at the returned candidate. A small Newton residual is insufficient if an original law or guard fails. Retain raw SI residuals as well as scaled values. |
| Failure publication | Admission/data/structure failures use located common diagnostics; numerical failure returns a failed attempt with diagnostics and no accepted state. A last iterate can be diagnostic evidence only. No `parcial` trajectory, previous-run reuse, silent clipping, changed bounds, relaxed tolerance, symbolic root substitution, native v2 or long-time-integration fallback. |

Compiler diagnostic families are `steady_invalid_selection`, `steady_unsupported_structure`, `steady_time_dependent`, `steady_missing_data`, `steady_invalid_units`, `steady_unbound_invariant`, `steady_constraint_conflict`, `steady_structure_mismatch`, `steady_domain` and `steady_original_residual`. Preserve the actual Newton codes: `nonlinear_invalid_settings`, `nonlinear_invalid_seed`, `nonlinear_nonfinite`, `nonlinear_singular_jacobian`, `nonlinear_ill_conditioned`, `nonlinear_stagnation`, `nonlinear_iteration_limit`, `nonlinear_line_search`, `nonlinear_domain`, `nonlinear_multiple_roots`. Do not report failed convergence as proof that no steady state exists.

**Uniqueness is a separate verdict.** A converged candidate with a nonsingular scaled Jacobian is a numerically regular local root, not a rigorous existence/uniqueness certificate for the entire box. Preserve deterministic competing-root probes and reject detected distinct admissible roots; do not let a successful secondary probe rescue a failed primary solve. Report the number of completed probes and their finite scope. The mandatory general success verdict is `local_regular_root`, with `globalUniqueness='not_proved'`; an exact uniqueness argument for the acceptance fixture is independent test evidence, not a hard-coded production certificate. Unsuccessful/inadmissible probes cannot justify uniqueness. A nonsingular determinant everywhere also must not be advertised as a global injectivity proof. If the user requires proved-global uniqueness for every admitted model, that is a larger interval/monotonicity certification slice and is not silently included here.

**Conditioning evidence.** Publish the original row/unknown ordering, unit-bearing scales, final scaled Jacobian condition number, smallest singular value, both fixed regularity thresholds, residual norm, final correction norm, termination reason and the existing iteration/evaluation/backtracking counters with their actual primary/probe scope. Recover final singular-value evidence from the same analytic selected-residual Jacobian in the steady compiler; do not change the Newton algorithm or its thresholds for this proposal. Include equation/quantity IDs, stage and source/formulation/data/settings signatures in every diagnostic. Unknown or unavailable metrics are explicitly unavailable, not zero. Near-singular roots reject even when their residual is small; conditioning is tied to the declared scaling and is not a physical-accuracy guarantee.

#### Fundamental-law acceptance and independent reference

Author a **new internal** two-region model through the existing shared journals and typed equation operations. Start from local component conservation and the first law, then record closed material boundaries, no reaction, fixed rigid geometry, negligible kinetic/potential storage and mechanical work, uniform single-phase bodies, positive constant masses/capacities, and the declared caloric validity interval `290 <= T <= 370 K`. Derive `dm1/dt=dm2/dt=0` and the two energy balances. Bind the two invariant masses explicitly; do not infer their values from a solver seed. Introduce an imposed heat input to region 1, a contact without storage between regions, and heat loss from region 2 to a reservoir. Equal/opposite contact terms and the lack of interface storage are explicit constraints. Caloric and heat-transfer laws below are **constitutive premises**, not consequences of conservation.

The retained dynamic laws are

`dU1/dt = Q - q`, `dU2/dt = q - q_e`.

Retain the contact/caloric rows rather than inserting their solved expressions:

`q = G1*(T1-theta)`,

`q + a*q^3 = G2*(theta-T2)`,

`q_e = H*(T2-T_a)`,

`U1 = m1*c1*(T1-Tref)`, `U2 = m2*c2*(T2-Tref)`.

Use shared declaration/substitution/combination operations to connect these rows to the original laws, with actual before/after expressions, units, source IDs and guards. Derive the dependent total first law by summing the two balances; keep it as a verification row, not an extra independent Newton equation. Steady specialization sets the two energy derivatives to zero while preserving the source dynamic model. The seven simultaneous unknowns are `(U1,U2,q,theta,T1,T2,q_e)`. The two mass laws remain in the report as verified invariant bindings. Nothing dispatches on these names or on a thermal-equipment type. If authoring cannot express a required step, record the shared operation gap before implementation; do not inject the terminal seven-row system into a fixture and describe it as a derivation.

Choose `m1=2 kg`, `m2=3 kg`, `c1=c2=300 J/(kg K)`, `G1=G2=12 W/K`, `a=1/57600 W^-2`, `H=6 W/K`, `Q=120 W`, `T_a=Tref=300 K`. Use seeds `(18000 J,18000 J,90 W,330 K,335 K,310 K,150 W)`, energy bounds `[-9000,63000] J`, temperature bounds `[290,370] K` and heat-rate bounds `[0,300] W`. Scales are `36000 J` for each energy, `60 K` for each temperature, `240 W` for each heat rate; residual scales are `240 W` for each energy-rate/contact/sink row and `36000 J` for each caloric row. Seeds deliberately violate the equations but satisfy the domain; they are not physical initial conditions.

**Independent analytic reference, test-only.** Conservation at steady state gives `q=q_e=Q`. The sink then gives `T2=T_a+Q/H`; the two contact relations give `theta=T2+(Q+a*Q^3)/G2` and `T1=theta+Q/G1`; finally `Ui=mi*ci*(Ti-Tref)`. Thus:

| Quantity | Reference |
|---|---:|
| `q`, `q_e` | `120 W` each |
| `T2` | `320 K` |
| `theta` | `332.5 K` |
| `T1` | `342.5 K` |
| `U1` | `25500 J` |
| `U2` | `18000 J` |

This derivation uses scalar conservation and the constitutive laws directly, with no production Newton, compiler residual, time integration or symbolic root solve. Positive `H,G1,G2` force one value at each step, proving uniqueness for this specified case; all values lie inside the declared domain. Total stored energy is `43500 J`, an output, not an imposed conserved energy. This is a driven steady flow of heat: global verification is `Q-q_e=0`, not conservation of the transient stored energy.

For residual order `(Q-q, q-q_e, q-G1*(T1-theta), q+a*q^3-G2*(theta-T2), q_e-H*(T2-T_a), U1-C1*(T1-Tref), U2-C2*(T2-Tref))`, the Jacobian determinant with the stated unknown ordering is `-G1*G2*H=-864` in the corresponding dimensional basis. The dimensionless scaled Jacobian at the reference has `cond_inf=35.5208333333` and `sigma_min=0.250053926386`; `cond_inf*epsilon=7.88721e-15`, `epsilon/sigma_min=8.87987e-16`. Preparation-only exact rational substitution yields seven zero residuals. These proposal calculations are now checked by a fresh shared-operation fixture and the independent reference; measured execution evidence is below.

**Acceptance thresholds.** Newton residual/step tolerances `1e-10`/`1e-12`, unchanged limits of 30 iterations and 20 backtracks; repeat with both tolerances divided by ten. Require maximum error scaled by `60 K`, `240 W` or `36000 J` respectively <= `1e-5`; original scaled residuals <= the requested Newton residual tolerance; individual and summed first-law residuals/`240 W` <= `1e-6`; invariant mass relative error <= `1e-6`. Recompute checks from returned physical quantities independently. Both runs must meet their thresholds; roundoff-level error need not decrease monotonically. Require actual execution of the generic Newton on the simultaneous block, with a nonzero initial residual; instrument tests to reject use of time integration, symbolic roots or a fixture-specific evaluator. The production uniqueness verdict stays local/unproved even though this reference proves this case globally.

Perturb `Q,H,G1,G2,a`, capacities and reservoir temperature and recompute the analytic formulas; use compatible kW/mW, kJ and temperature-unit inputs, arbitrary region/quantity names, and permuted rows/unknowns. A second nonthermal polynomial residual control verifies that no thermal identifier or physical family selects the solver. Domain narrowing that retains the reference must not change the root; narrowing that excludes it must fail without changing bounds.

#### Rejection and regression requirements

- New shared-operation full-model/HTTP/reader acceptance must fail on the pre-implementation tree, which rejects the steady study/version-5 selection. Preserve the exact baseline and failure before implementing; do not alter frozen tests or pretend a helper-only rejection proves end-to-end coverage.
- Remove a contact/caloric relation; duplicate a row; omit an unknown, scale or invariant datum; bind incompatible units; give invalid bounds or a seed outside guards. Require located structure/data diagnostics, never a solved state.
- Remove the heat sink with nonzero input: the summed first-law residual is incompatible. With both external heat terms absent and no total-energy specification, the closed pair has a continuum of steady energies; diagnose rank/nonuniqueness rather than select one from the seed. A seed exactly on that family must still fail the Jacobian gate.
- Use generic `u^2-1=0` on a box containing both regular roots to require `nonlinear_multiple_roots`; a declared positive-root domain is a separate unambiguous model. Include a singular root and a uniformly tiny but relatively well-conditioned Jacobian to distinguish singularity, relative condition and absolute inverse amplification. Finite probes missing a root must never produce a global-uniqueness claim.
- Exercise every inherited Newton rejection with deterministic fixtures: nonfinite residual/Jacobian, singular/ill-conditioned Jacobian, tiny correction with large residual, exhausted iteration budget, failed sufficient decrease, and no admissible trial. Retain their original codes and demonstrate that a successful secondary probe cannot rescue a primary failure. Assert no failed candidate is published as a solution.
- Reject time-varying forcing even if its value happens to balance at t0, unsupported derivative coupling/order, spatial/competing scaling selections, stale history after Undo, unaccounted constraints and unsupported closure calls. No time-marching fallback or residual reduction to an earlier graph.
- Inject post-solve original-law/guard failures; verify the independent final check blocks publication. Tamper with scalar results, signatures, residual evidence, regularity metrics and uniqueness claims; strict readers must reject them. Check save/reload, stale request cancellation, separate rejected-attempt storage and CSV values without a fabricated time column.

Add an explicitly versioned study variant `{tipo:'steady', version:1, metodoSolicitado:'damped_newton'}`; do not translate or reinterpret existing `temporal` keys, require a horizon, or reuse `initialValues` as guesses. Use common `rage.resultados` scalar series (`ejes:[]`, numeric `valores`) and optional versioned `steadyDiagnostics`, with separate local regularity, finite-probe multiplicity and global-uniqueness status. No artificial t=0/t=1 trajectory and no temporal budget integrals. Preserve numerical completion versus physical certification: successful balance checks do not certify real-fluid thermodynamics, dynamic stability or predictive accuracy. The existing Results workspace needs scalar table/export/diagnostics support in its shared renderer. This is a generic study presentation, not a library example, new page or Model layout change.

#### Implemented evidence and combined review handoff

The common compiler/report dispatch selects `SteadySystem` only for v5. It replays original laws, separates individually invariant bindings from seeds, keeps original SI rows and guards, checks structural matching and rational constraint consequences, then calls the unchanged Newton exactly once for the simultaneous block. A separate original-law/guard evaluation blocks publication of a faulty candidate. The closed pair without external heat is rejected for unmatched structural freedom, including an exactly balanced seed; singular full-match controls still reach the numerical Jacobian gate. No earlier prefix, time integration, symbolic-root replacement or native fallback supplies a steady result.

The new strict Python/TypeScript selection, study, compilation and scalar-result variants use the existing common API, data controls, rejected-attempt store and Results view. Results shows local regularity/global-unproved status, raw SI residuals, scaled checks and conditioning; CSV has quantity/unit/value columns only. F0 structure is reported independently of Newton convergence; transient initialization is explicitly not evaluated for a steady guess. Fingerprints bind the current selection, data, settings and scalar publication. These are content checks, not authentication or independent physical certification.

### F3 package-closure volumes in the steady path (demo-02-steady backend half)

**Delivered by Claude, 1 November 2026 (uncommitted; independent review pending).** A version-5 steady selection now admits package-closure volumes and reduced momentum journals. It specialises the same deduced derivative rows of the dynamic model at zero rate (decision `dec-demo02-dynamic-first`): no separate steady deduction exists.
- *Closure outputs* (T, V or P, phase amounts, compositions, h/u/v/M, presence, liquid volume) are algebraic unknowns that the package eliminates: `core/compilador/derived_closure.py` `SteadyClosures` forms the closure inputs from the steady unknowns and calls the same `derived_bridge` functions as dynamic execution at every residual and Jacobian evaluation. Their time derivatives vanish with the stationary unknowns. Phase presence is the certified flash at the root, never assumed; the published phase set must satisfy the recorded policy.
- *Solver*: the existing damped Newton with a central-difference Jacobian over the closure (step `1e-6 max(|z|, scale)`). Without a symbolic basis over package outputs, retained consequences (earlier stages of the selected laws, declared relations, summed balances) are verified numerically at the root with the residual scale of a selected row of the same unit, not proved symbolically; rational powers and finite numeric constants are admitted in this mode.
- *Momentum*: the reduced-journal admission of dynamic execution is reused; the path row at zero rate balances drive, head and loss.
- *Publication* (additive, strict readers extended): report field `thermodynamicClosures` (volume, package, mode, PH/UV, anchor, liquid-only, outputs with units, package constants), evidence field `thermodynamicClosures` (with `phaseSet` and `fingerprint`), series `closure.<quantityId>` and checks `thermodynamics.closure.<volume>` (tolerance 1 for an equilibrium certificate ratio, 1e-9 for a single-phase reconstruction). Package constants are not selection parameters.
- *Evidence* (`tests/test_steady_package_closure.py`): the Demo 0.1 flash drum steady root against an independent solution from direct `core.mezclas` calls and `scipy.optimize.root`: masses within 1e-10 kg, H within 8.5e-13 relative, T = 362.5404885 K within 5e-12 K, phase set {vapor-1, liquido-1} equal; 10 Newton iterations. The same deduced model integrated to 6000 s ends within 2e-4 of the root; the publication passes `read_result` and a tampered closure block is rejected. The momentum drum steady root matches its direct reference, and the steady outlet flow balances the liquid head against the loss. Existing steady and F3 suites pass (37 + 10).
- *What Codex's one-step "Steady state" Transform must show* (queue item 5; no backend change needed): the version-5 selection with the dynamic `states` rows, their quantities as `unknowns` with seeds/bounds/scales, the dynamic parameters and reconstructions (including the package-amount and floor-pressure reconstructions), checks scaled as rates; after Solve, the scalar states, the `closure.*` outputs (temperature, phase amounts and compositions, liquid volume), the certified phase set and the `thermodynamics.closure.*` check. Rejections to surface: `closure_input_unresolved` (a closure input not expressed in steady unknowns), `competing_closure`, `unsupported_momentum_regime`, and Newton failures with the package message when a trial point leaves the package domain.
- *Limits*: identity selection without algebraic rows beyond the closure; one vapour and one liquid; no symbolic consequence proof with package outputs; the finite-difference Jacobian inherits the closure tolerance (singular-value gates still apply).

### F3 fifth slice — inverse specifications in Solve

**Implemented by Claude, 30 October 2026 (user assignment; Codex-owned files untouched).** Primary workspace: **Solve**. New modules only: `core/solvers/inverse_spec.py` (generic kernel), `core/solvers/inverse_steady.py` (adapter on the slice-4 `SteadySystem`, by subclassing) and `tests/test_f3_inverse_specifications.py`. No dependency, library example, frozen M1 file or compiler/Model/frontend source was edited.

*Semantics.* A versioned Solve record `{format: 'rage.inverse-specification', version: 1, specifications: [{id, quantityId, targetDataId, residualScaleDataId}], freedInputs: [{quantityId, seedDataId, lowerDataId, upperDataId, scaleDataId}]}` adds one typed residual `quantity - target = 0` per specification and changes the same number of declared constant parameters from data to unknowns. It is not a Model equation: the forward v5 selection is first admitted unchanged at the declared inputs; the adapter then releases those bindings and re-runs specialization, rational admission, Groebner consistency, original guards, constraint consequences and balance checks on the augmented block; Newton, final original-row evaluation and regularity evidence are the unchanged `SteadySystem.solve`. The report keeps `sourceSignature` and the forward `formulationSignature`, adds `specificationSignature` (record plus referenced data declarations) and declares `invalidates: [solve, results]`, `retains: [model, transform]`. The freed input is published as `steady.<quantityId>` and the specification row as `steady.residual.specification.<id>`.

*Admission and diagnostics (codes).* `inverse_not_square` (unequal or empty specification/freed sets), `inverse_invalid_input` (not a declared constant parameter of the selection), `inverse_invalid_output` (not a solved or reconstructed quantity), `steady_invalid_units` (target units), `inverse_unreachable` (no complete matching of the augmented incidence: the freed input does not structurally reach the output), `inverse_rank_deficient` (scaled augmented Jacobian singular at the seed; when the forward block is regular, the Schur-complement sensitivity of outputs to freed inputs is reported), `inverse_target_unattainable` (Groebner-inconsistent for every input value, or, after a Newton failure with one specification, forward samples along the freed-input interval place the target outside the sampled range and classify the limit as `bound` or `turning_point`; never clipped; sampling is evidence, not proof). Other Newton outcomes, including `nonlinear_multiple_roots` for a target with two branches, pass through unchanged.

*Evidence.* `python -u tests/test_f3_inverse_specifications.py -v`: **13 tests pass** (about 65 s). Worst errors against independent references:

| Case | Reference | Worst error |
|---|---|---:|
| (a) Linear mixer, fix `x3`, free `F1` (targets 0.3/0.6/0.75) | analytic `F1 = F2 (x3-x2)/(x1-x3)` | 3.0e-16 relative |
| (b) Law-derived slice-4 thermal model, fix `T1 = 350 K`, free heat input | analytic `T1 = 300 + Q/3 + Q^3/691200`; forward v5 solve at the found Q | 8.5e-14 W; round trip 5.7e-14 K |
| (c) UNIFAC-original ethanol/water, `z1 = 0.4`, 101325 Pa, fix vapor fraction, free T (generic kernel over the package potentials `Sistema.mu`) | bubble and dew: `coexistencia_lv.resolver_punto`; beta 0.25/0.5/0.75: Gibbs `equilibrio_TP` at the found T; dew/bubble bracketed by `equilibrio_TP` at T +/- 0.01 K | T 1.1e-13 K; beta 5.5e-14; x, y 2.4e-14 |
| (d) Unattainable: heater 360 K with Q in [0, 150] W; `u = b(4-b)` with target 5 | sampled maximum 354.88 K at the bound; maximum 4 at interior b = 2 | `bound` / `turning_point` |
| (e) Non-square, invalid input/output, wrong units, unreachable | rejected during admission with Newton patched to fail if called | — |

Additional controls: `u = (b-1)^2` with the seed at the stationary point rejects `inverse_rank_deficient`, and a regular seed returns `1 + sqrt(2)` exactly; the two-branch target rejects `nonlinear_multiple_roots` and a one-branch interval returns b = 1. Negative control: with the reachability classification and the structural check patched out, the three unattainable/unreachable tests fail (3/3).

**F-1 (defect, `core/solvers/inverse_spec.py` `classify_unattainable`/`InverseProblem.diagnose`).** The reachability diagnosis ignores failed forward samples: the sampled range is formed only from converged samples, so a target that lies in a failed sub-interval is reported as `inverse_target_unattainable`, and the limit is labelled `bound` whenever the extremum is the first or last *converged* sample, not the declared bound. *Reproducer* (`repro_false_unattainable_steady.py`): `parameter_case([('x^3-x-p', '1'), ('u-x^2', '1')], {x: seed 1.9 in [0, 2], u: seed 3.61 in [0, 4]}, {p: 0.5})`, specify `u = 0.1`, free `p` (seed 0.5 in [-1, 1]). The primary Newton fails (`nonlinear_domain`), the scan converges at 16 samples and fails at 17 (wherever `x^3 - x = p` has several roots in [0, 2]), and the adapter raises `inverse_target_unattainable` "outside the sampled reachable range [1.06069, 1.75488], limited by a bound of the freed input". The target is attainable: the unique admissible root is `x = sqrt(0.1)`, `p = -0.284605`, and the same record with seed `x = 1.2` solves to it exactly. The ethanol/water kernel with target `beta = 1.2` shows the label error: 29 of 33 samples fail and the limit is reported as `bound` at T = 360.625 K, inside the declared [330, 400] K. *Required fix before commit:* claim `inverse_target_unattainable` only when no forward sample failed (otherwise re-raise the original Newton failure with the scan attached as evidence), and label `bound` only when the extremum is a declared bound of the freed input; add the reproducer above as a regression test and rerun the 13 tests. Minor, no fix required: the generic kernel also diagnoses `nonlinear_multiple_roots` failures, while the steady adapter passes them through; align them when F-1 is fixed.

*F-1 repaired (Claude, 30 October 2026); independent re-review: **accepted**.* `reachable_scan` now records each failed sample's input and Newton code. `classify_unattainable` returns a verdict only when every forward sample converged, and labels `bound` only when the extremum equals a declared bound of the freed input (otherwise `turning_point`). With any failed sample, `diagnose` re-raises the original Newton failure code and message with `reachability.kind = inconclusive` and the converged samples and failed inputs attached. The generic kernel now passes `nonlinear_multiple_roots` through without scanning, matching the steady adapter. New regressions in `tests/test_f3_inverse_specifications.py`: the reproducer (seed x = 1.9 gives `nonlinear_domain` with an inconclusive scan, 17 failed samples; seed x = 1.2 gives p = -0.284605 exactly); ethanol/water `beta = 1.2` is inconclusive, not `bound`; kernel multiple-roots passthrough. 16/16 pass (46 s), with unchanged worst reference errors; the heater `bound` (Q = 150 W) and `u = b(4-b)` `turning_point` controls still pass. The following were checked and accepted. Both routes reject `null`, list, string, number, `{}` and `true` records with structured 422 errors, and they also reject a missing datum, empty or non-numeric text, division by zero, a zero residual scale and inverted bounds. With the current auto policy every Jacobian is dense (`sparse_blocks.policy`), so the forward-sized block Jacobian is never used on the augmented system.
