Reproducer: the HTTP mixer with target text `pi/6` or `+0.6` is solved and published by the canonical Python readers (HTTP 200 on `/resolver` and `/compilation`), but `leerResultados` and `readCompilationReport` reject it ("Invalid inverse constant expression." / "Expected a constant number."). The user sees the solve fail only after Newton has run. A `%` target is rejected the same way; the contract already scopes that case ("broader Pint aliases ... should be normalized to SI"). Required: the client admits exactly the constant grammar that Python admits for constant data (at least `pi` and unary `+`), or `InverseControls` rejects such text when it is entered, with the same message, before any request. Add both texts to `verificar_inverse_steady.mjs`.
- **F3H-2 (Codex, Light): re-selecting in Transform silently discards the Solve record.** `save()` in `frontend/app/models/DerivedExecution.tsx:88` and `applySteadyTransformResult` in `frontend/app/models/derivedSelection.ts:20` write a new `derivedSelection` without `inverseSolve`. Editing any Model datum in the Solve "Declared data" fieldset (for example the second feed `F2`) changes `derivedSourceIdentity`, so the user must re-select in Transform, and that step then deletes every target, residual scale, seed, bound and scale entered for the inverse solve, without notice. This is a Solve-owned record (contract: it "owns this Solve record and its declarations separately from Model data"), so a Transform re-pin must not erase it silently. Required: carry `inverseSolve` into the new selection when it still reads against it (each specified quantity is still an unknown or a reconstruction, each freed quantity is still a parameter, and the data IDs are still fresh); otherwise drop it with a visible notice in Solve. Add a lifecycle case to the verifier.

The model is rejected safely, but the code and message are misleading. Until inverse specifications are lowered over package closures, the adapter should reject closure models with its own code that says so, and Solve should not offer the controls for them. This belongs to the Claude lane (`core/solvers/inverse_steady.py`) and is not a Codex repair.

### Performance and native acceleration: analysis (claude-b, 7 November 2026; read-only; user-requested)

The user asked whether to bring Rust in progressively. The goal is to make the engine faster and get it ready for interactive dynamic simulation. Python is not replaced and the core is not rewritten. A read-only analysis profiled `fase-a-adopcion` at 92c0f7d. Its full report is a private page on the claude-b account. This section records its conclusions and is the canonical record. No implementation is authorized until the user schedules it.

**Measured findings.**
- **Flowsheet Solve (R1 → drum → R2, cProfile):** about 99% of the time is spent in thermodynamic closures.
  - About 25-33% rebuilds the formation enthalpy on every call (`_shift` → `PackagePhase`, which queries `chemicals` tables through pandas and sqlite). That value is constant during a run.
  - About 43% re-evaluates closures for event margins at every accepted step, where the results already computed could be reused.
- **Preparation (373 s):** dominated by SymPy (replay, signatures, `simplify`), plus 61 s of LaTeX rendering. This is algorithmic work. It holds the mathematical traceability and stays in Python.
- **Expression evaluation:** the closure interpreter is 30-48 times slower than Python code generated from the same AST with common-subexpression elimination. On the n = 40 stage train, BDF fell from 4.95 s to 1.73 s with generated code alone. After that, SciPy BDF overhead is about 94% of the time, so a native RHS would not reduce it.
- **UNIFAC:** a scalar call costs about 110 µs whether it has 2 or 8 species, because the cost is NumPy dispatch, not arithmetic. Batched evaluation costs 0.4-1 µs per point. One run made 693 thousand calls. This is the clearest native-kernel candidate.

**Decisions it supports.**
- Python keeps model representation, deduction, transformation, thermodynamic science and orchestration.
- Native code is considered only where a measured advantage remains after the Python fixes.
- The existing compiler is evolved, not replaced. One lowered, versioned intermediate representation gets several backends: the current interpreter as the reference, generated Python code, and the Jacobian through the same path, which retires the duplication with `lambdify`.
- Any native module is optional, with a pure-Python fallback, so Rage runs without Rust.
- The offline Windows executable must package it (`empaquetado/construir.ps1`), consistent with `dec-distribution-download-first`.

**Gradual roadmap (each stage gated by measurements).**
- **P0:** Python fixes, plus a benchmark harness covering four problems: a simple ODE, the flowsheet, the stage train at large n, and a standalone discretized-PDE prototype. The harness measures total time, time per step, memory, scaling and latency. The Python fixes are:
  - cache the formation datum per run;
  - reuse closure evaluations for event margins;
  - keep LaTeX rendering off the Solve path.
- **P1:** the lowered IR with generated-code and Jacobian backends, verified bit for bit or within a stated tolerance against the interpreter.
- **P2:** an optional PyO3/maturin spike with a measured go/no-go criterion, covering a scalar UNIFAC kernel (with dγ/dT) and an IR evaluator. The kernel is scalar because the closure algorithms (Brent, Newton, stability descent) call γ sequentially, so batching cannot replace the single call. Adoption requires at least 3 times over the best Python alternative on the target and at least 25% end to end on the flowsheet or stage train, with every check unchanged. Alternatives were compared: Numba, Cython, JAX/XLA, CasADi-style C code generation, and Cranelift/LLVM.
- **P3:** batched closure evaluation.
- **P4:** an interactive runtime. Worker processes run the simulation. A command journal records parameter changes, events and perturbations with consistent simulation time. Results stream to the interface without blocking. Concurrent runs are supported. This is interactive (soft) real time, not hard real time.
- **P5:** spatial PDE execution, when the user resumes spatial work.

**Limits of the analysis.**
- The Rust figures are estimates, because no Rust toolchain is installed on this machine.
- cProfile inflates paths with many small calls, so the flowsheet shares are relative.
- The cubic EOS and IAPWS-95 inside a network were not profiled.
- The interpreter sums with `math.fsum` (correctly rounded). A generated or native backend that adds left to right changes the last bits, so exact-value validations stay on the reference backend unless the user approves a stated tolerance.

Full report (private page): https://claude.ai/artifact/16fdaNy5kBsWg7j9L3oJ9X.

## F4. Boundaries and real equipment

- [ ] Packages for condensables + non-condensable gases, parametrized by species and laws; water + air will be a validation case, not the architecture.
- [ ] Solubility/Henry when it is modelled; insolubility only as an explicit hypothesis with a domain.
- [ ] Choose the gas formulation according to regime and data; UNIFAC does not solve this part by itself.
- [ ] Open boundary with an external pressure and a flow obtained from a physical relation or a declared constraint.
- [ ] Inlet, outlet, reversal, per-phase extraction and phase disappearance with the correct donor.
- [ ] Separate the thermodynamic domain from the vessel's limits: capacity, allowable pressure, relief, deformation or stopping.
- [ ] Heat losses and wall storage through declared pieces and laws; no implicit coefficients.
- [ ] Pumps, valves and equipment as volumes/boundary relations according to the level of detail; do not add energy or momentum twice.
- [ ] Multiple flows, branching, mixing and larger networks: extend the topology and solve it, not just remove the three-volume limit.

**Output:** a vented pot and a sealed vessel are told apart by their boundaries and equations. The model is not adjusted to obtain an expected pressure or temperature.

Background, still a design: [open boundary with a non-condensable gas](open-boundary-water-air-design.md). Its proposed package names and scope are not published capabilities or a universal decision.

**Demo 0.1 dynamic flash drum feasibility audit (Claude, 30 October 2026).** Target: an ethanol/water drum with a feed, a vapour outlet and a liquid outlet; UNIFAC phase equilibrium through the common layer; energy with heat duty; level linked to the liquid inventory and a hydraulic outflow law; fixed or vapour-controlled pressure. Probe only through the shared journals and `SharedAuthor` (the Model operations), executed with `DerivedSystem`/`resolve_system`.

- **Expressible and executed now (liquid region, single phase).** Scripts: `.tmp/claude-demo-flash/source_probe.py` and `liquid_drum_probe.py` (from the repository root, `PYTHONPATH="tests;."`). The material journal on a moving free surface gives `dm_i/dt = q_feed,i - q_liquid,i - q_vapour,i`. The energy journal (enthalpy, uniform state, uniform and prescribed pressure) gives `dH/dt = V dP/dt + sum_s B_s h_s q_s + Q`. 45 workbench operations then substitute: feed `q_feed,i = F z_i`; liquid outlet `q_liquid,i = W m_i/M` (well-mixed donor composition, valid only for one phase); vapour outlet `q_vapour,i = 0` (declared subcooled liquid); stream enthalpy `h = u_ref + c (T - T_ref) + P/rho` (declared incompressible liquid); `Q = heatDuty`; level `= V/A` (declared vertical cylinder); and a Torricelli orifice `W = rho C_d A_o (2 g level)^(1/2)` (constitutive, level > 0). The constant-density volume `V = M/rho` comes from the thermodynamic journal. `H(m, T)` is obtained by substituting the caloric inventory and the volume in `H = U + P V`. Data: rho 800 kg/m3, A 0.5 m2, C_d 0.6, A_o 1e-3 m2, F 1.5 kg/s, z 0.3, T_F 330 K, Q 50 kW, c 3000 J/(kg K), 300 kg at 300 K, 1500 s. Against an independent DOP853 integration of the same premises: RK45 |dm| <= 1.4e-10 kg and |dH| <= 2.3e-3 J (H about 3.8e7 J); BDF |dm| <= 1.6e-8 kg and |dH| <= 3.6e-2 J. T tends to T_F + Q/(F c) = 341.11 K and the level goes from 0.75 m towards the analytic 0.4979 m (0.4989 m at 1500 s). Transform is the identity selection; Solve and Results publish the states, observables and the total-mass budget. Negative check: leaving the vapour outlet unclosed blocks execution with `unsupported_expression q__vapour__1(t)`; nothing falls back to an earlier model.
- **FD-1, blocker (Codex: `core/compilador/derived.py`; Claude: closure API).** Derived execution rejects every volume whose `termodinamica.tipo` is not `supuesto` (`unsupported_closure`, `derived.py` `_initialize`). The thermodynamic graph already carries the package nodes (`package-potential`, `component-partition-i`, `phase-amount`, `phase-energy/enthalpy/volume`, `phase-equilibrium-i`, `phase-stability`), but nothing lowers them. Contract: admit a volume whose thermodynamic journal recorded `select_closure`. In every RHS evaluation, lower those nodes by calling the common closure: UV for a rigid drum, PH for a prescribed pressure. The closure returns T (or V), the phase amounts n_i^k, the fractions beta_k, the phase molar h/u/v and composition, and their sensitivities for BDF. Pin the result by the package fingerprint. The numerical service already exists in the compiled assembly path (`core/compilador/compilador.py`: closure per RHS, phase margins and `aparicion_fase` events); the gap is the lowering from the deduction graph. Claude API delivered: per RHS call `close_state` (UV or PH, seeded with the previous state) and, for BDF, `closure_sensitivities`; see the contract below.
- **FD-2, blocker (Codex: `core/balances/material_equations.py`, `energy_equations.py`, `core/modelo/equation_workbench.py`).** The deduction graph creates `q_{s,i}` and `h_s` for each stream with no phase identity. The stream `extraccion` rule (`{tipo: fase, fase: liquido|vapor}`) is read only by admission and the compiled assembly, never by the graph builders. Contract: a shared Model operation (relation kind `stream_donor`) for a stream s that leaves volume v and extracts phase k. It declares `q_{s,i} = F_s x_i^k` (with M_i on a mass basis) and `h_s = h^k`, referencing the package nodes `phase_composition_i(k,t)` and `phase_enthalpy(k,t)`. Its conditions are the donor sign (F_s >= 0, or the reversal donor) and the presence predicate beta_k > 0. It is recorded as a constitutive premise. Without it, the only expressible donor is the whole well-mixed inventory, which is wrong for a two-phase drum. Claude API delivered: the donor values are `phase_draw(state, k, F_s)` (`component_molar_flows` = `q_{s,i}`, `molar_enthalpy` = `h_s`, `present` = the predicate).
- **FD-3, blocker (Codex: `derived.py`, F3 runtime).** Derived execution emits only `derived_validity` stops. Contract: lower the `phase-stability` complementarity as event functions, beta_k -> 0 for disappearance and TPD_k -> 0 for appearance. At an event, switch the active phase set, re-solve the algebraic block, and report `aparicion_fase`/`desaparicion_fase` events in Results. A disappearing phase drives its outlet to zero flow through the FD-2 presence predicate, not through a stop. Reuse the compiled path margins. Claude API delivered: `phase_indicators(state)` returns one event function `g_k = beta_k - tm_k` per candidate phase.
- **FD-4, blocker (Codex with Claude; depends on FD-1).** With two phases, the level needs `V_L = n_L v_L(T, P, x)` from the package `phase_volume`. A rigid drum then has `V_L + V_V = V_drum`, which makes the pressure vapour-controlled: an index-1 DAE with the closure. Contract: lower `phase_volume`; accept the declared geometry relation; and let the version-4 BDF DAE take closure outputs as algebraic quantities. The current level and orifice path holds only for one constant-density liquid region. See [F6](#f6-momentum-and-hydraulic-network). Claude API delivered: `ClosureState.liquid_volume`, `liquid_molar_volume` and the per-phase `v`; with `spec='UV'` the closure itself returns the vapour-controlled P of a rigid drum.
- **Feed enthalpy from the package (= F5-C2).** Today a stream enthalpy is a declared closure with numeric constants. A package value `h_F(T_F, P_F, z_F)`, including a vapour or two-phase feed, needs the same caloric relation node as F5-C2. Claude API delivered: `phase_caloric` at the feed (T_F, P_F, z_F) for a known single phase, or `close_state` with the feed H for a two-phase feed.
- **Claude thermodynamic entry points for derived execution (delivered 30 October 2026, uncommitted).** Module `core/termodinamica/derived_bridge.py`; tests `tests/test_derived_thermo_bridge.py` (16 checks against direct `core.mezclas` calls and outside finite differences, including an ethanol/water two-phase case, a single-phase liquid and a water/1-butanol two-liquid case). The Claude side of FD-1 to FD-4 and F5-C2 is complete: **Codex only has to call these functions from derived execution**; no thermodynamics is written in `core/compilador/` or `core/modelo/`. Every function is pure (it builds its `Sistema` from `package, species, liquid_only`, keeps no module state and caches nothing); a seed is passed in by the caller. Packages: `unifac-original-public` and `unifac-dortmund-public` (aliases `unifac-original`, `unifac-dortmund`). SI units: K, Pa, mol, J, m3, kg, s. `anchor`: `None` (package reference, ideal gas h = s = 0 at 298.15 K and 1 bar), `'gas'` or `'liquid'` (the constant formation shift of `chemical_equilibrium.PackagePhase`); one model uses one anchor for every energy, and every result carries a `fingerprint` (package `huella` plus anchor and formation source) to pin the lowering. Functions:
  - `species_caloric(package, species, T, P, *, anchor, liquid_only, formation_source)` -> per-species lists `h_gas, u_gas, dh_gas_dT, du_gas_dT` (absent for liquid-only systems), `h_liquid, u_liquid, dh_liquid_dT, du_liquid_dT, v_liquid`, `shift` [J/mol, J/(mol K), m3/mol].
  - `phase_caloric(package, species, T, P, n, *, phase='liquid'|'vapor', anchor, ...)` -> `H, U, V` [J, J, m3], `dH_dT, dU_dT` at fixed P and n [J/K], partial molar `h_partial, u_partial, v_partial` (= dH/dn_i, dU/dn_i at fixed T, P). This is the F5-C2 caloric inventory node `U = sum_i n_i u_i(T, P, x)`.
  - `phase_temperature(package, species, n, P, target, *, quantity='U'|'H', phase, anchor, ..., check_stability=True)` -> the `phase_caloric` dict at the reconstructed T plus `residual`, `single_phase_stable` and `stability` (min tm of the other trials). Temperature reconstruction for a declared single-phase volume; `single_phase_stable = False` is a policy violation to report, not a value to integrate through.
  - `reaction_enthalpy(package, species, nu, T, P, *, phase, n=None, anchor='gas', ..., check_elements=True)` -> `dH, dU` per reaction [J/mol of extent], `h, u` used. With `n`, partial molar values in that mixture; without, the pure species of the phase. For reporting and checks only: a balance written with `phase_caloric` energies already contains the reaction heat and must not add `dH` as a source.
  - `close_state(package, species, n, spec, a, b, *, anchor, seed=None, ...)` -> frozen `ClosureState`: `specification, n, T, P, V, H, U, phases` (each `PhaseState(id, kind, amount, beta, x, h, u, v, M)`, present phases only, ids `vapor-1`/`liquido-1`/`liquido-2`), `liquid_volume, liquid_amount, liquid_molar_volume` (None without liquid), `certificate` (`checks` with residual and tolerance, `certified`, `min_tm`, `appearance_margin`), `fingerprint`, `phase_set`, and `native` (the next seed). `spec='UV'`: `(a, b) = (U, V)` for a rigid volume; `spec='PH'`: `(a, b) = (P, H)` for a prescribed pressure. Pass the previous step's state as `seed` for the package's seeded Newton (about 10 ms instead of about 1 s); it never fixes a property. Wraps `Sistema.equilibrio_UV`, `equilibrio_PH` and `flash_rapido`.
  - `closure_sensitivities(package, species, n, spec, a, b, *, relative_step=1e-6, ...)` -> `jacobian` of `outputs` (`T`, `P` for UV or `V` for PH, each present phase amount) with respect to `inputs` (`n[i]`, then `U, V` or `P, H`), central differences seeded with the base state; `phase_set_changed = True` marks columns that cross a phase boundary and must not be used (FD-1 BDF Jacobian).
  - `phase_indicators(state)` -> per candidate (`vapor-1`, `liquido-1`, `liquido-2` for two or more species): `present, beta, tm, incipient_x` and the event function `g_k = beta_k - tm_k` (> 0 present, < 0 absent, 0 at the boundary; None when no trial minimum of that kind exists, then skip it), plus `event_functions` (appearance direction +1, disappearance -1) and `complementarity` (FD-3). Signature since the repair: `phase_indicators(state, previous=None)`. Pass the previous step's result as `previous`, so that liquid labels follow composition continuity; `labels` maps each tracked label to the closure's `package_id`. A change of `phase_set` between accepted steps is also an event, because None does not prove that a phase cannot appear.
  - `phase_draw(state, phase, total_outflow, *, basis='molar'|'mass', selection=None)` -> `present, molar_flow, mass_flow, component_molar_flows, component_mass_flows, x, molar_enthalpy, enthalpy_flow` [mol/s, kg/s, J/mol, W]. `phase`: `vapor`, `liquid` or a phase id; with two liquids `liquid` needs `selection` `higher_density`, `lower_density` or `combined`. An absent phase returns zero flows with `present = False`: that is the FD-2 presence predicate, not a stop (FD-2). With `indicators=` (the `phase_indicators` result of the same state), a liquid id is a tracked label, so a stream keeps drawing the same physical liquid.
  - Errors: `DerivedThermoError(code, message, detail)` with `code` in `UNSUPPORTED_PACKAGE`, `INVALID_INPUT`, `PARAMETER_MISSING` (native code in `detail`), `LIQUID_ONLY_SYSTEM`, `OUT_OF_DOMAIN`, `NOT_CONVERGED`, `UNSUPPORTED_STATE` (native reason in `detail`), `FORMATION_DATA_MISSING`, `FORMATION_SOURCE_MISSING`, `ELEMENTS_NOT_CONSERVED`, `PHASE_AMBIGUOUS`. Derived execution maps `OUT_OF_DOMAIN`, `NOT_CONVERGED` and `UNSUPPORTED_STATE` to structured stops and the others to admission diagnostics.
  - **Independent review of `derived_bridge` (Claude, 30 October 2026): approved for Demo 0.1 (vapour-liquid and single-liquid states); two liquid-liquid event defects remain.** The review was read-only. Its scripts are in `.tmp/claude-bridge-review/`, and the 16 tests pass. Checks against direct `core.mezclas` calls and outside differences: partial molar `h_i` equals `-T^2 d(mu_i/T)/dT` within 3e-7 J/mol in the liquid and the vapour of a ternary with a 1e-9 trace. An absent species takes its dilute limit, `dU/dT` matches differences within 1e-9 J/K, and `h_i = dH/dn_i`. With the liquid anchor, the esterification Delta_r H of the pure liquids at 298.15 K and 1 bar is -4515 J/mol, equal to `sum nu Hfl`. The vapour Delta_r H follows Kirchhoff exactly. `close_state` reproduces `equilibrio_PH` to 1e-9 K across the liquid, two-phase and vapour states. A distant seed, or a seed with other amounts, gives the unseeded T within 4e-13 K. Calls are repeatable, and the module keeps no mutable state. Seeded closure takes about 9 ms against about 100 ms unseeded. The two-phase UV Jacobian matches an outside difference within 1e-8. Per-phase draws at the phase amounts reproduce n and H exactly, and every error path returns its declared code. Independent counter-check: `thermo` 0.6.1 (UNIFAC Dortmund DOUFIP2016, its own Psat, cp and Hvap, with its own modified-Raoult PH flash) gives the same gamma to five digits at the bridge state, K within 0.5 %, T 357.71 K against 357.78 K and beta_V 0.447 against 0.455. Defects (FD-3 scope; reproducer `review_lle_events.py`, water/1-butanol at 300 K): (D1) on the single-liquid side of a miscibility gap, `liquido-2` has `event = None` up to z = 0.0288, while the binodal is at 0.029. The package test returns only the trivial liquid minimum there, so the appearance of a second liquid is never a sign change, and the docstring claim "None: the phase cannot appear near this state" is false. (D2) Liquid ids are ordered by density, so when the dense `liquido-1` disappears (z 0.642 to 0.645), the remaining liquid is relabelled `liquido-1`. Its event then jumps from 0.0017 to 1, `liquido-2` jumps from 0.998 to None, and no event crosses zero. Inside the gap, `phase_temperature` flags the unstable single liquid correctly (`single_phase_stable = False`, min tm -0.06 to -0.20). Fix (Claude, before liquid-liquid events are used): track liquid identity against the seed state, and give absent liquid candidates a refined non-trivial trial minimum, or declare that liquid-liquid appearance is detected only by a change of `phase_set`. Minor: a phase that is present at a trace still takes the full `F_s` in `phase_draw`, so the outflow law must vanish with the inventory. Native error messages pass through in Spanish. `ClosureState` is frozen but not hashable, because `certificate` is a dict. **Status: D1 and D2 repaired and re-reviewed (Claude, 30 October 2026): final verdict approved.** D1: `phase_indicators` now refines the binary liquid minima that the package's test leaves unrefined (`_minimos_sin_refinar`) within their grid cell. `liquido-2` therefore has a margin on the single-liquid side, -0.0027 at z = 0.0288 to +0.0008 at z = 0.0295, which is a sign change. The docstring states that None does not prove a phase cannot appear, and that a `phase_set` change is also an event. D2: the optional `previous` argument tracks liquid identity by composition continuity. `liquido-1` goes from +0.0017 at z = 0.642 to -0.0021 at z = 0.645, while `liquido-2` stays the butanol-rich liquid at 1.0. `phase_draw(..., indicators=)` resolves tracked labels. Three regression tests built from `review_lle_events.py` are in `tests/test_derived_thermo_bridge.py` (19 tests pass). The minor items (draw at a trace inventory, Spanish native messages, hashability) are unchanged. Re-review (read-only; `review_lle_tracked.py`): the 19 tests pass, and the original reproducers give unchanged caloric, closure and draw results. With `previous`, water/1-butanol marches in both directions across both binodals show exactly one sign change of the relevant liquid event. The steps near each boundary are 0.002 or less, and labels follow composition: when the dense liquid appears again on a decreasing march, it takes `liquido-2`. An ethanol/water V-L march with tracking is unchanged. The `_minimos_sin_refinar` source is committed `core.mezclas`. Remaining, as documented: the refinement covers binary systems only, the margin is still None far from a gap (z = 0.02), and untracked labels are the closure's. Derived execution must therefore pass `previous` and also treat a `phase_set` change between accepted steps as an event.
- **Minor usability findings (not blockers).** (FD-6) Declared relations accept arithmetic and numeric powers only: `sqrt`, `exp` and `log` are refused (`core/mathematics/dimensions.py` `parse_expression`), so the orifice law had to be written `(2 g level)^(1/2)`. (FD-7) Initialization solves only the selected states from `initialEquations`; it does not chain non-state intermediates such as U and V, so a composed `H(m, T)` relation had to be authored. Both stay inside the shared tools and need no example-specific path.
- **FD-1 to FD-4, F5-C2 feed enthalpy, FD-6 and FD-7 delivered by Claude for the Codex queue (30 October 2026; uncommitted; independent review pending).** Recorded in `codex-queue.md` items 1 and 5 with the file list. A Thermodynamics journal `select_closure` now lowers in identity explicit derived execution: the thermodynamic graph instantiates the candidate vapour and liquid (`instantiate_candidate_phases` on the partition and property sums, presence, phase molar mass, liquid volume) and links `m_i = M_i n_i`; the closure outputs are evaluated by `derived_bridge` at each RHS and probe, the phase set is the shared signature event and the phase indicators its margins, and donors are constitutive relations on the instantiated quantities. Verification (`tests/test_derived_package_closure.py`): an ethanol/water drum at 1 atm (feed 0.5 kg/s at 330 K and 2 bar with package enthalpy, vent `F_V = k n^V`, orifice on the package liquid level, 200 kW) against a DOP853 integration that calls `core.mezclas.equilibrio_PH` directly: masses within 3.6e-7 kg, H within 3e-10 relative (RK45) and 2.7e-9 (BDF), T within 2.3e-6 K, the vapour appears once at t = 70.264 s in both methods, inside the bracket of the reference's first two-phase sample; the lowered rates equal `phase_draw`. A closed rigid vessel (UV) heated at 200 kW reproduces `equilibrio_UV` (P 0.445 to 3.08 bar within 2e-8 Pa) and stops with `closure_out_of_domain` at the package pressure limit. A declared single-liquid policy stops with `politica_incumplida` when the liquid becomes unstable. Limits: identity selection v1 without scaling, algebraic or DAE rows; one vapour and one liquid; single-phase inlets; package changes keep the Transform selection (same mathematics) but change the result signature through the fingerprint. Pending extensions recorded by the independent review: (RV-2) the compiled flash path rejects every liquid-only volume after building its `Sistema(..., solo_liquido=True)`, so on that path the species admission is shown only by the absence of `unsupported_package`, and fingerprint and invalidation do not apply; derived execution is the only path that runs it. (RV-3) a single-phase policy stop is raised inside the RHS, so it is placed at the failing integration step rather than bisected; single-phase closures publish no event margins. The original Phase 2 note follows. No Claude-owned fix was needed in the Phase 1 audit. Phase 2 starts when FD-1 to FD-4 are delivered: author the two-phase drum with UNIFAC Dortmund on the same journals, check it against an independent integration that calls `core.mezclas` directly, and save it in `frontend/testing/modelFixtures.ts`.
  - **Independent review of codex-queue items 1, 2 and 4 (Claude, 31 October 2026; read-only for code): approve with fixes; the fixes are documentation and test-resolution only, no engine code. Re-check the same day: RV-4 repaired; RV-1 open on its test half; RV-5 new and minor.** Files reviewed: `core/compilador/derived.py`, `derived_closure.py`, `admission.py`, `core/mathematics/dimensions.py`, `core/termodinamica/equations.py`, `derivation.py`, `core/balances/energy_derivation.py`, the five new test files, and the frontend keys that landed afterwards. (1) *Package thermodynamics on every evaluation.* `ClosureRuntime.rhs`, every compiled observable/condition that reads a closure output, the BDF finite-difference Jacobian (through `rhs`) and `event_state` all call `PackageClosure.evaluate`, which calls only `derived_bridge.close_state` or `phase_temperature`; its cache key is the exact `(t, y)` bytes, so no stale value is reused. No caloric constant is authored: molar masses, inlet `h, M, h/M, w_i` are bound as `source: 'package'` parameters, and a contradicting datum raises `inconsistent_molar_mass`/`package_value_bound`. PH is selected when the pressure is bound and UV when the volume is bound; both bound is rejected; single-phase volumes need the pressure. The seed only changes cost: the bridge's seeded path ends with the full stability test, and a failed seeded call retries unseeded. (2) *Draw and events, reproduced independently* (own DOP853 calling `core.mezclas.equilibrio_PH` directly, rtol 1e-10, appearance bisected on the dense output): the vapour appears at 70.264139 s; the engine locates `aparicion_fase` at 70.264141 s (RK45) and 70.264142 s (BDF). The rigid vessel, with U0 from an independent `equilibrio_TV(340 K, V)` route rather than the test's UV root, gives P = 0.445357 → 1.280428 → 3.081162 bar at 0, 50 and 100 s, matching the claim. `test_lowered_phase_donors_equal_the_bridge_phase_draw` compares the lowered RHS with `phase_draw` at the final two-phase state (rtol 1e-9). (3) *Item 4.* Probes of `parse_expression` with units: `sqrt(x)` records `x >= 0`; `log(x)` records `x > 0` (none for a positive symbol); `exp(L)` and `log(L)` with L in m are rejected; `sqrt(-1)`, `log(0)`, `log(-y)` are rejected; `sqrt(x^2)` gives `Abs(x)`, which is correct with no condition. A dimensional `sqrt` is allowed with a half-power unit, as the contract permits. The chained initialisation solves each relation left with one time-dependent unknown until no progress is made, and then falls back to the closure energy inventory. (4) *Derivation standard.* No owner, species, CAS or example name occurs in the four core files. Donors, level and orifice are ordinary `declare_relation` constitutive rows on the instantiated phase quantities, and the instantiation is a recorded `instantiate_candidate_phases` transform of the generic sums. *Findings.* **RV-3 (minor, record):** a single-phase policy stop is raised inside the RHS (`politica_incumplida` as `Detencion`), so it is placed at the failing step, not bisected; single-phase closures return no event margins. The two-phase signature events are located to about 3e-6 s. **RV-4 (minor, contract doc): repaired (re-check, 31 October 2026).** `docs/contracts/common-model.md` now documents both optional keys, their values (`boolean`; `null | 'liquido' | 'gas'`), the single-liquid policy rule, the three journal actions and the removal values. This matches `derivation.py` `OPTIONAL`/`changes`/`normalize` and `validate_model_journal`. **RV-5 (minor, new; contract against implementation):** the contract says that `soloLiquido: true` is admitted only with an equilibrium package. However, `start` followed by `liquid_only` replays in Python with `closure: None`, and the TypeScript `replayThermodynamics` checks the policy only when a closure exists. A later `select_closure` with a non-liquid policy is still rejected, and the UI disables the control without a package, so nothing executes wrongly. Either reject it in `normalize` when no closure is bound, or say in the contract that the rule is enforced once a package is selected. *Tests (31 October 2026):* the new tests, derived, kinetics, F3, M1 (U1, thermal, coupled), thermodynamic/energy journals, common admission, liquid-only, bridge and chemical equilibrium suites pass together: 216 passed in 66.5 min. Frontend: `verificar_physical_journals.mjs` (1,398 checks), `verificar_kinetic_reactions.mjs`, `verificar_affine_algebraic.mjs` and `npm run test:motor-fundamental` pass. Reproduction scripts are in the review session scratchpad (`repro_drum_vessel.py`, `repro_reactor.py`, `reactor_peak.py`); the F5 verdict is under F5.
