## F6. Momentum and hydraulic network

- [x] Start from momentum conservation and its recorded reductions in the shared Model worksheet.
- [x] Relate pressure, elevation, velocity, wall resistance and forces for admitted constant-density laminar circular paths; retain bulk inertia and explicit applicability limits.
- [ ] Density, viscosity and other properties with source and domain; avoid correlations outside their regime.
- [ ] Pumps, valves and shaft work with a single sign convention.
- [ ] Dissipation and mechanical work consistent with energy, without double counting.
- [ ] Initialize and solve flows and pressures simultaneously; detect incompatible constraints.
- [x] Define the single-component, single-phase, externally isothermal reference-medium scope before extending to slip or two-phase hydraulics.
- [x] Execute prescribed differentiable flow/pressure recovery and dynamic flow with hydrostatic storage inventories through shared RK45/BDF and Results; verify analytical pressure, independent matrix exponential, reversed flow and perturbed rest.
- [ ] Extend beyond this reduced subset to general constrained networks and thermodynamically coupled energy/rotor dynamics.

**Output:** switching momentum on brings in real equations and properties. Switching it off keeps the declared flow closures and their limitations.

**Flash drum level (30 October 2026).** A level from a constant-density liquid volume and a declared orifice law `W = rho C_d A_o (2 g level)^(1/2)` run through the shared operations on RK45 and BDF. A two-phase level and vapour-controlled pressure need FD-4. See the [F4 flash drum audit](#f4-boundaries-and-real-equipment).

**Outlet flow from the momentum balance for package volumes (demo-02-momentum, Claude, 1 November 2026; uncommitted, review pending).** Derived execution now admits active momentum without a constant-medium `hydraulicNetwork` definition when each momentum volume uses a reduced journal: a straight hydraulic path whose `path.pressure-form` balance is in the ancestry of a selected differential row, or a vertical-gravity storage with an integrated pressure reference (`DerivedSystem._reduced_momentum_admission`). Density, port pressures and losses are recorded relations of the selected graph, not network data, and an operation that holds only through the recorded closure definitions (phase molar mass, liquid volume) is accepted as a consequence (`_closure_identity`). Verified case (`tests/test_derived_momentum_outlet.py`, author `tests/derived_momentum_inputs.py`): the Demo 0.1 ethanol/water package drum plus a horizontal path. Through shared operations, the drum's hydrostatic storage pressure is referenced to the liquid free surface `z_r = z_0 + V_L/A` and to the vapour-space pressure `P`, with the liquid density `M_L/v_L` from the closure; the path density is the donor liquid density; the sum of the two liquid donors `q_i = F x_i^L M_i / M_L` gives `q_1 + q_2 = F` through `M_L = sum x_i M_i`; and the path balance becomes `dF/dt = (A/L)[P + rho_L g V_L/A_d - P_out - K F^2/(2 rho_L A^2)]`, with a declared quadratic loss and the forward-flow condition `F > 0`. Against an independent DOP853 integration calling `core.mezclas.equilibrio_PH`: masses within 3.5e-9 kg (RK45) and 1.7e-8 kg (BDF), F within 6e-9 kg/s, H within 4e-11 and 1.8e-9 relative, T within 1e-6 K and floor pressure within 1e-9 relative; the vapour appears once at 118.409 s in both methods, and F rises from 0.400 to a peak of 0.482 kg/s near 120 s, at the vapour appearance, and ends at 0.444 kg/s (corrected 1 November, review RM-4). Open findings: (M-1) the path is isothermal with matter and energy off (no path holdup), so the drum's liquid enthalpy leaves directly; a path with its own inventory needs its energy journal. (M-2) Reverse flow is a stop through `F > 0`; a signed `|F| F` loss with donor switching is pending. (M-3) Authoring the case takes about six minutes, because each appended operation replays the whole history (F9 performance). (M-4) Resolved on the board the same day: `demo-02-momentum` and `demo-02-steady` no longer depend on `demo-02-contract`, because their physics does not use the typed ports; `demo-02-drum` now depends on all three.

## F7. Transport and spatial resolution

- [ ] Couple the current 1D matter transport with energy and local properties.
- [ ] Separate local equilibrium from finite interphase transfer: each needs different equations.
- [ ] Diffusion/convection/conduction, explicit initial and boundary conditions.
- [ ] Selective transfer by chemical potential or another declared law, with consistent transported energy.
- [ ] Interface, wall and phase with their own storage only when the model represents them.
- [ ] Conservative mesh, spatial/temporal convergence, positivity and the second law where applicable.
- [ ] Additional geometries and dimensions as later extensions; not implemented during UNIFAC.

**Priority output:** time and 1D + time. 2D/3D stay as future branches, without delaying the validation of one volume or one dimension.

**Readiness without execution (user decision, 5 November 2026, `dec-nonspatial-100-pde-ready`).** The target is a complete nonspatial engine, with spatial execution still deferred. The deduction kernel must nevertheless be able to deduce and publish PDE models that keep their spatial terms: local conservation with divergence and gradient operators, constitutive fluxes, and initial and boundary conditions on a declared domain. A model whose terms still need a mesh is published as such and handed to Transform as requiring discretization; it is rejected by Solve with a located reason, never silently reduced. Meshes, discretization and PDE solvers remain F7 work for later. A readiness audit of the current kernel against this requirement comes first.

**Readiness audit result (claude-b, read-only, 5 November 2026).** Partially ready. Present: typed local laws for matter, energy and momentum; the matter field view keeps the local form, applies a recorded Fick substitution and expands divergence and gradient in orthogonal coordinates (probe: dρ_A/dt = D_A d²ρ_A/dx² deduced through recorded operations); Gauss/Reynolds operations; space-derivative dimensions; the nondimensional `matematico` v2 document already carries coordinates, spatial operators, domains and condition kinds. Missing: a typed spatial domain with oriented boundary parts; boundary trace, normal derivative and typed initial/Dirichlet/Neumann/Robin/continuity conditions in the equation graph; a family-generic operator expansion and a Fourier closure; a 'requires discretization' publication and Transform handoff; and a located Solve rejection (today `unsupported_state` without equationId). Physical finding P-1: the Laplacian premise for a binary implies D_A = D_B and a constant total density, which the graph does not enforce. Ordered increments (milestone `pde-ready`): PDE-0 located `requires_discretization` rejection; PDE-1 typed spatial domain; PDE-2 trace, normal derivative and typed conditions (boundary conditions deduced by restricting a recorded constitutive relation to a boundary part); PDE-3 generic operator expansion and Fourier closure; PDE-4 publication block and graph-to-`matematico` adapter; PDE-5 Transform display; PDE-6 pointwise thermodynamic contract by homogeneity; PDE-7 acceptance with Fick, Fourier and Stokes first problem against analytic solutions. Non-goals: meshes, discretization, PDE solvers, new geometry.

**PDE-0 and PDE-1 committed (claude-b, 6 November 2026; reviewed: approve with low repairs, P1-P3 then repaired).** Solve rejects spatial rows with the located `requires_discretization` diagnostic (equationId and coordinates) in the derived, steady and explicit paths; coordinates are recognised only from declared chart axes or the coordinate role, so scalar arguments such as h(T) are not misreported (P1). `declare_spatial_domain` adds a typed spatial domain (chart, active coordinates, intervals with units, oriented boundary parts) as an additive workbench rule; volume integrals bind the declared coordinates only when a domain exists, and the signatures of all 18 fixture models are unchanged. The v2 compatibility layer maps the new code to `no_soportado` (P3). Next: PDE-2 (boundary trace, normal derivative and typed conditions), PDE-3 in parallel, then PDE-4 to PDE-7.

## F8. Thermodynamic extensions and diagrams

| Branch | Pending work | Closing requirement |
|---|---|---|
| Mixture EOS | Non-ideal vapor, compressible liquid, mixing rules and parameters | Properties and equilibrium derived from the same potential; roots and stability cross-checked |
| High pressure / critical | Suitable domains and methods, hard derivatives and continuity | Do not extrapolate the ideal gas or a low-pressure activity model |
| Solids | Solid data, melting, polymorphs, SLE and ice dynamics | Consistent equilibrium and latent energy; no invented three-phase split |
| LL / LLV | Complete plotting of branches and multiphase meetings | Certification of all the package's competing phases |
| Continuation | Folds, arc length and bifurcations | Explicit gaps, convergence and a measured budget |
| N components | Ternary/multicomponent slices and surfaces | The slice is specified and every component is kept |
| Special data | Electrolytes, association, polymers or other systems | A specific package and its own data; conventional UNIFAC is not presented as universal |

Diagrams are slices of a model, not new physical laws or calculations that govern the trajectory. Two different packages are not compared as if they had the same fingerprint.

**F8 LL/LLV and continuation, first delivery (Claude, 23 October 2026).** Binary T–xy/P–xy responses gain the optional `liquidoLiquido` block: L–L branches by pseudo-arclength continuation from certified-flash seeds, the L–L critical point where the binodal folds (h' = h'' = 0) and the certified L–L–V three-phase point (`core/coexistencia_ll.py`; [workspace §6b](equilibrium-diagrams.md#6b-liquidliquid-and-three-phase-points-f8-23-october-2026)). Evidence: analytic Margules binodal and critical point, the package's TP flash, the L–V tracer's meeting and Txy/Pxy consistency (`tests/test_diagram_liquid_liquid.py`); UNIFAC and NRTL probes certified in 0.8–4.2 s. Second delivery, the same day: ternary L–L at fixed T and P (`core/ternary_liquid_liquid.py`: tie lines by pseudo-arclength, plait point, binary-edge ends and the L–L–V triangle) and multicomponent composition-line slices (`core/composition_slices.py`), checked against an analytic Margules plait point, the TP flash and the binary branch ([§6c](equilibrium-diagrams.md#6c-ternary-liquidliquid-and-multicomponent-line-slices-f8-23-october-2026)); near a plait point the TP flash may publish one liquid within its tm band (recorded finding). Pending in F8: an HTTP route and contract for the ternary and slice results and their drawing (D4, Codex), arc length for L–V branches, three-liquid ternary regions, SLE, mixture EOS and the critical region.

**F8 D4 binary diagram view (Codex, 23 October 2026).** The existing reader/view now accepts and validates the optional `liquidoLiquido` block, draws two separate liquid series per branch without bridging branch ends, and marks certified critical and L–L–V compositions. Axis changes and display units use published compositions and conditions. Certificates, ending incidences, JSON/cache and explicit phase-labelled CSV export retain the same backend data; the legacy L–V CSV adapter default stays compatible. `node frontend/verificar_liquido_liquido.mjs`: 504 checks against actual Txy/Pxy publications and the analytic Margules critical point. The existing diagram regression passes 2,453 checks; TypeScript and scoped lint pass. No Claude thermodynamics file changes. Broader F8 work, including ternary/slice HTTP contracts and their view, remains separate.

**F8 D4 binary view, independent verification (Claude, 24 October 2026): accepted.** Real backend publications (`motor_diagramas.trazar`, the HTTP route body; no server) were collected for NRTL-LLE water/n-butanol (Txy 1 atm and 3 bar, Pxy 360 K) and UNIFAC-LLE (Txy 1 atm and 3 bar), plus the backend L–L tracer on the analytic Margules liquid (a = 660 K), the only source with a certified L–L critical point because both real packages end their branches at declared domain limits (283–313 K, 293–373 K) with explicit out-of-domain diagnostics. The real `GraficaCorteEquilibrio` and reader were transpiled, bundled with Next's own webpack and rendered in headless Edge (`--dump-dom`, one run per cut and axis; no dev server, no screenshot): `.tmp/claude-f8-d4-review/build.mjs`, 102 checks, 0 failures (`browser-report.json`). For both component axes every published pair point is drawn exactly once in each of the two liquid series; each series has one connecting path whose vertices are its own points in backend order, so no branch end is bridged; the critical star and the three L–L–V triangles are present exactly when certified; and all marks are an affine image of the published (composition, T or P) values with residual <= 6.2e-13 px. The drawing modules contain no thermodynamic computation; the client only selects the published composition and converts display units. Codex's `verificar_liquido_liquido.mjs` passes 504 checks. Server rendering cannot verify the chart, because Recharts 3 registers series in effects. The ternary extension was not started.

**F8 ternary and composition-line routes, contract and view (Claude, 24 October 2026; user-authorized).** `backend/multicomponent_diagrams.py` publishes the ternary L–L diagram and the multicomponent composition-line slice (own protocol revision 1, three additive routes, English contract section in `contratoDiagramasEquilibrio.ts`; the binary protocol is unchanged). Results' equilibrium view opens `MulticomponentEquilibrium.tsx` for volumes with three or more components: SVG tie lines, separate binodal sides, certified plait point, binary-edge pair, L–L–V triangle and line boundaries, all from the response. Evidence: `tests/test_multicomponent_diagram_route.py` (14 tests: analytic Margules equal activity, plait point and edge; UNIFAC benzene–water–ethanol equal x γ from the liquid model, TP flash at tie lines and in the triangle, binary branch at the edge, line boundaries at tie-line ends; 17 structured rejections) and `frontend/verify_multicomponent_diagrams.mjs` (1,079 checks on actual publications and a server render of the real charts); [§6d](equilibrium-diagrams.md#6d-ternary-and-composition-line-routes-contract-and-view-f8-24-october-2026). Not exercised against a live backend in a browser. Pending in F8: arc length for L–V branches, three-liquid ternary regions, SLE, mixture EOS and the critical region.

**F8 multicomponent view, live-backend browser exercise (Claude, 27 October 2026): accepted.** Own backend (8014) and frontend (3014) in the built-in browser. A benzene–water–ethanol original-UNIFAC volume (0.3/0.5/0.2 mol, 298.15 K, 1 atm, 100 W for 20 s) was built with the app's network-derivation helpers, seeded only into that isolated origin, and solved on the live v2 route (certified, two liquids, 45.015 °C at 20 s). A page script compared the raw HTTP bodies with every SVG mark (1e-9 px): at the sample condition, 173 certified tie lines, plait point and binary edge (534 checks); composition lines with no boundary (169) and with an L+L → L boundary at l = 0.6629 (141); at a prescribed 67 °C / 1.01325 bar (converted by the backend to 340.15 K / 101325 Pa), 149 tie lines, binary edge and the certified L–L–V triangle (460), and a line with an L+V → L boundary at l = 0.7899 overlaid on the ternary (147). 400 °C and an unnormalized line end were sent unchanged and rendered as structured `unsupported`/`invalid` diagnostics; the client only validates and places published compositions. Two view fixes: the line-end fieldsets overlapped at about 800 px (`minWidth: 0`), and the charts now state their basis (mole fractions and phase fraction in mol/mol); `frontend/verify_multicomponent_diagrams.mjs` still passes 1,079 checks. The Spanish visible text of `EquilibrioHoja.tsx` (sample slider, both-slices and update buttons, notices, accessible names and download names) was then translated to English with persisted keys and values unchanged (type check clean, view re-checked live); its binary both-slices button stays visible but disabled for three-component volumes. Evidence: `.tmp/claude-f8-live/`.


**F8 closed at its delivered scope (user decision, 29 October 2026).** F8 is done with the binary L-L/LLV and critical-point tracing, the binary D4 view and the ternary L-L/composition-line routes and Results view recorded above. The remaining extensions (L-V arc-length branches, three-liquid ternary regions, SLE, mixture EOS and the critical region) move to the planned milestone `f8b`, owned by Claude, on the project board.

**F8b L–V arc-length branches, first extension (Claude, 30 October 2026).** `core/termodinamica/lv_continuation.py` traces L–V coexistence by pseudo-arclength continuation through the package interface only (new `Sistema.lv_ln_k`: ln K_i from the same potentials as `mu`, finite at infinite dilution). Unknowns (x, ln K, ln T, ln P) with equal-mu, both normalizations and the slice specification: binary isobaric/isothermal branches from one pure end to the other, and N-species bubble/dew envelopes in (T, P) at fixed z up to the package domain limit. Tangent predictor, Newton corrector with the arc-length constraint, step control by iterations and tangent angle, exact pure-component and domain ends (the crossed bound becomes the closing equation), T/P turning points located by a secant on the step, and a per-point certificate (equal mu, normalization, phase distinction, tangent-plane stability) with bisected stable/unstable boundaries. Evidence, `tests/test_lv_arclength_continuation.py` (9 tests, about 25 s), worst errors: ideal benzene/toluene T–xy against the analytic Raoult–Poynting curve 2.3e-13 K and 2.1e-15 in y; ideal ternary bubble/dew pressures 2.5e-14 relative; UNIFAC ethanol–water against the existing bubble solver seeded by the TP flash 3.4e-13 K, 3.3e-16 in y; its turning point against the x = y azeotrope solver 0 K and 6.5e-9 in x, and 78.04 °C, x = 0.8961 against the literature 78.15 °C, 0.894; plain stepping in T stops at the fold (x = 0.89606) while the continuation reaches pure ethanol; UNIFAC ethanol–benzene P–xy maximum-pressure azeotrope 2.8e-14 relative in P, 4.2e-9 in x; UNIFAC water–1-butanol L–V branch changes stability on both sides of the L–L–V point within 3.3e-8 K and 2.2e-10/5.6e-9 in x of `coexistencia_ll.three_phase_point`; UNIFAC acetone–methanol–water bubble/dew envelopes bracketed by the package TP flash at ±1e-4 P (incipient phase within 8.1e-5). Traces take 0.15–3.6 s. Findings, not implemented: (1) exposure needs an HTTP route/contract revision and a Results view (T–xy/P–xy branches by arc length and (T, P) envelopes; `backend/`, the diagram contract and `frontend/` are Codex-owned), and the existing x-stepping tracer `core/diagramas_equilibrio.py` could adopt it; (2) turning-point compositions are limited to about 1e-8 by the finite-difference tangent (T and P to 1e-13); (3) envelope folds (cricondenbar, cricondentherm) and the critical region are not reachable with ideal-vapor packages limited to 5 bar and wait for the mixture EOS item; (4) unstable stretches are traced and published as unstable, not replaced by the L–L–V construction. **Independent review (Codex, 30 October 2026): findings, not accepted yet.** The predictor/corrector and pseudo-arclength hyperplane are correct; publication recomputes mu, normalization, phase distinction and stability. All 9 delivered tests pass (32 s). Independent probe `python .tmp/codex-f8b-review/probe.py` (results in `results.json` there): 53 ideal-curve points agree within 2.3e-13 K / 3.7e-15 in y; a separate chemical-potential root at x = y gives the UNIFAC ethanol-water azeotrope 351.1874260534 K, x = 0.8961254198 (continuation errors 5.7e-14 K / 6.5e-9). `lv_ln_k` matches mu within 5.0e-15 RT and stays finite at exact zero and 1e-300 mole fraction for ideal and UNIFAC packages; all 83 ethanol-water certificates pass fresh recomputation, and injected instability changes the verdict. Repairs: (1) `trace` closes a crossed bound and exits before angle/turning/stability checks (lines 313-328). An algorithm-only adapter with exact T = 350 + (x_0 - 0.001)^2, explicitly stubbed certification, crosses its minimum between the last two points (opposite temperature tangents) yet returns zero turning points; process terminal segments through event checks before accepting the end. (2) `certify` intersects positive supports and renormalizes (lines 178-182), hiding unequal compositions: ideal benzene/toluene at pure-benzene saturation, x = [1, 0], y = [0.5, 0.5], is certified although the actual benzene mu gap is ln(2) RT. This is a direct helper counterexample, not an observed physical trace; reject mismatched support before certification. Source/tests and the concurrent three-liquid work were not edited. **Repaired, re-review pending (Claude, 30 October 2026).** (1) Every accepted step, the closing step to a pure or domain end included, now runs the same events: sign change of d ln T/ds or d ln P/ds located on the step by the secant (or published as an unlocated bracket with `located: false`) and bisection of certificate-state changes; the closing step uses the arc-length step that lands on the end. Codex's endpoint-fold adapter now reports one located minimum at x_0 = 0.0010000544, T = 350.0000000000002 K. (2) `certify` compares mu over the full species set: a species present in one phase and absent from the other fails a new `common_support` residual and the point is `failed` (no trimming or renormalization); species absent from both are the exact subsystem. The x = [1, 0], y = [0.5, 0.5] case is rejected and the true pure end x = y = [1, 0] is still certified. Regressions built from the probe cases (`ReviewRegressions` in `tests/test_lv_arclength_continuation.py`; the probe's results.json records both failing on the first delivery): 11 tests pass, together with the three-liquid suite (18 tests, 101 s). **Final re-review (Codex, 30 October 2026): both reported repairs accepted.** Reran the unchanged probe first: its old missing-fold assertion now fails because the turning point is present (`re-review-original.log`); preserved the original probe/results as `probe-before-repair.py` and `results-before-repair.json`. Updated only the scratch probe's expectations, retaining its independent equations and adding a terminal stability-transition control: `python .tmp/codex-f8b-review/probe.py` passes, locating the minimum at x = 0.00100005438, T = 350.00000000000017 K; the terminal certificate transition is bracketed within 2.66e-8 arc units. Mismatched support is now `failed` with `common_support`, while the true pure end remains certified. The ideal and independent mu-root azeotrope comparisons, finite-dilution ln K checks and 83 fresh certificates reproduce the earlier errors. `python tests/test_lv_arclength_continuation.py` passes all 11 tests (32.55 s; `re-review-tests.log`). Acceptance covers these repairs and tested branches; endpoint sign-change detection is not a proof that arbitrary multiple unresolved folds cannot lie inside one step. No production/test or three-liquid file was edited; registries unchanged.

**F8b three-liquid ternary regions, second extension (Claude, 30 October 2026).** `core/termodinamica/three_liquid.py` extends the shared ternary layer (`core/ternary_liquid_liquid.py` coordinates, Newton and certificate) and the package's TP flash and stability test. At fixed T and P the L–L–L triangle (an invariant by the phase rule) is solved from the six mu equalities and certified; `region` seeds it from TP flashes that publish three liquids and from the two-liquid branches whose end is a liquid competitor, and reports, per side, the branch end lying on it; `phase_amounts` is the lever rule. `sweep_temperature` follows the triangle at fixed P and locates its end: a four-phase invariant where a vapor (L–L–L–V) or a fourth liquid (L–L–L–L) lowers the tangent plane, solved exactly from nine mu equalities, or a critical end point published as a bracket (two liquids merging). Evidence, `tests/test_three_liquid_ternary.py` (7 tests, about 45 s): the catalog has no species forming three liquids, so exact references use a ternary Margules test liquid on catalog ideal-vapor data. Symmetric triangle against the analytic root 9.1e-15; TP flash at interior feeds equal to the vertices within 9.4e-15 and amounts equal to the lever rule within 7.1e-15 mol (mass balance; every seed gives one triangle); flash 2e-3 outside each side gives two liquids within 2.5e-3 of that side's vertices, and the binary-edge branches end on the sides within 3.1e-7; L–L–L–L invariant against the analytic a/(4 ln 2), p = 2/3 and the centroid liquid (agreement to 1e-10 K); L–L–L–V point against the bubble temperature of a vertex from species data (agreement to 1e-8 K, vapor within 1e-7); an asymmetric case gives one triangle, flash within 8.9e-16 and a critical end point bracket (gap 7.4e-4) above which the flash shows two liquids; UNIFAC-LLE water/benzene/toluene at 25 °C has no three-liquid region, as in the literature. Findings, not implemented: (1) a real-system reference (for example water/aniline/n-hexane) needs pure-component data for species outside the catalog (`core/catalogo`, not Claude-owned); (2) the critical end point is bracketed, not solved from a critical condition; (3) `core/ternary_liquid_liquid.trace` still ends a branch at a third liquid as `inestable` and its HTTP route and Results view do not publish triangles or four-phase points (contract and view, Codex). Next in f8b: SLE, then mixture EOS and the critical region. **Independent three-liquid review (Codex, 30 October 2026): findings; not accepted yet.** All 7 tests pass (86.85 s). The implementation reuses common ternary Newton/certification and package flash/stability; fresh certificates compare all species. Independent Margules reference (a = 900 K, T = 310 K, P = 120 kPa): triangle error 4.5e-16, flash/mass errors <= 1.7e-15, rank-six fixed-T/P coexistence Jacobian, four-liquid invariant error 5.7e-14 K. All 16 ordinary sweep points pass fresh checks; outside-side L-L flashes converge to the vertices, with traced branch ends within 8.1e-7. Reproducer: `python .tmp/codex-f8b-review/three-liquid-probe.py`; measurements in `three-liquid-review-results.json` there. **LLL-1:** `sweep_temperature` (lines 156-163) publishes its supplied seed/cached certificate without recertification, on zero-length and nonzero sweeps. An altered normalized seed remains `cumple` despite fresh mu mismatch 0.01072 RT (tolerance 1e-10); invalid normalization and duplicate phases also retain `cumple`, and the zero-length sweep makes zero stability calls. Recompute every starting-point check against current package/T/P before publication. **LLL-2:** direct `triangle` (lines 54-60) returns `cumple` at 310 K, 1e6 Pa although the package rejects P > 5e5 Pa; enforce the shared domain gate before solving/certifying. These are public-helper counterexamples; ordinary region/reference cases pass. Production/tests and registries are unchanged. **Final three-liquid re-review (Codex, 30 October 2026): LLL-1 and LLL-2 accepted.** Reran `python .tmp/codex-f8b-review/three-liquid-probe.py` after updating only its obsolete scratch assertions to expect rejection; the independent Margules, finite-difference potential, flash/lever-rule, branch-end, phase-rule and invariant references are unchanged. Original scratch evidence is preserved in `three-liquid-probe-before-repair.py` and `three-liquid-review-results-before-repair.json`; new measurements/logs are in `three-liquid-review-results.json`, `three-liquid-re-review-probe.log` and `three-liquid-re-review-tests.log` in that directory. Altered-mu seeds (fresh mismatch 0.0107201921 RT against 1e-10), invalid normalization and duplicate liquids now return a fresh `no_cumple` certificate with `seed_not_certified` on zero-length and nonzero sweeps; the supplied cached certificate remains unchanged. Injected instability is evaluated once and rejected, while a valid seed receives a distinct fresh passing certificate. Both direct `triangle` and the package reject 1e6 Pa; the sweep returns no points and `domain_limit`. The independent ordinary-point/invariant references and all 16 fresh sweep certificates still pass. `python tests/test_three_liquid_ternary.py -v` passes all 9 tests (70.549 s). Acceptance covers the reported repairs and tested helper/continuation scope; the real-system reference, solved critical-end condition and HTTP/Results exposure remain pending as stated above. No production/test source or registry was changed.

**F8b solid-liquid equilibrium, third extension (Claude, 5 November 2026).** `core/termodinamica/solid_liquid.py` and `core/termodinamica/solid_data.py`. Derivation: a pure solid i (declared polymorph) coexists with a liquid of composition x when mu_i^S(T, P) = mu_i^L(T, P, x). The liquid side is the package potential g_i^L + RT ln(x_i gamma_i) (pure-liquid reference); the solid reference is the same pure liquid at (T, P), g_i^S = g_i^L - dg_fus,i, with dg_fus integrated (not posed) from the melting condition dg_fus(T_m, P_m) = 0, Gibbs-Helmholtz and Kirchhoff with dcp and dv declared constant on the record's window [T_min, T_m]. g_i^L cancels, so the pure liquid is never evaluated below the package's own liquid domain: ln(x_i gamma_i) = -dg_fus,i/RT, and tm_S,i = -ln(x_i gamma_i) - dg_fus,i/RT is the solid's tangent-plane distance on the layer's tm scale. Latent energy: L_i = hbar_i^L - h_i^S = dh_fus,i(T) + hbar_i^E. Operations: solid tm, liquidus and crystallization temperatures (N species, the first solid on cooling), binary solubility, simple eutectic and liquidus curves, each point with a fresh certificate (declared solids saturated, every other registered solid not supersaturated, window, normalization); `from_sistema` (the common package interface) or `from_unifac` (species with DDBST groups outside the catalog). Solid records: benzene (crystal I; T_m 278.69 K, dh_m 9.8663 kJ/mol, Oliver et al. 1948; dcp 13.0 J/(mol K) from NIST WebBook cp_L and cp_S near T_m) and cyclohexane (plastic crystal I, 186.1-279.82 K, dh_m 2.6769 kJ/mol, Ruehrwein and Huffman 1943; dcp declared 0, no solid cp listed); below T_min the state is rejected. Evidence, `tests/test_solid_liquid_equilibrium.py` (11 tests, about 2 s): ideal solubility against the van 't Hoff closed form 2.7e-15 relative and, with dcp, against a quadrature of Gibbs-Helmholtz from T_m 1.1e-15 in ln x; ideal eutectic by plain bisection on the closed forms 1e-9 K; liquidus through `from_sistema` on `mezcla-ideal` against the inverse closed form 1e-9 K; benzene + cyclohexane against Tsuji, Sue, Hiaki and Itoh, Fluid Phase Equilib. 257 (2007) 183-189 (NIST ThermoML, 12 liquidus points and the eutectic x_benzene = 0.2709, 232.11 K): UNIFAC Dortmund benzene branch within 1.03 K, cyclohexane branch within 3.56 K, eutectic 231.05 K and x = 0.2566; UNIFAC original within 5.7 K, eutectic 224.37 K, x = 0.2751; ideal solution 8.7 K off on the cyclohexane branch (eutectic 214.42 K); latent energy: Gibbs-Helmholtz and Kirchhoff on both records by finite differences (1e-7), the package's pure benzene liquid at T_m gives h_L - h_S = dh_m and equal g, L_i against dh_fus plus hbar_i^E from the liquid's h^E = g^E - T dg^E/dT (complex step) 9.9e-10, and the liquidus consistency (d ln a_i/dx_i)_T dx_i/dT = L_i/RT^2 from independently solved liquidus points 1.1e-9; ternary benzene/cyclohexane/toluene crystallization temperatures with every other solid tm > 0. Findings, not implemented: (1) solids are not yet candidates of `capa.candidatas` or the dynamic compiler (`core/compilador/`, Codex), and there is no SLE route, contract or Results view; (2) cyclohexane and further solid-forming species are not in the pure-component catalog (`core/catalogo.py`, not Claude-owned), so the real-system test uses the UNIFAC liquid alone, which is all the SLE condition needs; (3) solid solutions, compounds, polymorph transitions, the liquid's own L-L split along the liquidus and a pressure-dependent melting point (dv = 0 declared) are not modelled; (4) UNIFAC Dortmund parameters were fitted with SLE data that may include this system, so the Dortmund comparison is a consistency check rather than a prediction. Next in f8b: mixture EOS and the critical region.

**F8b mixture equation of state and critical region, fourth extension (Claude, 5 November 2026).** `core/termodinamica/cubic_eos.py`, `cubic_flash.py` and `cubic_critical.py`; layer package `peng-robinson-vdw1f` (`core/termodinamica/capa.py`, candidates vapor-1 and liquido-1). Scope choice against the layer contract: one residual Helmholtz function serves both phases, so the layer's fixed candidates, tm scale and complementarity apply unchanged, and it is the only route past the gamma-phi packages' declared domain (ideal vapour P <= 5e5 Pa, liquid T < 0.9 T_c) into the critical region. Peng-Robinson with van der Waals one-fluid mixing is the minimal published EOS that reproduces each pure critical point from catalog data alone: T_c and P_c of the species' Wagner record and omega from its definition on that same record (benzene 0.2094, toluene 0.2639, water 0.3436, ethanol 0.6433); Robinson-Peng 1978 alpha; k_ij = 0 through the layer. NRTL is not extended. Derivation: F = A^r/RT(T, V, n) is the only constitutive function; Omega_a, Omega_b and Z_c are solved from (dP/dV)_T = (d2P/dV2)_T = 0; P, ln phi_i = dF/dn_i - ln Z, h^r = -RT^2 F_T + PV - nRT and the (T, P) composition derivative follow by the chain rule (Michelsen and Mollerup ch. 3), with the (T, V) Hessian by complex step of the analytic gradient; energies use the catalog's cp_ig and the shared ideal-gas reference. Operations: tangent-plane stability on the layer's tm scale (Wilson, pure and binary-scan seeds, successive substitution then BFGS), certified TP flash (Rachford-Rice then Newton on G with the analytic Hessian; fugacity equality <= 1e-10, fresh stability of the split), PH and UV flashes, pure saturation, bubble and dew points and curves by continuation into the critical region, and mixture gas-liquid critical points by the Heidemann-Khalil criterion (Michelsen's algorithm, every critical point on the reduced-volume scan). Evidence, `tests/test_cubic_eos_mixture.py` (12 tests, about 50 s): Omega_a, Omega_b, Z_c against Peng and Robinson 1976; chain-rule derivatives against a complex step of F 1.2e-15; ln phi against the closed form 8.9e-15 and `thermo` PRMIX 9.7e-15; Gibbs-Duhem 1.6e-15, symmetry 2.7e-15, d ln phi/dn against finite differences 3.2e-9, d(g^r/RT)/dP = (Z - 1)/P and d(g^r/RT)/dT = -h^r/RT^2 within 1e-9; pure limits: the critical solver returns the catalog T_c, P_c (1e-10), water saturation against IAPWS-95 within 1.5 % at 450-640 K (15 % at 300 K, the known PR low-temperature error) and T_c within 0.04 %, benzene and toluene saturation against their Wagner records to 0.99 T_c (4.8 MPa) within 2.1 %; benzene + toluene bubble pressures at 333.15 and 353.15 K against Lee, Chen, Lee, Chang and Ko, Fluid Phase Equilib. 280 (2009) 42-48 (NIST ThermoML) within 3.7 % (k_ij = 0); benzene + toluene critical locus (x_benzene 0.25, 0.5, 0.75: 585.27 K 4.316 MPa, 578.19 K 4.521 MPa, 570.51 K 4.717 MPa) against an independent route, the binary determinant criteria in molar Helmholtz (v, x) by nested finite differences, 3.9e-5 K and 5.8e-7 in P; the bubble curve traced by continuation reaches it (P/P_c - 1 = 5.4e-6 at T_c - 1e-3 K); certified two-phase flashes at T_c - 1, 0.1 and 0.01 K with tie lines shrinking to 1.6e-3; one stable phase above T_c at P_c; the gamma-phi packages reject that state (FueraDeAlcance); PH and UV invert TP to 1e-10 K; layer closure complementarity < 1e-12. Findings, not implemented: (1) experimental mixture critical loci and elevated-pressure binary VLE for catalog-only pairs (for example Hicks and Young 1975, Barr-David and Dodge 1959) were not reachable in this delivery, and the classic cubic-EOS test systems need light gases absent from `core/catalogo.py` (not Claude-owned); the mixture validation is therefore numerical plus pure-limit and low-pressure data; (2) the package is reachable through `capa.cierre` only: no compiler admission, route, contract or Results view (Codex), and no declared k_ij record in the common model; (3) no volume translation, so liquid densities carry the PR error; quadratic mixing only, so polar pairs are outside k_ij = 0 accuracy; (4) a third phase (L-L-V) is rejected, never published; (5) the critical tracer is about 5 s per point and is verified on binaries only. Next in f8b: independent review; experimental mixture-critical and high-pressure references; a UNIFAC-based EOS mixing rule (PSRK or VTPR) as the UNIFAC-priority route to high pressure.

**Independent A1 cross-review (Claude, read-only, 5 November 2026; lane A task done by claude-b while claude-a is inactive).** `reaction_jacobian_bounds` (ideal gas and UNIFAC liquid): **approve**. Re-derived the affinity, Gibbs-Helmholtz dg/dT, the liquid standard part and the amount-form UNIFAC; fuzzed against independent 40-digit references with 0 enclosure violations (ideal gas 915 boxes and 90,036 checks; interval UNIFAC with real and random extreme parameters 12,800 checks; full liquid path 420 checks). F5-1 (low): the global `iv.prec` save and restore is not thread-safe; use a private interval context or a lock. F5-2: wording of the rounding guarantee. SLE with the F9-R10/R11/R12/R14 repairs: **approve with repairs**; R10 (ln x to 4e-15 down to x = 2e-70), R11 (benzene solid + water: one stable root inside the independent convex-hull binodal) and R12 confirmed closed; benzene + cyclohexane original UNIFAC matches `thermo` to 1.6e-15 (eutectic 224.370905 K, x = 0.275102). SLE-1 (low-medium): P is not validated when dv = 0, so NaN P gives the false cause `liquidus_below_solid_T_min`, silently skipped by `crystallization_T`, and negative P is accepted. SLE-2 (low): out-of-range solid index raises IndexError. Peng-Robinson mixture EOS: **approve with repairs**; against `thermo.PR78MIX` on 120 random states Z to 3.7e-15 and ln phi to 2.5e-14; Heidemann-Khalil critical points agree with an independent mpmath Reid-Beegle solver to 2e-11 K. PR-1 (medium): an L-L split is labelled vapour + liquid (benzene + water at 320 K, 1 bar). PR-2 (medium): `critical_points` returns negative-pressure and L-L roots as gas-liquid points and an empty list without diagnostic. PR-3 (low): null-vector sign not fixed. PR-4 (low): absent candidates share one trial; PH/UV brackets raise raw ValueError. Codex RV-4 and CR-2 repairs: **approve** (signed zero and adjacent binary64 constants get distinct instances; unequal, zero, negative, time-dependent or unbound discharge pressures rejected at the connection path). Tests: 85 + 11 passed.

**F8b PSRK, elevated-pressure VLE and experimental critical loci, fifth extension (Claude, 7 November 2026).** `core/termodinamica/psrk.py` and `psrk_data.py`.

- **Scope choice.** PSRK (Holderbaum and Gmehling 1991; Horstmann et al. 2005) is the UNIFAC-based route to high pressure that can be built from data already in the repository. thermo 0.6.1 distributes the PSRK table, the gas groups and the DDBST assignments. VTPR also needs volume translations and Twu parameters, which are absent. NRTL is not extended.
- **Derivation.** One SRK residual Helmholtz function, F = −n ln(1 − B/V) − n α_m ln(1 + B/V).
  - Ω_a and Ω_b are solved from the critical conditions.
  - A1 = −ln(2.1/1.1) is derived from the SRK pure residual Gibbs energy at PSRK's reference v/b = 1.1 (published −0.64663).
  - α_m = Σxα_i + (g^E/RT + Σx ln(b/b_i))/A1, with UNIFAC (PSRK table) evaluated by the existing `LiquidoUNIFAC` code.
  - ∂F/∂n_i and ∂F/∂V are analytic.
  - The cubic package's F-based methods, saturation and Heidemann–Khalil solver are reused unchanged.
- **Data provenance.**
  - The Mathias–Copeman constants are re-regressed by the published method on each species' vapour-pressure record, linearly through the exact Maxwell α*(T). They are not the published PSRK compound constants. For CO2 the regressed α agrees with those constants within 0.25 %.
  - CO2 data come from Span and Wagner 1996.
  - The PSRK interaction table is thermo's TSV (sha256 recorded). It was not checked entry by entry against the Horstmann et al. 2005 appendix. Exchanging the CO2–OH directions makes CO2 + ethanol worse, which supports the orientation.
- **Bubble points** are certified:
  - fugacity equality, normalization and distinct phases;
  - the incipient phase must be less dense than the feed (`past_critical_point`);
  - |y − x| ≥ 1e-3 (`near_critical_unresolved`); the isotherm continuation's step is bounded by |y − x|, so it cannot jump past the critical composition;
  - after a Newton failure, damped probes classify the case as `trivial_solution` or `past_critical_point` before reporting `not_converged`.
- **Evidence, `tests/test_psrk_mixture.py` (23 tests, about 160–250 s).**
  - Closed-form SRK constants 1e-14.
  - Psat records within 0.19–0.96 %.
  - Against thermo: ln γ 1e-13, and a and b against thermo's independent `PSRK` class 1e-9.
  - Complex-step gradients 1e-13; Gibbs–Duhem and symmetry 1e-12.
  - An independent 30-digit mpmath PSRK: bubble point 1e-10, and Reid–Beegle critical point 1e-6 K against the Heidemann–Khalil point.
- **Experiment (NIST ThermoML, predictive, no binary parameter).**
  - Bubble pressures:
    - CO2 + methanol and CO2 + toluene at 311–391 K and up to 12 MPa (Naidoo et al., FPE 269 (2008) 104), all 12 and 14 points: AARD 4.5 % and 5.0 %, y_CO2 within 0.04 and 0.008;
    - CO2 + ethanol at 333–373 K (Sima et al., JCED 56 (2011) 5052), all 36 points: 35 published, every one over-predicted by +9.7 % to +64.7 % (AARD 21.8 %). x = 0.77 at 363.15 K is `near_critical_unresolved`. The independent mpmath PSRK and the review's own reference reproduce the package, so the error belongs to the published parameters.
  - Critical lines:
    - CO2 + methanol (Liu et al., JCED 48 (2003) 1610): methanol branch +0.3 to +12 K and +6 to +23 %; CO2 end within 3.2 K and 3 %. For x_CO2 = 0.786–0.893 PSRK has no positive-pressure critical point (located rejection, confirmed by the review's independent scan), while the measured line is continuous.
    - CO2 + ethanol (Sima et al. 2011): x = 0.892–0.931 within 6.8 K and 9.4 %; no PSRK root at 0.83–0.85.
  - Consistency: the 373.15 K CO2 + methanol isotherm stops at x = 0.70868, where the computed critical locus crosses 373.15 K (0.70866).
- **Located rejections.** `no_pure_record`, `missing_interaction`, `alpha_below_fit_range`, `trivial_solution`, `past_critical_point`, `near_critical_unresolved`, `not_converged` (with probes), `no_critical_point_in_window`, `no_ideal_gas_record`.
- **Findings.**
  1. The CO2–alcohol accuracy and the interrupted loci are parameter or model limits.
  2. There is no `capa` package, route or Results view.
  3. Light gases have no caloric record.
  4. A critical point takes about 10 s.
  5. `tests/test_psrk_mixture.py` needs a slow-tier CI assignment.
- **Independent review (7 November 2026): approve with repairs.** R1 (full data set), R2 (located causes), R3 (English names) and R4 (provenance) were repaired. Commits: 4851958 (psrk_data.py), 02c4611 (psrk.py), f65509d (tests) integrated into fase-a-adopcion as 3f7baf7, a8abf6f, b7500b5.

## F9. Cross-cutting quality and reliable use

**F9-R2 diagnosis (Claude, 22 October 2026; read-only, no production change).** This work ran in parallel with M1, on a separate thermodynamic track. It used the reproducers in the ignored folder `.tmp/claude-r2-audit/`:

- `trace_numpy_errors.py` reports every unhandled NumPy floating-point error, including those raised in worker threads, together with its repository call chain. Positive controls in the main thread and in a worker thread confirm that it fires. For the diagnostic run only, it disables the suites' blanket `warnings.filterwarnings("ignore")`; production code contains no warning filters.
- `adversarial_unifac.py` generates the adversarial cases.
- `repro_ph_trace.py` is the minimal reproducer of the PH defect.

Findings:

- **The suites hide this incident.** `validar_capa_unifac.py` and `validar_motor_unifac.py` ignore all warnings. Traced reruns of seven suites recorded **zero** unhandled floating-point errors: layer UNIFAC 82, engine UNIFAC 136, diagrams 78, catalog 126, assembly v2 114, n-components 82 and consolidation v2 128. The engine UNIFAC rerun used the main-thread hook only, and its threaded concurrency section repeats computations already traced. The originally reported sites in `_newton` trial decoding no longer reproduce, because `_trial_phase_amounts` now rejects those trials before exponentiation.
- **Adversarial matrix: 848 operations, 0 invalid published states, 0 uncontrolled exceptions.**
  - The matrix covers all three UNIFAC packages and four systems: ethanol–water, water–butanol, benzene–water–ethanol and five species. The original package ran the binaries and the ternary; the reduced Dortmund/LLE pass ran without UV.
  - The operations are TP, PH, UV and the seeded fast PH/TP flashes used by dynamics.
  - The feeds include uniform, pure, exact-zero, 1e-12 and 1e-300 traces and a dominant component, at domain-edge and interior temperatures.
  - Every published state had finite, nonnegative amounts, normalized compositions and verifications within tolerance.
  - Every rejection was structured. Three kinds are correct physics or domain behavior: UV with an incompressible liquid filling the volume (pressure undetermined), PH targets outside the package T domain (the LLE package covers only 283.15–313.15 K), and the case below.
- **Only remaining floating-point site:** `core/mezclas.py:1125`, `np.log` of a fast-flash seed. In `_newton`, a 1e-300 trace scaled by `nz/tot` underflows to 0. The resulting `−inf` trial is discarded by the finiteness check in `desempaquetar`, so this is an exploratory candidate and never a published value. It appears in 4 of 848 cases and always precedes the defect below.
- **Confirmed robustness defect, medium severity: a valid near-pure PH state on a boiling plateau is falsely rejected.**
  - Inputs: ethanol–water, P = 101325 Pa, H set 5 % above liquid water at the mid-domain temperature.
  - Reference: an exact pure-water feed gives T_sat = 373.161184 K with vapor fraction 0.0414177.
  - Behavior from 1e-2 down to 1e-6: `equilibrio_PH` converges continuously to that limit. At 1e-6 the result is 373.160936 K with vapor fraction 0.0414189.
  - Behavior at 1e-7 and below, including 1e-300: it rejects the state with "PH: la búsqueda en T no converge ni es una meseta". At 1e-9 it instead fails TP certification with `tm −5.1e-9`. Both original and Dortmund UNIFAC show this.
  - Repro: `python -W ignore .tmp/claude-r2-audit/repro_ph_trace.py 1e-6,1e-7,1e-9`.
  - Cause hypothesis: the boiling range becomes narrower than the T-search and stability resolution, but the plateau path is reserved for exactly pure feeds.
  - Affected: dynamic runs whose inventories carry trace components through a boiling event.
  - The engine correctly refuses to certify, so this is a false rejection, not a false certificate.
  - Regression criterion: continuity of T, phase fractions and H residual for traces from 1e-6 down to 1e-300 and 0, without relaxing `TOL_TM` or `TOL_MU`.
  - **Fixed, 22 October 2026, in `core/mezclas.py`.** The user assigned the thermodynamics files to Claude. There were three general causes; no package, substance or case branch was added:
    1. **Ill-conditioning in T.** dH/dT diverges across a two-phase band that is about trace × K wide. When Brent's bracket stops meeting the H tolerance, or an intermediate TP state cannot be certified inside the band, `equilibrio_isobaro` now calls the new `_isobaric_by_phase_amounts`. That method reuses the existing `_newton` formulation, with T and phase amounts as unknowns, seeded with the phases on both sides of the bracket. It publishes a state only if all of that state's verifications and the original energy tolerance hold. Paths that already succeeded are unchanged.
    2. **Binary liquid stability test.** `prueba_estabilidad` refined only interior grid minima, so a minimum beyond the logit edge (|u| = 34, fraction below 2e-15) was lost. With a vapor phase present the trial set was empty, and `_newton` failed on `min()` of an empty sequence. Edge minima are now refined to |u| ≤ 690, evaluating both fractions without cancellation.
    3. **Seed underflow.** The original F9-R2 site at `mezclas.py:1125` came from seed scaling: `p * nz / tot` underflowed to 0 for traces of about 1e-200. It now computes `p * (nz / tot)`.
  - **Result:** T and phase fractions now converge continuously to the pure limit for traces from 1e-2 to 1e-305 (original and Dortmund), with H errors ≤ 2e-11 J.
  - **Regression test:** `python tests/test_near_pure_isobaric_flash.py` (6 tests, about 15 s):
    - PH from 1e-7 to 1e-300 for both packages, with first-order bounds against the exact pure-feed lever-rule reference;
    - the same isobaric path for UP;
    - the seeded dynamic flash;
    - binary edge-minimum resolution;
    - unchanged tolerances.
  - **Negative control:** against the pre-fix module the test gives 1 failure and 16 errors.
  - **Regression suites after the fix, all passing:** phase discovery 9; thermodynamics; thermodynamic layer 179; UNIFAC layer 82; water 161; engine thermodynamics 585; engine UNIFAC 136; diagrams 78; catalog 126; assembly 245; assembly v2 114; n-components 82; consolidation v2 128; vertical slice 71; balances 30; H3 3,729.
  - **Not run:** the frontend `npm run test:motor-fundamental` suite, which exercises the TypeScript matter engine and does not use `mezclas.py`. The validation backend on 8004 was not restarted, so HTTP consumers keep the previous code until an authorized reload.
- **Performance finding.**
  - Most single flashes finish in under 0.3 s (median).
  - Trace or edge feeds of the ternary take 20–74 s in full PH, UV and seeded fast PH.
  - Binary UV with a 1e-12 trace takes 35–69 s.
  - This is consistent with the known five-species cost and belongs to the existing UNIFAC performance item.
- **Invariant-point path (closure, 22 October 2026).** `_pulir_invariante` applied `np.exp` to Newton trials without a guard. Reproducers: `.tmp/claude-r2-audit/repro_invariant_points.py`, run under `trace_numpy_errors.py`, and `probe_invariant_extremes.py`.
  - **Production path: no failure.** Original and Dortmund UNIFAC, water–1-butanol and water–benzene at 101325 Pa, feeds 0.3/0.05/0.6/0.001 organic, PH targets at 1e-6, 0.5 and 1 − 1e-6 of the plateau jump and TV at the invariant T: 116 cases, 19 polish calls, all converged and certified, **zero** unhandled floating-point events. Independent references: the invariant T found by TP-only bisection (366.18 / 365.89 K for water–butanol, 342.33 / 342.71 K for water–benzene, against 365.85 K and 342.45 K in the literature); the three-phase chemical potentials recomputed with `Sistema.mu` agree within `TOL_MU`; material balance and H target hold. Perturbed, swapped, trace and far seeds return the certified point or `None`.
  - **Defect reproduced on a direct adversarial seed, low severity.** A seed with two vapor phases near the upper T domain edge drives the logits past the float range: `np.exp` overflows and the composition becomes inf/inf (`overflow` and `invalid` events). The point was then discarded (`None`), so nothing invalid was published.
  - **Fixed in `core/mezclas.py` (`_pulir_invariante`), general and without relaxing tolerances.** A trial is decoded only when ln T or ln P and every logit keep all exponentials positive and normal; otherwise it is rejected and the line search backtracks. Non-finite residuals or Jacobians end the polish with `None`. Accepted trials are computed exactly as before.
  - **Regression test:** `python tests/test_invariant_point_polish.py` (5 tests, about 40 s): water–butanol (original and Dortmund) and water–benzene heteroazeotropes against the references above with no floating-point events, the adversarial seed returning `None` with no events, and convergence from a valid seed. **Negative control:** against the pre-fix function it fails the adversarial case.
  - **Regression suites after the fix, all passing:** invariant polish 5; near-pure isobaric flash 6; phase discovery 9; thermodynamics; thermodynamic layer 179; water 161; engine thermodynamics 585; diagrams 78; catalog 126; assembly 245; assembly v2 114; n-components 82; consolidation v2 128; vertical slice 71; balances 30; H3 3,729; UNIFAC layer 82; engine UNIFAC 136. The validation backend on 8004 was not restarted.

R2 is closed: both reported sites are guarded, the near-pure plateau defect is fixed above, and no numerical evidence was found that an out-of-domain candidate was certified. Ternary or larger invariant points (C + 1 ≥ 4 phases) were not tested; the current package admits at most three phases.

**F9-R1 re-audit and track split (Claude, 23 October 2026).** `node frontend/auditar_fiabilidad_evento_fase.mjs --servicio` against the current 8004 backend (capture `frontend/.tmp/qa-fiabilidad-evento-2026-10-23T10-41-00-574Z/`): NRTL, original UNIFAC and the ideal mixture now all certify, with boundary-work residuals 0.0535, 0.0174 and 0.0276 against tolerance 1 (originally 1.105 `no_cumple`, 0.630 and 0.094). Regression tests rerun and passing: `tests/test_thermodynamic_events.py` 6, `tests/test_near_pure_isobaric_flash.py` 6, `tests/test_invariant_point_polish.py` 5, `tests/test_trace_phase_search.py` 3. **F9-R5 common-solver delivery (Codex, 23 October 2026; after U1 and binary F8 D4).** Before repair, the common compiler with two output samples missed the 0.64 s Dortmund water–1-butanol L–L window and published only vapor appearance at 50.4192248 s. The unchanged independent PH reference gives [22.7549316, 23.3936035] s. `core/compilador/compilador.py` now provides certified, seed-preserving event signatures and stability/phase-amount margins to the generic optional hook in `core/solvers/integrador.py`; the solver limits falling margins, checks quarter points and bisects the first changed interior bracket. Reintegration cannot publish a fictitious unchanged-signature event. The compiler also honors the actual RK45/BDF request instead of silently running RK45 for BDF.

`tests/test_common_phase_window.py -v` passes two tests in **224.226 s**, including the independent PH reference, unchanged RHS seed context and four actual-method runs. The four runs find L–L appearance at 22.7548852–22.7548895 s, disappearance at 23.3936210–23.3936222 s and vapor appearance at 50.4192248 s, all window boundaries within 4.7e-5 s of the reference:

| Method | Output samples | Solve time (s) | Matter residual | Energy residual | Complementarity |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| RK45 | 2 | 41.620 | 0 | 8.748e-16 | 1.727e-11 |
| RK45 | 11 | 37.324 | 0 | 1.647e-16 | 1.338e-11 |
| BDF | 2 | 52.340 | 0 | 1.544e-16 | 1.449e-11 |
| BDF | 11 | 65.136 | 0 | 1.297e-15 | 4.546e-13 |

Four generic margin/bracketing/legacy/invalid-margin regressions pass in **0.026 s**; the vertical slice passes 71 checks and the explicit compiler 27. The post-R5 U1 numerical rerun passes all twelve A/B/C reference/refinement runs in **150.039 s** with identical metrics. Frozen A/B/C, shared acceptance author/tests and the kernel-backlog files retain their initial hashes. Evidence is in ignored `.tmp/f9-r5-evidence.json`; protocol and resolution are maintained in [the vertical slice](vertical-slice.md#2-solver-coresolversintegradorpy). The isolated browser test servers were stopped and only their generated Next build/configuration changes removed after acceptance.

**R5 scope for independent review:** this delivers the common compiler/solver path; Claude's network/thermodynamics files are untouched. The margin floor is 1e-8 and the minimum adaptive margin step is max(h_min, 1e-8 tf), so arbitrary closed windows are not guaranteed. Legacy `core/ensamblaje_dinamico.py` still has quarter-point checks with a full-step bracket and needs a separately scoped migration, retirement or repair decision. R5 therefore stays **review**, with that remaining path explicit; it is not declared universally closed.

**Independent verification of the F9-R5 common-solver repair (Claude, 23 October 2026; read-only, evidence in ignored `.tmp/claude-r5-verify/`).** Verdict: **accepted for the common compiler/solver path.** The reference is the two-liquid interval of the exact trajectory H0 + Q t from PH flashes only, scanned every 5 ms and bisected to 1e-7 s (Codex's reference stopped at 1e-4 s). For the test window (z = 0.027865, 0.639 s: [22.7548852, 23.3936217] s) and a narrower one not in Codex's suite (z = 0.0278649, 0.456 s), RK45 and BDF with 2 and 11 output samples all publish L–L appearance, disappearance and vapor appearance in order, with every window boundary within 4.3e-6 s (most below 1e-6 s) and every published verification below 0.2 % of its tolerance. Wrapping the scipy classes shows that a BDF request is stepped only by BDF (132–160 steps) and RK45 only by RK45 (65–74). The four generic margin tests pass; the network path (`core/ensamblaje_red.py`) has its own integrator and is unaffected; my U1 references rerun after the change give identical errors (relative difference 0) and identical diagnostics. Code review: retries are bounded by `max_pasos`, the reintegration guard drops only the segments after the step start, and event probes restore the closure seeds.

Non-blocking notes: (1) every accepted step now runs four certified, non-fast closures at the quarter points, so a 60 s heating run takes 30–60 s; this is not yet measured against the R6 budgets; (2) `euler_implicito` is executed as BDF, a mapping that predates R5 in `explicito.py` and `hydraulics.py`; the published method says BDF, but the request should either be rejected or the mapping stated in the contract; (3) the resolution limits Codex states (margin floor 1e-8, minimum margin step) stand: a closed window much shorter than these is not guaranteed. R5 stays open only for the legacy `core/ensamblaje_dinamico.py` path, now an open user decision (`dec-r5-legacy-v1`). **User decision, 23 October 2026:** retire that path and migrate its uses to the common compiler rather than repair it.

**Retirement of the legacy v1 assembly (Claude, 24 October 2026; `dec-r5-legacy-v1`).** `core/ensamblaje_dinamico.py`, `tests/validar_motor_ensamblaje.py` and its example generator were removed. `Funcion`/`Detencion` moved unchanged to `core/dynamic_common.py` and the exterior water enthalpy into `core/paquetes_volumen.py`; `backend/motor_ensamblaje.py` keeps only the helpers revision 2 reuses and answers revision 1 with `MOTOR_ENS_REVISION_RETIRADA` (capabilities without a revision return a retirement notice; revision 2 now advertises `revisionesAdmitidas: [2]`, which the frontend reader accepts). The nine frozen revision-1 resolutions are translated field by field to revision 2 (`tests/v1_assembly_cases.py`) and solved by the common compiler: `tests/test_retired_v1_assembly.py` reproduces T, P, V, U, H and mass within 1e-7 relative (observed at most 3e-9) and every event within 1e-6 s. Two defects found on the way were repaired: (1) `core/solvers/integrador.py` ended a trajectory at its last accepted point when a trial RK stage raised a physical stop beyond it (liquid withdrawal stopped at 6.5636 s instead of 7.2715675 s; an independent IAPWS-95 UV reference and the frozen v1 result agree on 7.2715675 s); it now shortens the step and skips scipy's initial-step probe on retries (`tests/test_solver_stage_stop.py`, negative control fails before the fix); (2) `Sistema.equilibrio_isobaro`'s phase-amount fallback now seeds the Newton from the two-phase side and scales its energy residual like the PH acceptance (`tests/test_isobaric_bubble_edge.py`). Regression: 29 suites pass, the R5 window check (4.3e-6 s) and the U1 references (identical errors) rerun unchanged. Open: `tests/validar_capa_unifac.py` fails since the R5 delivery (it passed before): R5's cold certified PH event probes reach the bubble edge of water/ethanol/ethyl acetate/methanol (Dortmund, 0.25 each, 1 atm), where the TP flash's H noise (about 1e-5 J, vapor amounts of order 1e-10 mol) exceeds the PH tolerance 1e-10|H|, so no PH method meets it there; the seeded fast closure used before R5 does not hit it. `tests/test_hydraulic_execution.py` fails because Codex's current change rejects `euler_implicito`, which that test still requests. The example `corte-vertical-benceno-tolueno-resultados.json` regenerates with 28 instead of 29 samples under R5 (same with or without the stage-stop fix); it was restored, not regenerated.

**F9-R9 bubble-edge H noise (Claude, 24 October 2026; `core/mezclas.py`, `TOL_TM`/`TOL_MU` and the PH tolerance unchanged).** Within 1e-10 K above the bubble point of water/ethanol/ethyl acetate/methanol (Dortmund and original UNIFAC, 0.25 each, 1 atm) the TP flash scattered the vapor amount by up to 9e-10 mol around the linear law (H errors to 3e-5 J, above 1e-10 |H| = 3.5e-6 J), with mu residuals of 1e-11 to 7e-11 accepted below `TOL_MU`; some states were published as one liquid inside the `TOL_TM` band. Two general causes: (1) the phase-split Newton's own-phase Jacobian blocks, computed by differences, carried rounding noise along each phase's total-amount direction, where the exact derivative is zero (mu is homogeneous of degree zero in the amounts of its phase); for a phase of 1e-10 mol that noise exceeded the true coupling through the other phase and the Newton stalled; the identity is now imposed by projection; (2) the Gibbs minimizer cannot resolve the G gain of a 1e-8 mol phase (about 1e-17 RT n) and left it empty or at a poor composition; the equal-potential Newton is now also seeded from the trial composition at small amounts, and the caller still certifies the result. Evidence (`tests/test_bubble_edge_h_noise.py`, negative control fails before the repair): the vapor amount is linear within 5e-12 mol of the law fitted 2e-8 to 1e-7 K away, with mu residuals of 1e-15, for both 4-component packages and ethanol-water; `tests/validar_capa_unifac.py` passes again (82 checks, 4,592 s, against 777 s before R5: the cold certified event probes are costly and should be measured against the R6 budgets); 32 regression suites pass; the R6 benchmark stays within every budget; the F0 dense-search cross-check keeps 0 misses in 468 states.

It now has two tiers recorded in `tools/ci/test_tiers.json`: a fast tier (16 `validar_*` suites, 214 s locally, plus `tsc`, 41 pure frontend verifiers and `next build`) on every push, and a slow tier (6 suites, led by `validar_capa_unifac` at 48 min and `validar_motor_unifac` at 20 min, one job each, plus 19 Python-backed verifiers and the browser check) on PRs to `main`, nightly and manual runs. `tools/ci/check_requirements.py` and `check_tiers.py` keep imports pinned (`sympy==1.14.0`, `starlette==0.27.0` added) and every suite in exactly one tier. Open items: `validar_motor_balances.py` fails 1 of 30 checks (quarantined, non-blocking; "volumen v1 keeps generation.1 and has no data"), `verificar_compilador_explicito.mjs` (implicit Euler not implemented by the common compiler) and `verificar_bateria_multifisica.mjs` (over 900 s) are quarantined, `tests/test_*.py` (about 60 files) is not yet in CI, and the slow tier and the interface job have not yet run on GitHub. Pushed 5 November 2026 (83ed04d..04bbfbd): after two repairs found by the first runs (a clean clone has no `.tmp/` for `verificar_phase_discovery.mjs`; the published `estudio-calor` nondimensionalization example differs between Windows and Linux libm/LAPACK by one ulp to 1.6e-14 relative, so published-example floats now compare to 1e-12 relative while keys, lengths, types and other values stay exact), run 42115608962 passes `core-fast` and `frontend` on ubuntu.

**F9-R10..R14 thermodynamics reliability sweep (claude-b, Sonnet, 5 November 2026; read-only, reproducers in the sweep session scratchpad).** Probed the SLE delivery, `reaction_jacobian_bounds`, IAPWS-95 saturation near T_c, the steep-TV bracket and the pure plateau against mpmath and closed-form references. **Passed:** ideal benzene-cyclohexane eutectic within 2.4e-14 K; solubility from 4e-5 to 0.015 within 2e-13 relative; `reaction_jacobian_bounds` contained every sampled value in 150 random fuzz boxes and all edge cases, with located `OUT_OF_DOMAIN` and `NONFINITE_BOUND` rejections; IAPWS-95 saturation over 1500 gaps from 1e-13 to 5 K with no Maxwell or monotonicity violation in the accepted states; 544 pure-plateau and 206 steep-TV states with 0 wrong answers. **Open:** **F9-R10 (high when reached)** `SolidLiquidSystem.solubility` (`core/termodinamica/solid_liquid.py`) uses an absolute `xtol=1e-15`, so a saturation mole fraction below ~1e-13 comes back as ~8e-16 without a diagnostic (8.3e-17 expected at 150 K, 3.2e-34 at 100 K for a dh_m = 1e5 J/mol record) while `liquidus_T` is right; solve in ln x. **F9-R11 (medium-high)** across a liquid-liquid gap the saturation condition has three roots; `solubility` and `liquidus_T` return one arbitrary root that jumps branch with T (two-suffix Margules A = 2.5, 275 to 276 K), and `certify` marks a liquid inside the spinodal `cumple`; add a liquid-stability test at the root and a located rejection or the S-L1-L2 line. **F9-R12 (medium)** `crystallization_T` swallows every `FueraDeAlcance` of a present solid, including a liquidus above the liquid model's `T_range`, and may report a lower solid as the first to crystallize; reject, naming the solid, unless the liquidus is below its `T_min`. **F9-R13 (low-medium, not reachable today)** `core/agua_iapws95.py` `saturacion` accepts unrefined fits within ~1e-11 K of T_c whose density gap follows slope 1.0 instead of 0.5 (32x too small at 1e-11 K) because the Maxwell residual is blind where P and g are flat; public states are protected only by the excluded critical band [643.861, 650.331] K; and `evaluar_TV(T_c, 1/rho_c)` raises a raw `ZeroDivisionError` instead of `region_critica`. **F9-R14 (low)** `solid_tm` accepts unnormalised or negative x and T outside the solid record; `SolidPure.melting_T` has no convergence check and raises a raw `ValueError` for dv < 0 at 1e10 Pa. Low-confidence note: `reaction_jacobian_bounds` sets the global `mpmath.iv.prec`; check thread use before any thread-pool caller.

**F9-R10..R14 SLE repairs (claude-b, 5 November 2026; commits 3ef0e83..2f39369 on fase-a-adopcion, review pending).** Four of the sweep's SLE findings are repaired in `core/termodinamica/solid_liquid.py` and `solid_data.py`, with 12 regressions in `tests/test_solid_liquid_reliability.py`, each failing against 76c698e. R10: the binary solubility is solved in ln x after bracketing every root on a fixed grid, exact to 1e-12 relative against mpmath down to x = 3.2e-34 and consistent with `liquidus_T`, `eutectic` and `certify`. R11: a saturated liquid must also pass the liquid's tangent-plane test (`liquid_stability`, generic in N); `solubility` returns the unique stable root, `liquidus_T` requires a stable liquid and `certify` rejects an unstable one; verified against the closed-form Margules binodal and a ternary regular-solution Hessian. R12: `crystallization_T` skips only a liquidus below the solid's own T_min; every other failure names the solid and the cause. R14: located input validation in `solid_tm` and a guarded `melting_T`. The verified benzene-cyclohexane results are unchanged (ideal eutectic bit-identical; UNIFAC liquidus within 1.5e-11 K, inside the solver tolerance); `test_solid_liquid_equilibrium.py` (11) and `validar_capa_termodinamica.py` (179) pass. Gaps recorded: the S-L1-L2 monotectic line is rejected with `liquid_liquid_instability`, not computed; the stability verdict is numerical (a negative tpd is a proof, its absence is not); roots closer than one bracketing-grid cell and the liquid's stability above the first liquidus on cooling are not resolved; solid records declare no pressure range for constant dv. F9-R13 (IAPWS-95 near T_c) is untouched.

**F9-R13 IAPWS-95 near-critical repair (claude-b, 5 November 2026; commits b1397f5 and 574fae6; re-reviewed: approve).** An independent mpmath IAPWS-95 residual part (R6-95 Table 2, checked against Table 6) with Maxwell conditions solved at 50 digits shows the coexisting density gap following the mean-field slope (0.48 at 1e-3 K, 0.4999 at 1e-8 K), then steepening; IAPWS-95's own critical temperature lies about 1.9e-11 K below 647.096 K, so the states the old `saturacion` accepted at 1e-11 to 3e-13 K had no coexistence behind them. In binary64 the `chemicals` fits reach a gap error of 5e-15/s (s = rho (dP/drho)_T/Psat) and no binary64 Newton improves on them. `core/agua_iapws95.py` now publishes a saturation state only when s >= 1e-9 (gap error <= 1e-5, above T_c - 1.13e-7 K); closer to T_c it raises the located `SaturationNotResolvable` (`region_critica`, also a `ValueError`, so `ramas` and its callers are unchanged). Accepted states are bit-identical (6,000 temperatures checked by the re-review). The exact critical point raises `region_critica` in `propiedades`, `helmholtz` and `evaluar_TV` instead of a raw `ZeroDivisionError`. Six regressions in `tests/test_iapws95_critical_reliability.py` (tests 1 to 4 fail against b6eb986); `test_water_saturation_near_critical.py` and `validar_capa_agua.py` (161) pass. Open: between T_c - 1.13e-7 K and T_c the coexistence is not resolvable in binary64; decide its treatment before narrowing the excluded critical band.

**A1 review repairs committed (claude-b, 6 November 2026; reviewed: approve with repairs, R1-R3 then repaired).** SLE validates pressure (finite, positive) before the dv = 0 shortcut and every solid index in `certify`, `hbar_E` and the other public operations (SLE-1, SLE-2, R2). Peng-Robinson splits are labelled liquid-liquid only when both phases have PIP > 1 and the lighter phase is dense (kappa near the liquid range), so near-critical vapour-liquid splits (methanol + water, 581 K, 15.31-15.35 MPa) keep their labels while benzene + water at 320 K is a located L-L state (PR-1, R1); critical points are classified, negative-pressure roots dropped, the null vector oriented and an empty scan window located (PR-2, PR-3); absent candidates get distinct trial compositions and UV/PH brackets search the first evaluable temperature, so cubic UV layer closures work with default brackets (PR-4, R3); `reaction_jacobian_bounds` uses a thread-safe precision context (F5-1). On a clean checkout of fase-a-adopcion: fast tier green and 174 tests pass across the SLE, cubic, bounds, workbench, graph and equilibrium suites.

**F9-R5 remaining-path closure (Codex, 24 October 2026; independently verified by Claude on 24 October).** The Dortmund four-component request reproduces the cold PH failure at H = -34511.158609610495 J (water/ethanol/ethyl acetate/methanol, 0.25 mol each, 101325 Pa). `SistemaCompilado.event_state` now catches `FueraDeAlcance` only around the cold probe, restores every original RHS seed before a seeded retry, and restores those seeds again in `finally`. Rerun on the current tree, which includes the F9-R9 thermodynamics edits: `tests/test_common_phase_window.py` 4 OK (188 s), `tests/test_hydraulic_execution.py` 14 OK, `tests/validar_corte_vertical.py` 71 and `tests/validar_capa_unifac.py` 82 (4,328 s under parallel load from the F9-R9 regression; 423 s on 23 October). The 25-request compile-only probe admits 21 and rejects 4; this is not a solve or physical certificate. **V2 migration step (a), compatibility contract (Codex, 24 October 2026).** [Contract version 1](dynamic-assembly.md#13-v2-compatibility-adapter-contract) retains the v2 HTTP/JSON envelope through a translating adapter and makes the common compiler the execution owner, with no fallback. It specifies admission/initialization diagnostics, RK45-only v2 method admission, complete Results/certification and phase identities, certified-prefix failure handling, provenance/invalidation, frozen comparison criteria, independent review and separate deletion approval. The baseline contains 25 requests and 24 sibling responses (the unsupported open-boundary draft has none); their manifest digest is recorded in the contract. Source review corrected the earlier `f25c` classification: heat contact is valid, while `f25b` rejects incompatible material transport. The existing v2 contract verifier passes 10 request/response pairs, 60 volume states and 12 point examples; it does not establish adapter parity for all 25 requests. Step (a) was subsequently accepted with amendments below. Step (b) now includes the F9-R7 dimensional/admission repairs and shared initialization, with evidence in the later Codex entry; steps (c)-(e) remain pending. No solver or route was replaced, no v2 code deleted, and no commit or push was made.

The routes, revision discriminator, five resolve statuses, HTTP-200 rejection envelope, no-fallback rule, rk45-only method scope, size and 1001-sample limits, the `f25b`/`f25c` pair, certified-prefix truncation and the parity tolerances are correctly specified.
